### R-009 — Shared Postgres helper module (B-010)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-08 (branch postgres-helper)
- **Outcome:** `src/genkei/common/db.py` exposes the lazy connection pool (`get_pool` / `reset_pool` / `set_pool`), `connection()` context manager (commits on success, rolls back on exception), `bulk_upsert()` using `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` via `executemany` with safe SQL identifier composition, and `ingest_run()` context manager that records `meta.ingest_runs` rows in three short transactions with error truncation at 8000 chars. ### R-010 — Shared HTTP client (B-011)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-08 (branch http-client)
- **Outcome:** `src/genkei/common/http.py` exposes `RateLimit` (with `per_second` / `per_minute` factories), `RetryPolicy` (4 attempts, 1s→30s exponential, 50% jitter, retries on 408/425/429/5xx by default), and `HttpClient` wrapping `httpx.Client`. Sliding-window rate limiter, source-tagged User-Agent (`genkei/<ver> (+<source>)`), `Retry-After` header honored on 429 with fallback to backoff for invalid values, network exceptions retried up to `max_attempts` then re-raised. Test-friendly via `httpx.BaseTransport` injection plus pluggable `sleep` / `clock` callables. Twenty-two unit tests cover rate-limit factories, sliding-window behavior, backoff math (deterministic + jittered), User-Agent defaults/overrides, retry on retryable status, retry on network exceptions, max-attempts ceiling, `Retry-After` parsing, `get_json` happy path + error path, and end-to-end rate-limit enforcement.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/common/http.py`, `tests/common/test_http.py`. Commits `dd1ea73`, `b2fca19`, `aa82d1b`, `ecf559c`. Follow-ups noted (not blockers): retry currently fires for any HTTP method (consider GET-only allowlist if non-idempotent verbs land), `RemoteProtocolError` not in retry catch, HTTP-date `Retry-After` falls back to backoff, limiter is single-threaded.

### R-015 — DeFiLlama Postgres schema designed (B-016)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-09 (branch postgres-schema, commit `9ef8833`)
- **Outcome:** Two hand-written Alembic migrations land the per-source `defillama` schema and four tables under the conventions in `docs/storage.md`. `defillama.protocols` is a slug-keyed entity dimension with `BIGSERIAL` surrogate, `chains TEXT[]` GIN-indexed, and standard `first_seen_at` / `last_updated_at` lifecycle fields. `defillama.chain_tvl` (PK `(chain, ts)`), `defillama.stablecoins` (PK `(asset_id, chain, ts)`), and `defillama.prices` (PK `(asset_key, ts)`) are time-series facts; the second migration converts each to a TimescaleDB hypertable (30-day chunks for TVL/stablecoins, 7-day chunks for prices to leave room for intraday). Every fact row carries the provenance trio (`source_endpoint`, `fetched_at`, `ingest_run_id` → `meta.ingest_runs`). Stablecoin metadata (symbol/name/peg_type) denormalized inline rather than introducing a premature dimension table; protocol metadata is upserted in place (no SCD2). Hypertable migration intentionally raises `NotImplementedError` on downgrade — per `docs/storage.md`, peel-back the schema migration to drop the underlying tables instead.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260509_create_defillama_schema.py`, `migrations/versions/20260509_create_defillama_hypertables.py`. Applied end-to-end against the homelab Postgres: `alembic upgrade head` advanced to revision `6d578bda9706`; `information_schema.tables` and `timescaledb_information.hypertables` confirm all four tables and three hypertables in place. Tests stay 107/107.

### R-017 — DeFiLlama collector refactored onto Postgres (B-017)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-09 (branch refactor-postgres, commit `eaa807d`)
- **Outcome:** New module `src/genkei/ingest/defillama.py` replaces the file-based `scripts/collect_defillama.py`. Open decision in B-017's acceptance criteria (raw audit trail location: filesystem vs Postgres vs object storage) resolved on **Postgres** — new migration `c4e180fcf605` creates `meta.raw_blobs(id, ingest_run_id FK ON DELETE CASCADE, endpoint_name, url, payload JSONB, fetched_at)` with `UNIQUE(ingest_run_id, endpoint_name)`. The collector wraps work in `db.ingest_run(source='defillama', endpoint='collect')`, fetches each configured endpoint via the shared `HttpClient` at 5 req/sec, and inserts one `meta.raw_blobs` row per success. Required endpoints (prices/protocols/chains/stablecoins) raise on failure → run marked `failed`; per-chain history failures append to `meta.ingest_runs.metadata.partial_endpoints` and the run still completes successfully. `data/raw/` is no longer written.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260509_create_meta_raw_blobs.py`, `src/genkei/ingest/defillama.py`, `tests/ingest/test_defillama.py` (6 unit tests for target-building helpers), `tests/ingest/test_defillama_integration.py` (2 end-to-end tests against the testcontainers harness — full happy-path lands 5 blobs across all endpoints, partial-endpoint failure records `partial_endpoints` metadata). Migration applied to homelab — alembic head is now `c4e180fcf605`.

### R-018 — DeFiLlama normalizer refactored onto Postgres (B-018)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-09 (branch refactor-postgres, commit `eaa807d`)
- **Outcome:** New module `src/genkei/normalize/defillama.py` replaces the file-based `scripts/normalize_defillama.py`. Reads `meta.raw_blobs` rows for a given (or latest-success) collector run, then `bulk_upsert`s into the four B-016 tables: `defillama.protocols` (slug-keyed dim), `defillama.chain_tvl` (per-focus-chain history), `defillama.stablecoins` (per asset+chain supply), `defillama.prices` (per coingecko key). Idempotent — every write is `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` on the table's natural PK; `protocols.first_seen_at` is intentionally never in the upsert payload so the DB default fires once at insert and survives every subsequent update. Each normalizer execution is itself a `meta.ingest_runs` row with `endpoint='normalize'` and `metadata.source_run_id` pointing at the collector run it consumed. **Scope decision:** the new normalizer is data-lake-shaped, not report-shaped — the legacy script's classification logic (momentum, trend, zombie risk) lived in the normalizer because the markdown brief consumed it; that logic returns when B-025 rewrites the report against Postgres.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/normalize/defillama.py`, `tests/normalize/test_defillama.py` (10 unit tests covering `as_float` / `parse_history_timestamp` / `chain_history_stem` / `_stablecoin_supply` plus per-table normalizers), `tests/normalize/test_defillama_integration.py` (2 end-to-end tests — full run writes to all four tables; re-run is idempotent, never overwriting `first_seen_at`).

### R-021 — Provenance columns on every fact row (B-021)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-10 (rolled up from R-015 + R-018; verified on branch phase1-cleanup)
- **Outcome:** All four `defillama.*` fact tables carry the provenance trio in their schema (`source_endpoint TEXT NOT NULL`, `fetched_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()`, `ingest_run_id BIGINT NOT NULL REFERENCES meta.ingest_runs(id)`) per R-015, and the normalizer (R-018) writes them on every emitted row. The legacy bullet about a `change_7d_source: 'history'|'api'` field is obsolete — the data-lake-shaped normalizer doesn't synthesize change percentages anymore (raw values only; downstream consumers recompute with the source they prefer), so there's no derived figure whose origin needs labelling. The `--show-source` CLI surface mentioned in the original criteria folds into the pending CLI work (B-038/B-045) rather than this ticket.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260509_create_defillama_schema.py` defines the trio on `protocols`, `chain_tvl`, `stablecoins`, `prices`. `src/genkei/normalize/defillama.py` includes the trio in every `bulk_upsert` payload. Verified on the live homelab DB: every row in the four tables has `source_endpoint`, `fetched_at`, and `ingest_run_id` populated.

### R-022 — Retry + backoff in the DeFiLlama collector (B-022)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-10 (rolled up from R-010 + R-017; verified on branch phase1-cleanup)
- **Outcome:** `genkei.ingest.defillama.collect` instantiates `HttpClient(SOURCE_NAME, rate_limit=RateLimit.per_second(5))` (R-010), which already wraps every request in `RetryPolicy(max_attempts=4, initial_backoff=1s, max_backoff=30s, jitter=0.5)` with `Retry-After` header support and retries on 408/425/429/5xx + network exceptions. Per-endpoint partial-failure semantics: required endpoints (prices/protocols/chains/stablecoins) raise on exhaustion → run marked `failed`; chain history is `required=False` and failures append to `meta.ingest_runs.metadata.partial_endpoints` so the run still completes successfully (R-017).
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/ingest/defillama.py` constructs the `HttpClient` with rate limit; `src/genkei/common/http.py` carries the retry policy; `tests/ingest/test_defillama_integration.py::test_optional_endpoint_failure_records_partial_metadata` exercises the partial-failure path end-to-end against the testcontainers harness.

### R-023 — DeFiLlama backfill mode + Phase 1 hygiene migrations (B-019)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-10 (branch phase1-cleanup, commits `f6bb302` + `80cb37e`)
- **Outcome:** Closes the last high-priority Phase 1 item with full Option C scope (every backfillable DeFiLlama endpoint), plus the two D-010 hygiene additions baked in from the start. **Three new migrations:** (1) `defillama.protocol_tvl` — sibling to `defillama.protocols`, PK `(slug, chain, ts)`, FK to `protocols(slug)`, 30-day hypertable chunks; (2) compression policies on all four `defillama.*` hypertables (segmentby = most-filtered column per table, orderby `ts DESC`, compress chunks > 30 days old); (3) 90-day retention on `meta.raw_blobs` via a TimescaleDB `add_job` running a `meta.delete_old_raw_blobs` procedure daily inside Postgres. **Backfill collector** (`python -m genkei.ingest.defillama --backfill --since YYYY-MM-DD [--endpoint X]`) walks daily timestamps for prices, iterates known slugs for `/protocol/{slug}`, and known asset_ids for `/stablecoin/{id}`. Each fetch lands a `meta.raw_blobs` row with a backfill-shaped `endpoint_name` (`prices_historical_<date>`, `protocol_<slug>`, `stablecoin_<id>`). chain_tvl is intentionally not backfillable here — G-012, daily collector already pulls full chain history every run. **Resumability** per D-011: every prospective fetch checks `meta.raw_blobs.url` within a 14-day window; already-fetched URLs skip the HTTP call and copy the cached payload into the current run so the selected `source_run_id` still normalizes completely. Crashed-then-resumed runs do zero redundant work. **Backfill normalizer** (`python -m genkei.normalize.defillama --backfill`) dispatches blobs by `endpoint_name` prefix into `defillama.prices`, `defillama.protocol_tvl`, `defillama.stablecoins` — decoupled from collector per D-012, mirroring the existing daily collect/normalize split. Two new pure normalizers (`normalize_protocol_history` walks `chainTvls.<chain>.tvl`, filters G-013 synthetic sub-buckets like `Ethereum-borrowed`; `normalize_stablecoin_history` walks `chainCirculating.<chain>.tokens` and falls back to `chainBalances.<chain>.tokens` for compatibility). Per-item failures recorded in `meta.ingest_runs.metadata.partial_endpoints` rather than tanking the run.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260510_create_defillama_protocol_tvl.py`, `migrations/versions/20260510_add_compression_policies.py`, `migrations/versions/20260510_add_raw_blobs_retention.py`. `src/genkei/ingest/defillama.py` (backfill orchestration + `_cached_blob` resumability + URL builders). `src/genkei/normalize/defillama.py` (`normalize_protocol_history`, `normalize_stablecoin_history`, `normalize_backfill` orchestrator). `tests/normalize/test_defillama.py` (2 unit-test classes covering both historical normalizers including the synthetic-sub-bucket filter and legacy stablecoin fallback). `tests/ingest/test_defillama_backfill_integration.py` (full backfill end-to-end + resume-skip path, 2 tests against the testcontainers harness). Migrations applied to homelab — alembic head `308b3f1284a2`, all four compression policies live, retention job scheduled daily. Smoke-tested against the homelab end-to-end: 3-day `--since` prices backfill landed 15 new `defillama.prices` rows; resume re-run issued 0 HTTP requests. Tests now 125 (105 unit + 20 integration; 18 skipped locally because Docker absent). architecture.md gained D-011, D-012, G-013, G-014, snapshot updated to reflect Phase 1 closure.

### R-027 — FRED macro-series ingester (B-028)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-10 (branch fred-macro, commit `50339d0`)
- **Outcome:** First Phase 2 source — proves the canonical pattern (HttpClient + ingest_run + raw_blobs + bulk_upsert) generalizes to a non-DeFiLlama API. **New schema (migration `e0e8baa01b39`):** `fred.series` entity dim (PK `series_id`, holds title/units/frequency/last_updated/popularity metadata) and `fred.observations` time-series fact (PK `(series_id, ts, realtime_start)`, hypertable, 90-day chunks, compression on chunks > 30 days old). **Vintage-aware (D-013):** each FRED revision lands as a distinct row keyed on the date it first became current — `realtime_end` carries forward (`9999-12-31` for the still-current value). This is what makes as-of backtests correct; Q1 GDP first published in late April vs revised in May vs at the annual revision are three rows, not one overwrite. **Single-mode collector (D-014):** no `--backfill` flag — FRED's `/series/observations` returns the full series in one call regardless. Each daily run uses `realtime_start=1776-07-04&realtime_end=9999-12-31` to pull every vintage; new revisions land via the vintage PK. Series IDs read from `macro_series:` in the existing `config/watchlists.yml` (single source of truth — no separate `config/macro_series.yml` needed). API key in URL is **redacted** (G-015) — `_redact_key()` replaces the literal `FRED_API_KEY` with `***` before the URL hits `meta.raw_blobs.url`; tests assert the literal key never lands in the audit table. New gotchas captured: G-015 (API key in URL → redact), G-016 (FRED uses non-ISO short timezone offset `-05`), G-017 (missing values are `"."` not null — preserved as NULL in the row), G-018 (the `seriess` array key is canonically misspelled in FRED's responses).
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260510_create_fred_schema.py`, `src/genkei/ingest/fred.py`, `src/genkei/normalize/fred.py`, `tests/ingest/test_fred.py` (~12 unit tests for series-loader + URL builders + key redaction), `tests/normalize/test_fred.py` (~10 unit tests for date/timestamp/value parsers + per-table normalizers including a two-vintage observation test), `tests/ingest/test_fred_integration.py` (2 end-to-end tests against the testcontainers harness — full collect+normalize + idempotent re-normalize + URL-redaction assertion), `.github/workflows/fred-daily.yml` (cron 11:00 UTC on `[self-hosted, beelink]`, reads `FRED_API_KEY` from a same-named GH Actions secret), `.env.example` updated with `FRED_API_KEY=` and registration link. Migration applied to homelab — alembic head `e0e8baa01b39`. Tests 121 → 150 (29 new — 27 unit + 2 integration); 20 skipped locally because Docker absent. **Pending live smoke-test:** FRED API key registration is the user's manual step (free, instant at https://fredaccount.stlouisfed.org/apikeys); CI run after the key is set as a GH Actions secret will be the first end-to-end hit against the live FRED API.

### R-028 — SEC EDGAR ingester, option B (B-027)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-10 (branch sec-edgar, commit `b56e1cb`)
- **Outcome:** First equity-side source. Lands two of the three SEC API surfaces — `/submissions/CIK{cik}.json` (filing index + company metadata) and `/api/xbrl/companyfacts/CIK{cik}.json` (XBRL fact history). Per-filing structured payloads (Form 4 insider transactions, 13F institutional holdings) split into B-079 + B-080 follow-ups per D-015 — to be picked up driven by Phase 5 experiments rather than guessed. **New schema (migration `0f3acd7fbf46`):** `sec.companies` entity dim (PK `cik` zero-padded 10-char), `sec.filings` plain table (PK `accession_number`, indexed for `(cik, filed_at)` and `(form_type, filed_at)` query patterns), `sec.facts` hypertable on `period_end` (30-day chunks, compression on chunks > 30 days old). **Restatement-aware fact PK (D-016):** `(cik, concept, unit, period_end, accession_number)` so the same `(concept, period)` reported by both a 10-Q and the subsequent 10-K lands as two rows — the FRED-vintage equivalent for SEC restatements. **Collector design:** reads CIKs from `equities:` in `config/watchlists.yml` (one-time SEC `company_tickers.json` lookup populated all 28 equities; GOOG/GOOGL share Alphabet's CIK and dedupe to one fetch). 8 req/sec rate limit (under SEC's 10/sec per-host cap, G-021). `SEC_USER_AGENT` env var required for identification — SEC has no API key, the User-Agent IS the auth (G-022). Walks the submissions index, follows `filings.files[]` for older-history pages so the full filing history per ticker lands. **Normalizer:** dispatches blobs by `endpoint_name` prefix — submissions blobs into `sec.companies` (upsert) + `sec.filings`, history pages into `sec.filings` (root-level parallel-array shape rather than nested under `filings.recent`), companyfacts blobs walk `facts.{taxonomy}.{concept}.units.{unit}` and emit one row per `(taxonomy:concept, unit, period, accession)` into `sec.facts`.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260510_create_sec_schema.py`, `src/genkei/ingest/sec.py`, `src/genkei/normalize/sec.py`, `tests/ingest/test_sec.py` (unit tests for company-loader CIK-dedup + URL builders + UA resolution), `tests/normalize/test_sec.py` (unit tests for company / filings / facts normalizers including the parallel-array shape, history-page root variant, and XBRL hierarchy walk), `tests/ingest/test_sec_integration.py` (2 end-to-end tests against the testcontainers harness — full collect+normalize across submissions + history + facts blobs, plus idempotent re-normalize). `config/watchlists.yml` updated: every equity entry gained a `cik:` field via one-time `company_tickers.json` lookup. `.github/workflows/sec-daily.yml` runs at 11:30 UTC on `[self-hosted, beelink]` (after the FRED slot to avoid concurrent runs sharing the runner). `.env.example` documents `SEC_USER_AGENT=` with the format SEC requires. architecture.md gained D-015 (option-B scope), D-016 (restatement-aware fact PK), G-021 (10 req/sec is per-host), G-022 (User-Agent identification required). Migration applied to homelab — alembic head `0f3acd7fbf46`. Tests 155 → 178 (23 new — 19 unit + 4 integration); 23 skipped locally because Docker absent. **Pending live smoke-test:** `SEC_USER_AGENT` must be set as a GH Actions secret (Michael Tookes michaeltookes92@gmail.com or similar) before the workflow can exercise the live SEC API — placeholder string will get rejected.

### R-029 — CoinGecko crypto market-data ingester (B-034)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-10 (branch coingecko-crypto, commit `4f4b1be`)
- **Outcome:** Third Phase 2 source. Second crypto price feed alongside DeFiLlama — cross-checks the price and adds `market_cap_usd` + 24h `volume_usd` that DeFiLlama doesn't expose. **New schema (migration `05c48dd08fb0`):** `coingecko.coins` entity dim (PK `coingecko_id`, holds symbol/name/market_cap_rank/genesis_date/categories) and `coingecko.market_data` time-series fact (PK `(coingecko_id, ts)`, hypertable, 30-day chunks, compression on chunks > 30 days). **Collector modes:** daily Demo collection uses `/coins/{id}/market_chart?days=365&interval=daily` for the rolling historical window; `--backfill --since YYYY-MM-DD` requires `COINGECKO_API_TIER=pro` and fetches `/market_chart/range` in 365-day chunks. Reads `coingecko_id` from `crypto:primary` + `crypto:secondary` tiers of `config/watchlists.yml` (7 entries). Two calls per coin = 14 calls per daily run. **Rate-limit/auth handling (G-023, G-025):** a CoinGecko key is required, sent via the tier-specific auth header, and rate-limited at `per_minute(25)`. Key stays out of `meta.raw_blobs.url` — no separate redaction needed. **Normalizer parallel-array handling (G-024):** `/market_chart` returns three "parallel" arrays (prices / market_caps / total_volumes) that don't always align by index — head/tail offsets are common for newer coins. The normalizer indexes each by timestamp, intersects the three sets, and emits rows only where all three series have a value at that ts.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260510_create_coingecko_schema.py`, `src/genkei/ingest/coingecko.py`, `src/genkei/normalize/coingecko.py`, `tests/ingest/test_coingecko.py` (unit tests for watchlist loader, URL builders, required API-key resolution, Pro backfill tier gating, range chunking, rate-limit defaults), `tests/normalize/test_coingecko.py` (unit tests for the three-array timestamp-zip including missing-timestamp drops + non-numeric values), `tests/ingest/test_coingecko_integration.py` (2 end-to-end tests against the testcontainers harness — collect+normalize + idempotent re-normalize). `.github/workflows/coingecko-daily.yml` runs at 11:45 UTC on `[self-hosted, beelink]` after the FRED (11:00) and SEC (11:30) slots so the three workloads don't fight for the runner. `.env.example` documents `COINGECKO_API_KEY=` and `COINGECKO_API_TIER=demo` in the required block. architecture.md gained G-023 (required Demo auth), G-024 (parallel arrays don't align by index), and G-025 (Demo history is capped to a rolling 365-day window; full backfill is Pro-gated). Migration applied to homelab — alembic head `05c48dd08fb0`. Tests 178 → 208 (30 new — 27 unit + 3 integration); 25 skipped locally because Docker absent. **Pending live smoke-test:** CoinGecko demo key registration is a free instant signup at https://www.coingecko.com/en/api/pricing; pushing it as a GH Actions secret unlocks 25/min throughput.

### R-030 — Agent harness decided: Claude Code, single-agent, three patterns from TradingAgents (B-048)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-10 (branch agent-harness-decision; planning doc `~/.claude/plans/noble-twirling-nygaard.md`; recorded as D-017 + D-018 in `docs/architecture.md`)
- **Outcome:** Triggered by user surfacing TradingAgents (https://github.com/TauricResearch/TradingAgents) and Pi Agent (https://github.com/earendil-works/pi) as harness candidates. Both researched in depth. **Decision (D-017): Claude Code is the harness; we are not building a custom Python framework.** TradingAgents is a real LangGraph-based multi-agent system but its agents fetch live data per run, invalidating the data-lake investment; multi-agent debate adds tokens without lift for our Q&A research use case. Pi is a mature multi-provider TypeScript CLI agent (48K stars, MIT, daily releases) with first-class markdown agent registry and subprocess isolation — genuinely good for *coding* task decomposition (scout → planner → worker → reviewer) but mismatched for research Q&A, and switching harnesses costs daily-workflow disruption. Three patterns borrowed from TradingAgents (D-018): (1) structured research methodology as a checklist Claude reads at session start: frame the question → macro context → asset fundamentals → flow/positioning → cross-source signals → counter-thesis check → conclusion + horizon tag → decision-log entry; (2) append-only decision log with outcome reflection cycle keyed on realized returns; (3) two-phase analysis → risk separation in the methodology to prevent premature consensus. Multi-agent shape and Pi adoption explicitly **deferred not rejected** — both re-enter consideration if scope expands to scheduled multi-agent deep-dives. **Sequencing:** the methodology + decision-log + skill scaffolding (B-049, B-050 rescoped) lands after Phase 3 CLI (B-037 → ~B-044) is built enough that Claude can query the lake ergonomically.
- **Evidence:** `docs/architecture.md` D-017 + D-018 + last-updated bump. `docs/backlog.md` B-049 rescoped to "Write the structured research methodology + reflection prompts" and B-050 rescoped to "Implement the decision-log + skill scaffolding" — both reflect Claude-Code-as-harness rather than the prior "Python harness to be chosen" framing. `~/.claude/plans/noble-twirling-nygaard.md` is the planning artifact (local-only, not in repo).

### R-031 — CLI scaffolded with first subcommand (B-037, B-038, B-039)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-10 (branch cli-scaffold, commit `16bc699`)
- **Outcome:** Phase 3 starts. Three Phase 3 items shipped together since the scaffold without a working subcommand is half-built. **B-037 (CLI name + surface):** locked at `genkei`; the top-level subcommand surface (`prices`, `filings`, `tvl`, `macro`, `news`, `watchlist`, `query`) is registered from day one so `genkei --help` shows the full shape and stubs report which backlog item implements them. **B-038 (CLI scaffold):** built on Typer (D-019) — type-hint-driven matches the rest of the codebase, auto-generates `--help` from docstrings + signatures with no boilerplate. Top-level commands registered via `app.command(...)` rather than nested sub-apps with callbacks; reserve `add_typer` for actual subcommand groups (e.g. `genkei query sql ...` later). Reads `GENKEI_DATABASE_URL` via existing `genkei.common.db` helpers — the CLI is *not* its own configuration system. **B-039 (`genkei prices`):** queries `coingecko.market_data` for crypto tickers; resolves via the shared `genkei.cli._watchlist` helper (centralized so every future subcommand resolves tickers the same way). `--ticker / --since / --until / --limit / --json / --config` flags. Friendly error paths: equity ticker (e.g. AAPL) explains no price source ingested yet and points at SEC EDGAR for filings instead; unknown ticker points at the watchlist; missing data range suggests `--since` adjustments. End-to-end smoke-tested against the live homelab with seeded rows.
- **Evidence:** `pyproject.toml` adds `typer>=0.12` to runtime deps and a `[tool.ruff.lint.per-file-ignores]` for `src/genkei/cli/*.py = ["UP045", "UP007"]` — see G-027 for why. `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` (Typer app + 6 stub registrations + `main()` entry point that catches `SystemExit` to return exit codes), `src/genkei/cli/prices.py` (`prices_cmd` + DB query helper + human-table formatter), `src/genkei/cli/_watchlist.py` (typed dataclasses for crypto/equity/macro entries + lookup helpers + `classify()` for sleeve disambiguation). `tests/cli/test_main.py` covers help output / stub backlog pointers / unknown-ticker error / equity-ticker explanation / crypto query path / `--json` shape / `--since > --until` validation. `tests/cli/test_watchlist.py` covers parsing all three sleeves / case-insensitive lookups / classify dispatch / malformed-entry skip / missing-file error. Tests 223 → 240 (all unit; 25 skipped locally because Docker absent). architecture.md gained D-019 (Typer over Click), G-027 (Python 3.9 venv + Typer annotation eval requires `Optional[T]` in CLI files), G-026 (`enterContext` requires Python 3.11+ — local venv uses `addCleanup` pattern). Phase 3 row in roadmap marked 3/11 done with B-040 (`filings`) and B-042 (`macro`) called out as the next high-leverage subcommands.

### R-032 — Implement `genkei filings` subcommand (B-040)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-16 (branch genkei-filings, commit `dc27f2f`)
- **Outcome:** Second + third real CLI subcommands ship together (B-040 + B-042) once the equity backbone went live after fix-ingest-pipelines. `genkei filings --ticker AAPL` queries `sec.filings` by `cik` (resolved via the shared `genkei.cli._watchlist` helper), filterable by `--form` / `--since` / `--until`; ordered by `filed_at` DESC. Friendly errors steer crypto tickers (e.g. BTC) to `genkei prices`, reject equities without a CIK in the watchlist, and reject `--unit` without `--concept`. **XBRL mode (`--concept`):** switches the query to `sec.facts`. Accepts both bare (`Revenues`) and namespaced (`us-gaap:Revenues`) concept names; bare matching uses `split_part(concept, ':', 2)` since the column stores the namespaced form. `--unit` (USD / shares / USD/shares / pure) filters further. `--json` emits one row per result with all columns; human mode renders a fixed-width table with comma-formatted values. Live smoke against homelab: AAPL FY2025 revenue $416,161,000,000 returned via `--concept Revenues --unit USD`; AAPL 10-K list 2021-2025 returned via `--form 10-K`. **Not shipped (deferred follow-up):** synthesized SEC.gov filing URL in the output — currently surfaces `accession_number` + `primary_document` from which the URL can be constructed.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/cli/filings.py` (`filings_cmd`, `_query_filings`, `_query_facts`, `_format_filings_human`, `_format_facts_human`, `_resolve_equity`), `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` registers via `app.command("filings")(filings.filings_cmd)` and drops the stub. `tests/cli/test_filings.py` covers human/JSON output for both modes, unknown-ticker friendly error, crypto-ticker redirect to `genkei prices`, equity-without-CIK rejection, `--unit` without `--concept` rejection, and `--since > --until` validation. `tests/cli/test_main.py` stub-pointer test repoints from `filings` to `tvl` (B-041 still stub). Tests 251 → 280 (all unit; 25 skipped locally because Docker absent), ruff clean. Phase 3 row advances 4/11 → 6/11.

### R-033 — Implement `genkei macro` subcommand (B-042)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-16 (branch genkei-filings, commit `dc27f2f`)
- **Outcome:** Vintage-aware CLI over `fred.observations`. `genkei macro --series DGS10` returns the time series with `--since` / `--until` filters; series must be present in `macro_series:` of `watchlists.yml` (friendly error names the file). The interesting work is vintage handling — D-013's vintage-aware schema lets the same `ts` carry multiple `realtime_start` rows, and the CLI exposes three modes: **default** collapses to one row per `ts` via `DISTINCT ON (ts) ORDER BY ts DESC, realtime_start DESC` (what we believe today); **`--as-of YYYY-MM-DD`** filters `realtime_start <= as-of` so the result matches what was known on that date (revision archaeology); **`--all-vintages`** returns every revision row with no per-`ts` dedupe. `--as-of` and `--all-vintages` are mutually exclusive. Output tags which mode was used so JSON consumers and humans can tell which slice they're looking at. Live smoke: DGS10 latest 4.47% (2026-05-13 ts, 2026-05-16 vintage); CPIAUCSL `--all-vintages` correctly surfaces 2 revisions per ts (an early-month and mid-month vintage from FRED's vintage_dates rewrite). **Not shipped (deferred follow-up):** comma-separated cross-series (`--series DGS10,DGS2`) from the original acceptance criteria. The vintage modes weren't in the original criteria but materially raise the leverage of the command for revision-aware analysis.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/cli/macro.py` (`macro_cmd`, `_query_observations` with vintage-mode branching, `_format_human`, `_resolve_series`), `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` registers via `app.command("macro")(macro.macro_cmd)` and drops the stub. `tests/cli/test_macro.py` covers human/JSON output, unknown-series friendly error, `--as-of` + `--all-vintages` mutual exclusion, `--since > --until` validation, plus a `QueryObservationsSqlShapeTests` class that captures generated SQL (via a fake cursor) to assert `DISTINCT ON (ts)` presence per mode and `realtime_start <= %s` when `--as-of` is set. Phase 3 row advances 4/11 → 6/11; remaining open: B-041 tvl, B-043 news, B-044 watchlist, B-045 query, B-046 caching, B-047 docs.

### R-034 — Implement `genkei tvl` subcommand (B-041)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-16 (branch cli-tvl-watchlist, commit `2137d58`)
- **Outcome:** Fourth real CLI subcommand. `genkei tvl --chain Ethereum` queries `defillama.chain_tvl` (8K rows live; Ethereum currently $44.2B TVL); supports `--since` / `--until` / `--limit` / `--json` like the other commands. `genkei tvl --protocol aave-v3` queries `defillama.protocol_tvl` — that table is empty today (per-protocol collector not yet wired); the empty path doesn't fail, it prints a hint pointing at `genkei watchlist health` and suggesting `--chain` as the working alternative. Default mode (no scope flag) renders a chains overview: latest TVL per chain sorted desc — a quick "what's tracked" view returning 4 chains (Ethereum, Solana, Bitcoin, Sui). `--chain` and `--protocol` are mutually exclusive. **Not shipped (deferred follow-ups, both noted in commit message):** the `--window 7d` rolling-change classification + the momentum/zombie-risk tagging from the original acceptance criteria (these reference the daily-brief MVP's classifier in `reports/defillama_daily.py`, not ported yet); per-protocol TVL ingester so `--protocol` returns data.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/cli/tvl.py` (`tvl_cmd`, `_query_chain_tvl`, `_query_protocol_tvl`, `_query_chains_overview`, three human formatters), `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` registers via `app.command("tvl")(tvl.tvl_cmd)` and drops the stub. `tests/cli/test_tvl.py` covers human/JSON output for all three modes, `--chain` + `--protocol` mutual exclusion, `--since > --until` validation, the critical empty-protocol-mode-points-at-health-check assertion, and the overview query regression that proves `--limit` applies after the global TVL sort. Phase 3 row advances 6/11 → 8/11.

### R-035 — Implement `genkei watchlist` subcommand (B-044)
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-16 (branch cli-tvl-watchlist, commit `2137d58`)
- **Outcome:** First real CLI subcommand *group* — registered via `app.add_typer(watchlist.app, name="watchlist")` rather than the `app.command(...)` shape the single-action commands use. Three subcommands: **`list`** dumps the watchlist by sleeve (crypto/equities/macro), `--sleeve` filter to one; **`health`** queries `meta.ingest_runs` for the latest run per `(source, endpoint)`, joins with primary-table row counts, and tags each row OK / STALE / FAIL / MISSING / EMPTY — explicitly designed loud because silent ingest failure cost ~4 days last time (G-027/G-028/D-020); **`gaps`** walks every watchlist asset and reports the most recent data point per asset, tagged GAP / NONE / OK against a configurable `--threshold-hours` (default 36, doc notes to raise for monthly/quarterly macro). The `PRIMARY_TABLES` + `EXPECTED_ENDPOINTS` registries are pinned by unit tests so silent registry drift surfaces in CI. First live run of `health` immediately surfaced three real gaps that needed surfacing: `coingecko collect/normalize` MISSING (the keyless cron hasn't run yet post-D-020 merge), `coingecko.market_data` EMPTY (downstream of MISSING), and `defillama.protocol_tvl` EMPTY (per-protocol ingester not active). First live run of `gaps` produced "54 assets, 47 GAP, 7 NONE" — the 7 NONE exactly the 7 crypto watchlist assets matching the EMPTY coingecko table; the 47 GAP includes monthly/quarterly macro series where the default 36h threshold is too tight (expected behavior; the doc tells users to bump `--threshold-hours`).
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (`app` sub-Typer, `list_cmd` / `health_cmd` / `gaps_cmd`, `_query_source_health`, `_query_asset_gaps`, `_health_status_tag`, three human formatters, `PRIMARY_TABLES` + `EXPECTED_ENDPOINTS` registries). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` uses `app.add_typer(watchlist.app, name="watchlist")` and drops the stub. `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` covers `list` default + sleeve filter + invalid sleeve + JSON, `health` status-tag matrix (OK/STALE/FAIL/MISSING/EMPTY plus running/partial as FAIL) + format-surfaces-missing-loudly + JSON, `gaps` formatter + command-renders + JSON, and a `ExpectationsRegistryTests` class that pins the source/endpoint registry. Tests 280 → 319 (+39 new), ruff clean. Phase 3 row advances 6/11 → 8/11; remaining open: B-043 news, B-045 query, B-046 caching, B-047 docs.

### ~~B-079 — SEC Form 4 (insider transactions) ingester~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-16 (branch insider-transactions, commit `76bbf67`)
- **Outcome:** First *new data* added to the lake since CoinGecko — not a query surface over existing rows but a brand-new ingestion path. Form 4 insider transactions were intentionally split out of the original SEC ingester (B-027 option B → D-015) because the structured payload is per-filing XML rather than in the submissions index. **New schema (migration `8a1c7d4f2b96`):** `sec.insiders` entity dim (PK `reporter_cik`) and `sec.form4_transactions` (PK `(accession_number, transaction_idx)`) — both non-derivative (open-market) and derivative (option-style) transactions land in the same wide table with `is_derivative` as the discriminator since the common query pattern is "all insider activity for this issuer." Not a hypertable; ~50-100k rows steady-state at watchlist scope. Indexed `(issuer_cik, transaction_date DESC)` and `(reporter_cik, transaction_date DESC)` for both natural query angles. **Amendment handling:** Form 4/A amendments carry their own accession_number, so they land as separate rows — accession-level rather than vintage-style PK, but functionally equivalent (queries asking "what's current truth" pick the most recent accession). **Collector** (`src/genkei/ingest/sec_form4.py`) walks `sec.filings` for `form_type='4'` rows without a cached `form4_<accession>` blob and fetches the XML from EDGAR Archives (note: public Archives host, not data.sec.gov). URL pattern strips the `xslF345*/` XSLT viewer prefix to get the raw XML basename. Two modes: incremental (`--limit 200`, ~25s, safe for daily cron) and `--backfill` (no limit, ~76 min for 36 634 historical filings at SEC's 8 req/s). Soft-failure mode — a 404 on one filing logs + records partial and continues, doesn't fail the whole run. **Normalizer** (`src/genkei/normalize/sec_form4.py`) parses each `<ownershipDocument>` into insider + transaction rows; handles multiple reporting owners per filing (joint trusts attribute one XML transaction to N rows, one per reporter, with transaction_idx incremented), missing-date rows (skip), malformed XML (skip + log). Default mode picks up every `form4_*` blob whose accession isn't yet in `sec.form4_transactions` — naturally idempotent. **CLI** (`genkei insiders`): `--ticker AAPL` resolves via the watchlist for the issuer-view query; `--reporter-cik` queries by insider across every issuer they file on (auto zero-pads to 10 digits); `--code P/S/A/F/M/G/...` filters by SEC transaction code; `--derivative`/`--non-derivative` mutually exclusive scopes; `--since` / `--until` / `--limit` / `--json` with Decimal→string serialization to preserve precision (matches the post-merge filings fix). Friendly errors steer crypto tickers to `genkei prices` and reject equities without a CIK. **Live smoke:** migration applied (alembic head `8a1c7d4f2b96`); 5-filing incremental collect + normalize cleanly landed 10 unique insiders + 18 transactions; `genkei insiders --ticker JPM` immediately surfaced same-day sells from Erdoes (CEO AWM), Lake (CEO CCB), Petno (Co-CEO CIB), Beer (CIO) on 2026-05-15 — real insider cluster activity on the first real query, which is exactly the signal use case from the original B-079 scoping note ("driven by B-060 insider-buying monitor"). **Not shipped (deferred follow-ups):** historical `--backfill` run (one command away — `python -m genkei.ingest.sec_form4 --backfill` — documented in the commit body but not run since it'd take 76 min); GH Actions workflow extension to chain `collect_form4` + `normalize_form4` after the existing daily SEC steps (the incremental mode is designed for it but the workflow file wasn't touched). B-060 (insider-buying monitor / cluster detection) is the natural next-branch experiment on this data.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260516_create_sec_form4.py` (sec.insiders + sec.form4_transactions, indexes, FK to sec.companies + sec.filings + sec.insiders + meta.ingest_runs). `src/genkei/ingest/sec_form4.py` (`Form4Target` dataclass, `select_uncached_form4s`, `strip_xsl_prefix`, `build_form4_xml_url`, `collect`, soft-failure `_fetch_one`, argparse with `--backfill` / `--limit` / `--json`). `src/genkei/normalize/sec_form4.py` (`parse_form4_xml` returning `(insiders, transactions)` tuple, `_build_transaction_row` covering both branches, `fetch_unnormalized_form4_blobs` with two modes, `normalize` orchestration with insider-row deduplication across the batch). `src/genkei/cli/insiders.py` (`insiders_cmd`, `_query_by_issuer`, `_query_by_reporter`, `_format_human` with optional issuer column for reporter view, `_format_role` for officer/director/10%-owner flags, `_resolve_equity`). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` registers `app.command("insiders")(insiders.insiders_cmd)` and updates the surface comment. `tests/ingest/test_sec_form4.py` covers XSLT-prefix stripping + URL construction. `tests/normalize/test_sec_form4.py` (16 tests) covers non-derivative parsing (real AAPL Borders sale), derivative parsing (Cook option exercise with underlying-security + exercise/expiration dates), multi-reporter (joint trust attributing one transaction to two reporters with separate transaction_idx), malformed XML, missing issuer-CIK, holdings-only filings (insider row but no transactions), missing-date transaction skip, and the `_payload_to_xml` unwrapper. `tests/cli/test_insiders.py` (13 tests) covers mutual-exclusion guards (--ticker/--reporter-cik, --derivative/--non-derivative), watchlist resolution + crypto-ticker redirect + equity-without-CIK rejection, reporter-CIK auto-padding to 10 digits, --since/--until validation, and Decimal serialization. Tests 323 → 354 (+31 new), ruff clean. Phase 3 row advances 8/11 → 9/11; remaining open: B-043 news, B-045 query, B-046 caching, B-047 docs.

### ~~B-060 — Experiment: insider-buying monitor~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-17 (branch insider-cluster-detector, commit `5a69607`)
- **Outcome:** First Phase 5 experiment on top of the data B-079 just landed — the experiment B-079 was scoped to feed. **Pure detector** in `src/genkei/experiments/insider_clusters.py` (first occupant of the experiments/ module): `detect_clusters(transactions, *, direction, min_reporters=2, window_days=7)` groups by issuer, sorts by date, greedily emits non-overlapping windows where ≥ `min_reporters` distinct insiders transacted. `window_days` is *span* (last − first), not gap-between-consecutive — "5 officers bought within a week" is materially stronger than "5 over a year with no >7-day gap." Direction-filter helpers (`query_buy_candidates` / `query_sell_candidates`) load from `sec.form4_transactions`: buys are code='P' + acquired_disposed='A' + non-derivative (excludes 'A' grants/awards = comp); sells are code='S' + acquired_disposed='D' + non-derivative (excludes 'F' tax-withholding). **CLI** (`genkei insider-clusters`) wraps the detector with `--ticker`, `--sell`, `--min-reporters` (defaults 2), `--window-days` (defaults 7), `--since`/`--until`, `--json`, `--config`. Default scope is the whole watchlist (no --ticker filter); per project convention the `--json` mode emits Decimals as strings to preserve precision. Human output renders date_range × reporter_count × $value table with up to 4 reporters per row + role tags (officer/director/10%-owner). **Live smoke** after a partial backfill (~2 450 Form 4 blobs normalized into 10 234 transactions across 43 issuers, including 141 cross-issuer rows post-G-031): canonical JPM 2026-05-15 4-officer sell cluster ($6.55M, Erdoes/Lake/Petno/Beer) surfaces exactly; sell view since 2026-01-01 with ≥3 reporters also returns AVGO 4-officer 2026-03-16..17 ($88M), META 4-officer 2026-02-15..20 ($24M, CFO/CTO/COO/dir), AMD 4-officer 2026-02-11..18 ($35M led by Lisa Su), MSTR 4-officer 2026-03-24..31; buy view since 2024-01-01 surfaces the **ValueAct CRM 8-reporter 2025-12-05 $200M conviction-buy cluster** — exactly the activist-position-taking signal the experiment was built to catch. **Scope departures from the original acceptance:** built as a CLI module rather than a notebook (the project picked Claude Code over notebooks per D-017; the CLI is the canonical query layer); ships *both* buy and sell detection since the algorithm is symmetrical and sell clusters surfaced immediately in real data. **Not shipped (deferred follow-ups):** the "watchlist alert hook" piece — no scheduled `/schedule` Routine that periodically runs the detector and writes a `reports/insider-clusters-YYYY-MM-DD.md`; threshold tuning to suppress TSM-style 28-reporter "clusters" that are scheduled ESPP/comp purchases coded as 'P' (user can already filter via `--min-reporters` or a $-value cap; a built-in `--min-dollar-value` flag would be a small enhancement). **B-079 cleanups bundled into this branch:** `.github/workflows/sec-daily.yml` extended to chain `collect_form4` + `normalize_form4` after the existing SEC normalize step so new Form 4s land daily without manual triggering; migration `2c9f5e1d3a47` (G-031) drops `form4_transactions_issuer_cik_fkey` because Form 4s about Company B can appear in Company A's `/submissions/` index when the filer cross-serves on multiple boards (the XML's `<issuerCik>` is the authority and often points outside our watchlist — caught the first time the backfill normalized beyond the smoke set, ~141 cross-issuer rows now landing cleanly).
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/experiments/insider_clusters.py` (`Transaction`, `ReporterSummary`, `Cluster` dataclasses; `detect_clusters` with guards + greedy windowing + sort by reporter_count desc; `_summarize` aggregating per-reporter shares/value; `query_buy_candidates` / `query_sell_candidates` SQL loaders with --since/--until/--issuer-ciks scoping). `src/genkei/cli/insider_clusters.py` (`insider_clusters_cmd`, `_cluster_to_dict` for JSON, `_format_human` with per-reporter role tags + up-to-4-then-+N display, `_resolve_equity_or_exit`, `_ticker_for_cik` reverse-lookup). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` registers `app.command("insider-clusters")(insider_clusters.insider_clusters_cmd)` and bumps the surface comment. `migrations/versions/20260517_drop_form4_issuer_fk.py` (G-031). `.github/workflows/sec-daily.yml` adds `Collect uncached Form 4 XML primary docs` + `Normalize Form 4 raw blobs` steps after the SEC normalize, bumps timeout 20→25 min. `tests/experiments/test_insider_clusters.py` (24 tests across 7 classes — guards, no-clusters cases, two-reporter-within-window, outside-window-no-cluster, window-boundary-inclusive, issuer isolation, greedy advance behavior with multi-cluster sequences, config overrides, per-reporter aggregation correctness, value=None when no prices, sort order by reporter_count). `tests/cli/test_insider_clusters.py` (10 tests — argument validation, default-vs-sell loader dispatch, ticker→cik scoping, watchlist error paths, human output ticker lookup, JSON Decimal-as-string serialization). Tests 354 → 394 (+40 new), ruff clean. Phase 5 first signal-generating experiment shipped. Phase 3 CLI surface remains 6/7 real subcommands (B-043 news + B-045 query still stub).

### ~~B-045 — Implement `genkei query` escape hatch~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-17 (branch genkei-query, commit `b30ce92`)
- **Outcome:** Closes out the Phase 3 CLI surface — now 7/7 real subcommands; only `news` (B-043) remains stubbed pending an actual GDELT data source. The escape hatch unlocks the "on-demand AI researcher" use case in CLAUDE.md: with the lake at 192k insider transactions + 442k XBRL facts + 229k macro observations, the agent can now ask any ad-hoc question the typed subcommands don't express (the original B-079 split intentionally avoided guessing schemas; this is what makes that bet pay off). **Three safety guards** per the acceptance criteria, all enforced server-side: (1) **read-only** — every query runs inside `BEGIN READ ONLY ... ROLLBACK`, Postgres rejects DML/DDL at the engine level. Shipped as a transaction mode rather than a dedicated DB role (functionally equivalent enforcement, no separate role to provision; a role-based variant is a small follow-up if/when we want belt-and-suspenders). (2) **statement_timeout** — `SET LOCAL statement_timeout = N` per query (default 30s, max 300s), inlined into the SQL string because `SET LOCAL` doesn't accept bind params (safe — value is range-validated int). (3) **row cap** — user's SQL wrapped in `SELECT * FROM (<user_sql>) AS q LIMIT N` so the cap is enforced server-side (default 100, max 100 000); capped results render a `(row cap)` marker in table mode. **Defense in depth:** the wrap also turns INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE into a parse-time SyntaxError (DML can't appear in a subquery), so the READ ONLY mode is a backstop rather than the only line of defense. **Multi-statement input** (`;` outside string literals) is rejected at parse time via a character-walk that respects quote state and Postgres's `''` escape convention — `WHERE col = 'a;b'` and `'O''Brien;was;here'` don't false-positive. Adds `--file` for SQL from disk, `--format table|json|csv` (plus `--json` shorthand matching other subcommands), and the same Decimal→string JSON convention the other subcommands use. Known Postgres errors (QueryCanceled, SyntaxError, ReadOnlySqlTransaction, UndefinedColumn/Table/Function, InsufficientPrivilege, GroupingError, DatatypeMismatch, InvalidTextRepresentation) render as one-line agent-readable `query error [Kind]: msg` lines on stderr, not stack traces — important for keeping the agent's debugging loop tight. **Live smoke:** `SELECT count(*) FROM sec.form4_transactions` returned 192,323; JSON top-issuer query returned CRM 52k, GOOG 25k; `--timeout-seconds 1` against `SELECT pg_sleep(3)` cancelled in <1s; INSERT cleanly rejected; `SELECT 1; SELECT 2` rejected at parse position 8; `--limit 5` against `generate_series(1,1000)` returned exactly 5 with the `(row cap)` marker. **Not shipped (deferred follow-ups):** dedicated read-only Postgres role (the transaction-mode guarantee is functionally equivalent); stdin pipe input (positional + `--file` cover the common shapes); EXPLAIN-wrap shortcut (users can write `EXPLAIN SELECT ...` themselves and it works fine). B-046 (CLI session caching) is the next CLI item but lower priority — premature until we feel query latency.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/cli/query.py` (`query_cmd` Typer command, `execute_readonly` with READ ONLY + statement_timeout + LIMIT wrap, `wrap_query_for_safety` + `_strip_trailing_semicolons` + `find_multi_statement_position` (quote-state walk), `_read_sql` / `_validate_sql` input handling, three formatters `format_table` / `format_json` / `format_csv`, `USER_ERROR_TYPES` allowlist of Postgres errors that render cleanly, `_json_default` for Decimal + datetime serialization). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` registers `app.command("query")(query.query_cmd)`, drops the `_stub("query", "B-045")`, and bumps the surface comment from `[B-045, stub]` to `[B-045 ✓]`. `tests/cli/test_query.py` (39 tests across 8 classes — strip-trailing helpers, multi-statement detector with quote-state coverage (`'a;b'`, `'O''Brien'`, `'a'; SELECT 2`), wrap behavior including trailing-semicolon strip, table/JSON/CSV formatters with edge cases (Decimal precision, embedded commas, None handling, dates as ISO, cap marker visibility), and CLI guards + execution paths with `execute_readonly` mocked to assert error rendering for ReadOnlySqlTransaction / QueryCanceled / SyntaxError). Tests 394 → 433 (+39 new), ruff clean. Phase 3 CLI surface advances 9/11 → 10/11 (only `news` / B-043 stub remains, blocked on a GDELT data source rather than CLI scaffolding).

### ~~B-049 — Write the structured research methodology + reflection prompts~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-17 (branch research-methodology, commit `6d37f3e`)
- **Outcome:** First half of the Phase 4 agent harness work, shipped alongside B-050 since the prompts and the skills that load them are useless in isolation. **`prompts/research-methodology.md`** walks the structured checklist exactly as scoped in D-018: Frame the question (asset / sleeve / horizon / what would change my mind) → Macro context (default `genkei macro --series DGS10 / T10Y2Y / VIXCLS / DTWEXBGS / BAMLH0A0HYM2`, vintage-aware via `--as-of` when timing matters) → Asset fundamentals (split by sleeve: `genkei filings --concept us-gaap:RevenueFromContractWithCustomerExcludingAssessedTax` for equity revenue trajectory, balance-sheet pulls; `genkei tvl --chain` for crypto trajectory) → Flow & positioning (`genkei insiders` + `genkei insider-clusters` for the rare conviction signal; stablecoin supply via `genkei query` for on-chain dry powder) → Cross-source signals (where two+ sources agree or contradict — Phase A) → Counter-thesis (Phase B — strongest case for being wrong, with specific trigger conditions) → Conclusion (recommendation + sleeve + horizon + confidence calibrated against prior reflections + key risks + position-sizing implication + reassessment trigger) → Decision-log entry. Every section calls out concrete CLI commands so the methodology is operational, not abstract. **`prompts/reflect-on-decisions.md`** walks the outcome-pairing cycle: scan `docs/research/decisions/` for `status: pending` files past their horizon (weeks → 28d, months → 180d, years → 365d), pull realized prices via `genkei prices --since <date> --until <today> --json`, compute alpha vs SPY (equity) or BTC (crypto), append `## Outcome` block with resolved date + asset return (annualized when horizon >1y) + benchmark return + alpha + trigger-condition status + a specific 2-3 sentence reflection (which signal carried / failed, calibration miss, takeaway for future decisions in the same sleeve/signal pattern), then flip `status: pending` → `resolved` (or `deferred` when equity-price feed is missing — explicit honest record over fabricated outcome data). Both prompts are committed to the repo and discoverable cold from `CLAUDE.md`'s References block.
- **Evidence:** `prompts/research-methodology.md` (full structured checklist with concrete `genkei` commands per section, Phase A / Phase B separation, decision-log integration). `prompts/reflect-on-decisions.md` (eligibility logic, per-sleeve outcome computation, reflection content guidelines with bad/good examples, optional aggregate snapshot section). `CLAUDE.md` References block updated to point at both prompts so cold reads find them. No Python deps added (pure prose deliverables). Validated end-to-end together with B-050 — the prompts are referenced by the two `.claude/skills/` SKILL.md files in the same commit.

### ~~B-050 — Implement the decision-log + skill scaffolding~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-17 (branch research-methodology, commit `6d37f3e`)
- **Outcome:** Second half of Phase 4 agent harness — the directory + template + skills that turn the prompts from B-049 into an operational workflow. **Decision log** lives at `docs/research/decisions/` with a `_template.md` skeleton (frontmatter: date, asset, sleeve, horizon, confidence, status, trigger_reassessment; body: Frame / Macro / Fundamentals / Flow / Phase A / Phase B / Conclusion; placeholder `## Outcome (filled in by /reflect-decisions)` section) + a `README.md` documenting the directory contract. **One concrete example decision** lands alongside the scaffolding: `2025-12-05-valueact-crm-buy-cluster.md` — the ValueAct Capital Management 8-reporter $200M activist add-on cluster surfaced live by `genkei insider-clusters --since 2025-12-01 --until 2025-12-31` against the homelab lake. The example walks the methodology end-to-end (macro regime call → CRM fundamentals via `genkei filings --concept us-gaap:RevenueFromContractWithCustomerExcludingAssessedTax` → flow via `genkei insider-clusters --ticker CRM` → Phase A bull/bear case → Phase B counter-thesis with explicit AI-disruption trigger → Buy / equity-core / years / medium-confidence conclusion with sizing and reassessment triggers). It makes the abstraction tangible AND seeds the `/reflect-decisions` cycle — `status: pending` until horizon (2026-12-05) or trigger fires earlier. **Two project-local skills** mirror the `run-missions` shape (the only prior project skill): `.claude/skills/research/SKILL.md` (`/research <question>` — pre-flight reads the methodology + recent reflections + runs `genkei watchlist health` sanity-check, walks the methodology section-by-section against the user's question, lands a decision file, commits + pushes); `.claude/skills/reflect-decisions/SKILL.md` (`/reflect-decisions` — manual today, reasonable weekly cadence, designed to wire to `/schedule` once exercised; idempotent because it skips already-`resolved` entries). Both skills register on the Claude Code skills list immediately after SKILL.md creation (verified in this session — both appeared after the file was written). **Frontmatter validator** (`tests/test_research_decisions.py`, +33 tests across 3 classes) pins the contract `/reflect-decisions` depends on: required keys present (date / asset / sleeve / horizon / confidence / status / trigger_reassessment), `date` is ISO, `sleeve` ∈ {equity-core, crypto-core, crypto-tactical, macro-aware}, `horizon` ∈ {weeks, months, years}, `confidence` ∈ {low, medium, high}, `status` ∈ {pending, resolved, deferred}, `trigger_reassessment` is a non-empty string, pending files include the `## Outcome` placeholder for the reflection cycle to fill in, template itself satisfies the contract + includes Phase A / Phase B headings. Same loud-failure philosophy as `genkei watchlist health` for ingest sources — silent drift in decision-log shape would mis-classify entries to `/reflect-decisions`. **Live `/research` demo** of the user-driven flow is the one piece NOT exercised in this branch (the SKILL.md is registered + the example decision file proves the template / frontmatter / validator path works end-to-end, but no actual `/research` session was driven through to write a new decision file). It's a separate session whenever you want to kick the tires — the ValueAct CRM cluster on the lake is the natural first real test, or any new cluster that surfaces in tomorrow's daily SEC run.
- **Evidence:** `docs/research/README.md` (directory structure + frontmatter contract + workflow + why-this-exists framing). `docs/research/decisions/_template.md` (frontmatter skeleton + section headers + Outcome placeholder). `docs/research/decisions/2025-12-05-valueact-crm-buy-cluster.md` (one concrete worked example from a real cluster on the lake; status: pending; resolves at horizon or trigger). `.claude/skills/research/SKILL.md` (pre-flight + walk + land + commit + constraints + skill boundary; mirrors `run-missions` shape). `.claude/skills/reflect-decisions/SKILL.md` (pre-flight + walk + outcome pairing + reflection-content guidelines + commit + aggregate-snapshot). `tests/test_research_decisions.py` (`DecisionDirStructureTests` + `DecisionFrontmatterContractTests` + `TemplateFileShapeTests` — 33 tests pinning the contract). `CLAUDE.md` References block updated. Tests 433 → 466 (+33 new), ruff clean.

### ~~B-081 — DeFiLlama per-protocol TVL collector~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-17 (branch tvl-collector, commit `f2c4bb6`)
- **Outcome:** Closes the persistent `defillama.protocol_tvl` EMPTY signal that `watchlist health` has flagged since the watchlist command shipped (R-034). Surfaced again as a research-blocker in the LINK /research session (could not pull Chainlink TVS). The pre-existing `_backfill_protocols` + `normalize_protocol_history` infrastructure already handled the bulk-of-~3k-protocols backfill case; this branch wires a small watchlist subset (~7 protocols) into the daily flow so the table stays current without the 76-minute full backfill on every run. **New `protocols:` section** in `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml` with 8 primary-tier slugs grouped by category: oracle (chainlink-requests, chainlink-staking), lending (aave-v3, compound-v3), DEX (uniswap-v3, curve-dex), liquid staking (lido), CDP / stablecoin issuer (sky-lending — note: MakerDAO rebranded to Sky in 2024 and DefiLlama split it into sky-lending + sky-rwa; chainlink is split into -requests + -staking because there's no top-level `chainlink` slug in their catalog). Slug choices reflect actual DefiLlama catalog rather than the acceptance criterion's "chains we already cover" suggestion — category coverage proved more useful for cross-source signals. **`ProtocolEntry` dataclass + `Watchlist.protocols` + `find_protocol()` + `classify()` routing** in `cli/_watchlist.py`. **`collect()` extended in `src/genkei/ingest/defillama.py`** — loads watchlist slugs via inline `_load_watchlist_protocol_slugs()` (mirrors the coingecko + sec inline-YAML-parse pattern; deliberately doesn't import from cli/), then for each slug fetches `/protocol/{slug}` and stores as a `protocol_<slug>` raw blob using `PROTOCOL_HISTORY_PREFIX`. Soft-failure per slug — a 404 doesn't fail the run. **`normalize()` extended in `src/genkei/normalize/defillama.py`** — daily normalize now iterates `protocol_*` blobs (in addition to the four fixed-name blobs it already processed) and dispatches them through the pre-existing `normalize_protocol_history` function. Bulk-upserts to `defillama.protocol_tvl` after the `defillama.protocols` upsert so the FK on slug is satisfied. **Live smoke against homelab landed 109k rows across 7 slugs spanning 2019-2026**: curve-dex 30k, uniswap-v3 30k, aave-v3 19k, compound-v3 9k, lido 9k, sky-lending 3k, chainlink-staking 2k. `genkei tvl --protocol aave-v3` returns the multi-chain breakdown (Arbitrum $463M, Avalanche $326M, Base $420M, Binance $171M, etc.); `genkei tvl --protocol chainlink-staking` shows the v0.2 pool at $414.8M with a small day-over-day outflow visible — **partially fills the LINK on-chain signal gap from the /research session even before B-082 ships**. `defillama.protocol_tvl` flipped EMPTY → OK in `watchlist health`. **Known edge case (deferred, not blocking):** `chainlink-requests` returns 200 from DefiLlama but produces 0 normalized rows. Its `chainTvls` payload uses only sub-bucket chain names (`Ethereum-borrowed`-style) which the normalizer skips per G-013. Worth investigating in a follow-up if Chainlink-Requests volume becomes important to track — would either need a normalizer relaxation for known sub-bucket-only payloads, or a different source for oracle request volume. **Mild waste, not blocking:** `/protocol/{slug}` returns full history on every call, so the daily run effectively reprocesses all history — idempotent via the `(slug, chain, ts)` PK upsert so harmless, but could be trimmed to recent-window-only if 109k rows × 7 protocols / day ever becomes a perf issue.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml` (new `protocols:` section, primary tier of 8 slugs with categories + rationales explaining why each is in the lake). `src/genkei/cli/_watchlist.py` (`ProtocolEntry` frozen dataclass, `Watchlist.protocols` field, `find_protocol()` case-insensitive lookup, `classify()` returns `"protocol"` for matching slugs, `_optional_string` helper unchanged). `src/genkei/ingest/defillama.py` (`_load_watchlist_protocol_slugs()` inline YAML parse with primary-first ordering + dedup + missing-file safety; `collect()` now accepts `watchlist_path` kwarg, builds `protocol_<slug>` blobs after the existing top-level fetches, soft-failure per slug). `src/genkei/normalize/defillama.py` (`normalize()` extended with a `protocol_*` blob iteration that calls the pre-existing `normalize_protocol_history`; bulk-upsert to `defillama.protocol_tvl` ordered after `defillama.protocols` upsert for FK satisfaction). `tests/cli/test_watchlist.py` adds `LoadProtocolsTests` (6 tests — primary+secondary parsing, missing-slug skip, case-insensitive find, classify routing, absent-section safety, packaged-watchlist-has-at-least-one-primary-protocol contract pin). `tests/ingest/test_defillama.py` adds `WatchlistProtocolSlugLoaderTests` (5 tests — primary-first ordering, dedup, missing-protocols-section returns empty, missing-file returns empty (not raise), malformed-entry skip). `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` updated for the new required `protocols` field on the `Watchlist` constructor. Tests 468 → 479 (+11 new), ruff clean. Phase 2 ingest gap of "protocol_tvl persistently EMPTY" — closed.

### ~~B-082 — Chainlink LINK staking flow ingester~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-17 (branch chainlink-requests, commits `b3fad44` + `0f93360`)
- **Outcome:** The LINK /research session's #1 data gap — Chainlink v0.2 staking-pool *flow* (stake-ins vs unbond-exits over time, as distinct from the TVL snapshot B-081 added). All five acceptance criteria covered infrastructure-wise; live data flow gated on Etherscan API key config (operational, not engineering — same precedent as B-034 CoinGecko / D-020). **Collector** (`src/genkei/ingest/onchain_staking.py`) hits Etherscan V2 `/v2/api?module=logs&action=getLogs&chainid=1` per pool — V1 was deprecated mid-2025 and now hard-fails. `PoolConfig` dataclass parameterizes the schema-generic table; Chainlink v0.2 community staking pool (`0xBc10f2E862ED4502...DFCDB5e`, deployed Nov 2023 at block ~18638000) is the only pool today but Lido / RocketPool / EigenLayer slot in by adding entries to `DEFAULT_POOLS`. Two modes: `--backfill` (deployment block → head) and incremental (default; resume from `max(block_number)` per protocol_slug). Block-range walked in 50k-block chunks (~7 days each on Ethereum) to stay under the 1000-log/call Etherscan response cap. Soft-failure per chunk; per-pool errors bubble as run-level partials without failing the whole batch. **Graceful-skip-when-no-key path** matches D-020: ETHERSCAN_API_KEY missing → records a successful run with 0 rows + a loud one-line warning pointing at `https://etherscan.io/myapikey`. Verified live (run id=64, status=success, rows_written=0, metadata.has_api_key=False). User registers a free key + `gh secret set ETHERSCAN_API_KEY ...` whenever they want events to flow; the code is then ready end-to-end. **Schema choice** (migration `5d3e8b9c1a02`, applied): `onchain.staking_events` rather than the acceptance criterion's suggested `chainlink.staking_flow` — generic across protocols was offered as the alternative ("or under a broader `onchain.*` schema if we want to generalize") and is the right call given the obvious near-term extensions (Lido, RocketPool, EigenLayer). PK `(tx_hash, log_index, block_timestamp)`, hypertable on block_timestamp with 90-day chunks + compression policy mirrors sec.facts (R-019). Indexes on (protocol_slug, block_timestamp DESC) and (staker_address, block_timestamp DESC) — same query shapes as the SEC Form 4 indexes. **Watchlist health integration:** `onchain_staking` added to PRIMARY_TABLES + RECURRING_ENDPOINTS (`collect` only — no separate normalize step since logs decode inline). Live `genkei watchlist health` correctly surfaces the new source as EMPTY until a key is configured — the same loud-failure visibility the rest of the lake gets. **Sister branch wins bundled here:** (a) dropped `chainlink-requests` from the protocols watchlist after diagnosing that DefiLlama returns the payload but with empty `tvl`/`tokens`/`tokensInUsd` arrays (the protocol genuinely doesn't lock value); (b) updated B-083 (Chainlink Labs revenue) with the discovery that DefiLlama's `/summary/fees/{slug}` endpoint is free and covers Chainlink oracle service fees, raising B-083 from low → medium priority and rewriting its acceptance criteria around the free path — eliminates the paid-API gating. **Not shipped (deferred follow-ups, all documented in B-082's acceptance text):** the `genkei staking --asset LINK` CLI subcommand (explicit "separate item if/when needed" deferral); the aggregate views (cumulative pool size, net-flow-per-day, distinct-staker-count) are queryable via `genkei query` against `onchain.staking_events` rather than a typed surface; USD amount backfill via coingecko price join (column exists as NULLable, filled later); GH Actions workflow wiring (skipped — when ETHERSCAN_API_KEY lands, the existing schedule mechanism can be added then with a small cron).
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260517_create_onchain_staking_events.py` (`onchain` schema + `onchain.staking_events` table + 2 indexes + hypertable + compression policy; applied to homelab — alembic head 2c9f5e1d3a47 → 5d3e8b9c1a02). `src/genkei/ingest/onchain_staking.py` (`PoolConfig` dataclass + `CHAINLINK_V02_POOL` constant + `DEFAULT_POOLS` list; `resolve_api_key()` returns Optional[str] with whitespace handling; `fetch_logs()` Etherscan V2 wrapper handling success / "No records found" benign-empty / real-error-raise; pure hex decoders `hex_to_int` / `hex_to_address` / `decode_amount_token` for 18-decimal LINK; `event_type_for_topic()` topic→name mapping; `parse_log()` log→row decode with skip-on-malformed; `iter_block_chunks()` range splitter; `latest_block_for_pool()` resume helper; `fetch_current_head_block()` Etherscan eth_blockNumber proxy; `collect()` orchestrator with graceful-skip-on-no-key + per-pool head-block resolution + per-chunk soft-failure; argparse with `--backfill` / `--json`). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (PRIMARY_TABLES + RECURRING_ENDPOINTS gain `onchain_staking` entries; comment explains why onchain_staking lists only `collect`). `tests/ingest/test_onchain_staking.py` (27 new tests across 8 classes — hex helpers (canonical forms + empty/0x edge / lowercase / short-payload reject); 18-decimal token amount decoding (full LINK, fractional, first-word-only when data has multiple words); event-topic classification (Staked / Unstaked / unknown skip); per-event parsing (canonical Staked + Unstaked, unknown-topic skip, missing-topics skip, missing-staker-topic skip); block-range chunking (small / split / exact-multiple); resolve_api_key environment handling (unset / empty / whitespace / trimmed); Etherscan fetch_logs response shapes (success / "No records found" benign empty / real-error raise); collect() graceful-skip path (no DB, no network when key missing — mocked ingest_run + assertions on metadata.has_api_key=False). `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` `ExpectationsRegistryTests` updated for the registry expansion (now expects 5 sources including onchain_staking; collect-only for onchain_staking, both collect+normalize for the four classic ingesters). Tests 479 → 506 (+27 new), ruff clean.

### ~~B-083 — Chainlink Labs revenue / oracle-service-fee data~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-18 (branch chainlink-coverage, commits `c1135be` + `dacc532`)
- **Outcome:** Closes the **second half** of the LINK /research data gap. B-082 covered on-chain staking flow; this covers oracle service demand (fees) + protocol-captured revenue. Together they're the fundamentals trifecta for LINK that the original /research session couldn't get. Built around the free DefiLlama path the chainlink-requests B-081 follow-up surfaced (rewriting B-083's acceptance criteria to drop the paid-API gating). **API-shape discovery during live smoke:** the bare `/summary/revenue/{slug}` endpoint mentioned in the rewritten acceptance criteria turned out to return 500 across the board (DefiLlama deprecated it). Both fees and revenue actually live behind `/summary/fees/{slug}?dataType=dailyFees|dailyRevenue` — same endpoint, query-param differentiator. First implementation used the deprecated path and 0 revenue rows landed; one-line URL fix re-landed all 11k revenue rows on the next collect. **Schema** (migration `7e4d2a1f8b35`, applied): `defillama.protocol_fees` hypertable on ts (30-day chunks, compression matches `protocol_tvl`), PK `(slug, ts)` with FK to `defillama.protocols`. Two nullable value columns (`fees_usd`, `revenue_usd`) rather than one because revenue is a *subset* of fees in DefiLlama's model — fees = total paid by users; revenue = protocol's captured cut after passthroughs to LPs/node-operators. Some protocols only have fees (revenue=NULL); the (slug, ts) key carries both when both are known so queries don't need a JOIN. **Collector + normalizer extensions** to `genkei.ingest.defillama` + `genkei.normalize.defillama`: collect() now hits both dataType variants per watchlist slug with independent soft-fail (a 500 on revenue doesn't block fees for the same slug). New `normalize_protocol_fee_series()` parses the shared `totalDataChart` shape with a `value_field` parameter routing to the right column; new `merge_fee_revenue_rows()` collapses parallel fees + revenue row sets into one row per (slug, ts) — needed because a naive bulk_upsert would NULL-out the column from the first insert when the second one lands. **Critical pre-smoke bug fixed:** the new `protocol_fees_*` and `protocol_revenue_*` blob prefixes both start with `protocol_`, which would collide with the existing PROTOCOL_HISTORY_PREFIX dispatcher in both `normalize()` and `normalize_backfill()`. Explicit prefix-check order (fees + revenue first, then history) prevents the collision. **chainlink-requests restored to the watchlist** (removed yesterday because /protocol/ had empty TVL; /summary/fees/ has rich data — $11k/day today, $6.5M all-time, 888 daily points back to Aug 2023). The protocols collector + fees collector both iterate every slug and each soft-fails per-slug, so a slug can participate in only one without breaking the other. **Live smoke against homelab** landed 11,234 rows across 8 slugs spanning 2019-2026: sky-lending 2373 rows ($1.1M fees / $511k revenue today), curve-dex 2021, lido 1844 ($1.4M / $140k), uniswap-v3 1838 ($502k / $83k — only 568/1838 revenue rows due to DefiLlama coverage), aave-v3 1523 ($1.1M / $145k), **chainlink-requests 888 fees rows + 342 revenue rows ($11k fees today, revenue trailing to NULL)** — the actionable LINK signal: DefiLlama-attributed Chainlink protocol revenue dropping toward zero, real bearish data for the next LINK research session. `defillama.protocol_fees` flipped EMPTY → OK in `watchlist health`. **Two scope deviations from the rewritten acceptance:** (a) the original on-chain ingest path was meant to stay as fallback — didn't ship since DefiLlama coverage is complete enough; can re-open if data quality drops. (b) No new `genkei fees --protocol …` typed CLI surface; data is queryable via `genkei query` for now. **Two data-quality follow-ups noted, neither blocking:** chainlink-requests latest-day revenue is NULL (older days had values trending down); uniswap-v3 only has 30% revenue coverage (likely a DefiLlama data-quality issue, not ours).
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260518_create_defillama_protocol_fees.py` (defillama.protocol_fees table + ts hypertable + compression policy; applied to homelab — alembic head 5d3e8b9c1a02 → 7e4d2a1f8b35). `src/genkei/ingest/defillama.py` (new PROTOCOL_FEES_PREFIX + PROTOCOL_REVENUE_PREFIX constants; `collect()` extended with per-slug per-dataType loop and per-(slug,kind) soft-failure). `src/genkei/normalize/defillama.py` (`normalize_protocol_fee_series()` payload parser with value_field routing; `merge_fee_revenue_rows()` (slug, ts) collapser; `normalize()` + `normalize_backfill()` dispatch loops extended with the prefix-collision protection). `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml` (chainlink-requests restored with a comment explaining the participate-in-fees-only pattern). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (PRIMARY_TABLES `defillama` entry gains `defillama.protocol_fees`). `tests/normalize/test_defillama.py` adds two new classes — `NormalizeProtocolFeeSeriesTests` (5 tests: totalDataChart parsing, value_field routing, intra-payload dedup, missing-totalDataChart skip, non-dict skip) and `MergeFeeRevenueRowsTests` (5 tests: fees-only, revenue-only, both-merge-into-one, different-ts-stays-separate, different-slugs-stays-separate). Tests 515 → 525 (+10 new), ruff clean. Closes the chainlink coverage thread on this branch's free-data scope: B-081 + B-082 + B-083 covered TVL + flow + service demand. The next chainlink-related item (B-086 full staking surface mapping) is research, not new code; B-084 oracle market share remains paid-data gated and on hold.

### ~~B-062 — Experiment: crypto protocol revenue vs token price~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-18 (branch experiment-crypto-protocol, commit `b69e715`)
- **Outcome:** Second Phase 5 experiment (first since `insider-clusters` shipped under B-060) and the first cross-source crypto fundamentals signal. Joins the data B-083 just landed (`defillama.protocol_fees`, ~11k rows across 8 slugs) against `coingecko.market_data` (~7 tokens) to compute trailing P/F (price-to-fees) and P/R (price-to-revenue) ratios over time, then diagnoses divergence between price-trend and revenue-trend over a configurable lookback window. The signal the LINK research session was trying to detect manually — Chainlink-attributed protocol revenue trending down while LINK price held — now surfaces automatically. **Scope departure from the original "Notebook + per-protocol results" acceptance criteria:** built as a CLI subcommand + experiments module rather than a notebook, per D-017 (Claude Code over notebooks). Mirrors the `insider_clusters` shape exactly — pure detector in `experiments/`, thin CLI wrapper in `cli/`. **Module** (`src/genkei/experiments/protocol_revenue.py`): four dataclasses (`FeeRevenuePoint`, `PricePoint`, `Snapshot`, `DivergenceReport`); `build_snapshots(fees, prices, *, window_days)` joins per-day, computes inclusive trailing-window aggregates, annualizes via `(trailing * 365) / window_days` (multiplication first to avoid Decimal rounding), divides market cap by annualized for P/F + P/R; `diagnose_divergence(snapshots, *, slug, coingecko_id, window_days, lookback_days, significance_pct)` picks the latest snapshot whose ts ≤ now-lookback as the baseline and emits a four-way classification (`price-leads-up` / `price-leads-down` / `aligned` / `insufficient-data`) with the per-side material/non-material logic handling the asymmetric cases (price moves while revenue flat = divergence, etc.); `load_fee_series` + `load_price_series` are the lake helpers. **CLI** (`src/genkei/cli/revenue_divergence.py` → `genkei revenue-divergence`): default mode iterates every watchlist protocol with a `coingecko_id` mapping and renders one row per protocol sorted by absolute divergence magnitude; `--slug` zooms to one protocol's detail; `--since DATE` adds the per-day snapshot time series to the output; `--window-days` / `--lookback-days` / `--significance-pct` tune the math; `--json` for agent consumption. **Watchlist extension**: `ProtocolEntry` gains an optional `coingecko_id` field; pre-populated for all 8 watchlist protocols (`chainlink-staking` + `chainlink-requests` → `chainlink`; `aave-v3` → `aave`; `compound-v3` → `compound-governance-token`; `uniswap-v3` → `uniswap`; `curve-dex` → `curve-dao-token`; `lido` → `lido-dao`; `sky-lending` → `sky`) so the experiment activates automatically as more tokens land in the CoinGecko watchlist. **Live smoke against homelab Postgres**: chainlink-requests flagged `price-leads-up` with price +13.0% / revenue -9.6% over 90d and P/F expanding 1581x → 1976x; chainlink-staking flagged `price-leads-up` with price +13.0% / revenue +3.9% (price expanding faster than fees) and P/F 130x; other 6 mapped protocols cleanly tagged `insufficient-data` because their tokens aren't in the coingecko watchlist yet — loud "missing data" tag mirrors the `watchlist health` philosophy. The two Chainlink slugs sharing the same LINK price denominator but different fee streams validates that the join shape produces meaningfully different signals per (slug, token) pair. **Bugs surfaced + fixed during development**: (a) `from __future__ import annotations` breaks Typer's `Annotated[bool, typer.Option("--json")]` parsing — Typer falls back to the parameter-name-derived `--json-out`; removed the future-import + converted parameter annotations from `X | None` to `Optional[X]` to match the project's CLI convention.
