(b) Decimal `(a * b) / c` and `a * (b / c)` produce different rounding under default precision; the implementation now uses the left-associated form so the result is a clean integer when inputs allow. **Not shipped (deferred follow-ups)**: ramping the coingecko watchlist with the 6 protocol tokens (AAVE/COMP/UNI/CRV/LDO/SKY) so all 8 mapped protocols flow data — explicit "data flow first, code change is zero" deferral, the experiment is already wired; the `/research` skill referencing this command in `prompts/research-methodology.md` (small docs update worth bundling with the next protocol-revenue surface change rather than now); persisted divergence-history table for backtest (would need a daily job that snapshots `meta.signals` from the latest run — Phase 6 territory, not this experiment). 37 new tests (28 pure-algorithm in `tests/experiments/test_protocol_revenue.py` + 9 CLI integration in `tests/cli/test_revenue_divergence.py` with mocked `load_fee_series` / `load_price_series`). Full suite 528 → 565, ruff clean.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/experiments/protocol_revenue.py` (dataclasses + `build_snapshots` + `diagnose_divergence` + `_classify` + `load_fee_series` + `load_price_series`). `src/genkei/cli/revenue_divergence.py` (CLI command, registered as `revenue-divergence` in `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py`). `src/genkei/common/watchlist.py` (`ProtocolEntry.coingecko_id` field). `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml` (8 protocol entries gain `coingecko_id:` mappings). `tests/experiments/test_protocol_revenue.py` (28 unit tests) + `tests/cli/test_revenue_divergence.py` (9 integration tests). Live smoke output captured in PR description against homelab Postgres `genkei_capital`.

### ~~B-087 — Add Sui-native protocols to the DeFiLlama watchlist~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-20 (branch sui-native-protocols, commit `5344965`)
- **Outcome:** First of the four B-087/088/089/090 implications filed by the 2026-05-20 SUI /research session. Seven Sui-native slugs landed in `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml`'s `protocols:` section, all under a new "Sui ecosystem" comment block: **navi-lending** (Lending, NAVX → `navi`), **suilend** (Lending, SEND → `suilend`), **cetus-clmm** (DEX, CETUS → `cetus-protocol`), **scallop-lend** (Lending, SCA → `scallop-2`), **bluefin-spot** (DEX, BLUE → `bluefin`), **bluefin-pro** (Derivatives, BLUE → `bluefin`), and **deepbook-v3** (DEX, DEEP → `deepbook`). These seven entries use six unique `coingecko_id` values because Bluefin Spot and Bluefin Pro share `bluefin`. **Slug selection methodology:** the 6 candidate slugs in B-087's backlog text (cetus, navi-protocol, aftermath-finance, etc.) were the *aspirational* names — actual DefiLlama slugs were verified live against `defillama.protocols` and turned out to use `-clmm` / `-lend` / `-spot` / `-pro` suffixes because DefiLlama splits multi-product protocols into per-product entries. Aftermath was deliberately excluded from this branch (its largest product `aftermath-amm` is only $3M TVL and the protocol has no governance token yet, so it'd add a row that can't contribute to `revenue-divergence`). DeepBook V3 added on top of the original 6 since it's Sui Foundation's canonical native orderbook and represents core Sui infrastructure even at modest TVL. **CoinGecko mapping methodology:** DefiLlama's `/protocols` payload had `gecko_id` NULL across all 7 candidates (bash-checked via raw_blobs JSON extraction). coingecko_id values were resolved via the CoinGecko `/search?query=<symbol>` endpoint, cross-checked by symbol + name match. The `scallop-2` id quirk (vs `scallop`) is the canonical Sui-protocol entry — `scallop` was taken by an unrelated coin. Suilend launched a SEND token at some point post-Solana-Solend (the same team's Sui pivot) — `coingecko.com/coins/suilend` confirms. **Live smoke against homelab Postgres (ingest_run_id 85 collect + 86 normalize):** all 7 slugs returned HTTP 200 on both `/protocol/{slug}` (TVL) and `/summary/fees/{slug}` (fees + revenue dataType variants) endpoints. Row counts that landed in `defillama.protocol_tvl`: cetus-clmm 2,910 (history back to 2022-11-16, though latest row is 2026-02-04 — Cetus appears to have stopped reporting TVL there), scallop-lend 2,070, navi-lending 2,044, suilend 1,598, bluefin-pro 603, bluefin-spot 541, deepbook-v3 524. In `defillama.protocol_fees`: scallop-lend 871/871/871 (rows/fee/rev), cetus-clmm 870/870/870, navi-lending 810/810/810, suilend 801/801/801, bluefin-spot 537/537/537, bluefin-pro 307/307/307, deepbook-v3 3/3/3 (very recent listing). **All 7 protocols now visible in `genkei revenue-divergence`** — though they currently show "insufficient-data" because their tokens (NAVX/SEND/CETUS/SCA/BLUE/DEEP) aren't in the `crypto:` watchlist, so `coingecko.market_data` has no price/market_cap rows to compute against. **This is the same pre-existing gap as for aave-v3 / compound-v3 / uniswap-v3 / curve-dex / lido / sky-lending** — those have coingecko_id mappings too but their tokens aren't in `crypto:` either. The literal acceptance criterion ("populate the coingecko_id... so revenue-divergence lights up on the next daily run") is satisfied as far as the protocols section is concerned; the "lights up on next daily run" part requires the orthogonal step of wiring the coingecko ingester to also fetch every unique `coingecko_id` from the `protocols:` section (or adding the tokens to `crypto:` manually). That follow-up is worth a small backlog item but was deliberately not scoped into B-087 — adding 6 tokens to `crypto:` is an investment-intent decision (do we want to *track* these tokens, separate from wanting their *protocol data*) the user should make explicitly. **Decision-log integration:** `docs/research/decisions/2026-05-20-sui-position-assessment.md` "Backlog implications" section #1 struck through and cross-referenced to this item; #2/#3/#5 cross-referenced to B-089/B-088/B-090; #4 references B-085 priority bump.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml` (44-line addition under `protocols:`, new "Sui ecosystem" comment block). `docs/research/decisions/2026-05-20-sui-position-assessment.md` (5-line cross-reference update in the Backlog implications section). Live homelab smoke: ingest_run 85 (defillama collect) and 86 (normalize), both `success`. Real row counts above verified via `genkei query` against `defillama.protocol_tvl` and `defillama.protocol_fees`. CLI verification: `genkei tvl --protocol navi-lending` and `genkei tvl --protocol cetus-clmm` returned real TVL data; `genkei revenue-divergence` lists all 7 new slugs sorted alongside the existing 8. Tests stay 570/570 (no Python files touched — diff is YAML + Markdown only), ruff clean.

### ~~B-091 — Wire protocol-token coingecko_ids into the CoinGecko ingest path~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-20 (branch protocol-token, commits `2e93329` + `ba4585b` + `d3db588` + `7b0eb03`)
- **Outcome:** Closes the data-flow gap B-087 surfaced. With B-087 landed, 14 watchlist protocols carried a `coingecko_id` but only 2 (chainlink-staking + chainlink-requests) produced real signal in `genkei revenue-divergence` — the other 12 returned `insufficient-data` because their tokens weren't in the `crypto:` watchlist, so the CoinGecko ingester never fetched them. This branch picked recommended option **(a)** from B-091's acceptance criteria: extend `genkei.ingest.coingecko.load_coins()` to read the union of `coingecko_id`s from `crypto:` and `protocols:`, deduped. Pure data-flow change, no investment intent implied. **Implementation** (`src/genkei/ingest/coingecko.py`): `load_coins()` now iterates `watchlist.crypto` first (preserving legacy ordering + symbol/name pairs), then `watchlist.protocols` for any `coingecko_id` not already seen. Protocol-derived `CoinTarget`s carry an empty `symbol` (the watchlist's `protocols:` section is keyed on the DefiLlama slug, not the token symbol) — the authoritative symbol + name still land in `coingecko.coins` from the CoinGecko API response itself; `CoinTarget.symbol` is only used in log lines. **+4 new unit tests** in `tests/ingest/test_coingecko.py` covering: union with both sections populated, dedup across crypto/protocols when chainlink appears in both, protocol-without-coingecko_id is skipped, protocols-only watchlist still yields a usable list. Test count 570 → 574, ruff clean. **Scope creep, in B-091's favor — the bigger bug B-091 surfaced**: live smoke caught that the daily CoinGecko (and SEC and FRED) crons had been reading the LEGACY `config/watchlists.yml` at repo root, not the canonical `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml` the simplify refactor moved everything to. Each ingest module defined its own `DEFAULT_WATCHLIST_PATH = Path("config/watchlists.yml")` constant, CWD-relative, pointing at the stale file. That meant every `coingecko_id` mapping added in B-062 (the original protocol entries) and B-087 (Sui-native) was invisible to the production cron — the daily run had been ingesting the same 7 crypto-core coins for weeks while the canonical file silently grew protocol mappings. Fixed in commit `ba4585b`: all three ingesters now import `DEFAULT_WATCHLIST_PATH` from `genkei.common.watchlist` (which uses `__file__`-relative resolution that works both in dev and installed packages), and the legacy `config/watchlists.yml` was deleted entirely (it was a strict subset of the canonical file — no removals, no migrations needed). **Second surfaced bug, also fixed**: the B-087 watchlist had `coingecko_id: deepbook` for the DeepBook V3 entry, but `deepbook` on CoinGecko is an unrelated coin (symbol ADB, name "DeepBook" — different project entirely). The Sui DEEP token's correct id is `deep` (verified via CoinGecko `/search?query=DEEP` — the first hit is `deep | DEEP | DeepBook`). Fixed in commit `d3db588` with an inline NOTE in the watchlist entry's rationale field so the next reviewer doesn't repeat the mistake. **Live homelab smoke (ingest_run_id 87 + 88 + 89 + 90 + 91 + 92)**: collect run with the union loader now fetches 19 coins (7 crypto + 12 protocol-derived after dedup of `chainlink` and `bluefin` which appear in both spots). `coingecko.coins` grew 7 → 19. New `coingecko.market_data` rows landed 366 daily entries each (2025-05-21 → 2026-05-20) for all 12 new tokens — except `deep` which initially landed via the wrong `deepbook` id (1 epoch-zero row, garbage); targeted re-fetch + targeted normalize (`normalize(source_run_id=90)`) landed the corrected 366 rows for `deep` cleanly. **`genkei revenue-divergence` end-to-end verification**: 14 mapped protocols × 12 producing real divergence classifications + 2 correctly tagged `insufficient-data` (compound-v3 and deepbook-v3 — both because their `defillama.protocol_fees` history doesn't yet span the 90-day lookback window; expected behavior, not a bug). Real signal surfaced — most notably for the Sui ecosystem, which the morning's SUI /research session couldn't drill into: navi-lending shows strong `price-leads-up` (price +24% / revenue -81.5%), cetus-clmm shows `price-leads-up` (+69% / -40.5%), suilend shows `aligned` but at -33% / -85% (fundamentals collapsing alongside price). **This data would have materially strengthened the SUI bear case in the morning's research session** — the per-protocol drill-down B-087 enabled now shows Sui DeFi protocols falling harder than the chain-level TVL suggested. Worth a follow-up `/research sui` session in 2-4 weeks once enough trailing-window data accumulates to see whether the protocol-level metrics confirm or refute the morning's "trim to underweight" call. **Decision-log integration**: SUI decision file cross-reference (already pointing at B-091 for implication #5's parent gap "revenue-divergence lights up") is now load-bearing — the data it referenced as future-state is now live.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/ingest/coingecko.py` (extended `load_coins()` to union protocols section, +24 lines). `tests/ingest/test_coingecko.py` (+4 new tests, +99 lines). `src/genkei/ingest/{coingecko,sec,fred}.py` (DEFAULT_WATCHLIST_PATH now imported from `genkei.common.watchlist`, -3 stale constants). `config/watchlists.yml` deleted (-349 lines, was unreferenced after the imports were corrected). `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml` (deepbook → deep correction + NOTE in rationale, +2/-2 lines). Live homelab: ingest_runs 87 (collect 19 coins) + 88 (collect, run id passed in via background command) + 89 (normalize) + 90 (targeted deep collect) + 91 (normalize-all, no-op for deep) + 92 (targeted normalize of run 90, lands deep cleanly). Tests 570 → 574, ruff clean, no warnings.

### ~~B-090 — Crypto peer relative-strength view + CLI surface~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-21 (branch relative-strength, commits `0c8a058` + `188f707` + `c14011e` + `26e0087` + `fde1e30`)
- **Outcome:** Closes the last of the four B-087/088/089/090 implications filed by the 2026-05-20 SUI /research session. The SUI-vs-SOL math that was the *single most decisive signal* in the morning's bear thesis (a manual `genkei query` joining `coingecko.market_data` against itself at four anchor dates) is now a one-line CLI call — `genkei relative-strength --ticker SUI --peer SOL --window 365` reproduces the exact -22.8pp number against live data. **Postgres view** (migration `b1c2d3e4f5a6`, applied to homelab): `analytics.crypto_relative_strength` emits one row per (asset, peer, window_days) for every coingecko_id pair across 5 default windows (7d / 30d / 90d / 180d / 365d). Each side's `lookback_price` resolves via correlated subquery to the most recent observation at-or-before `latest_ts - window_days`; each coin anchors on its own latest_ts so late-arriving rows on one coin don't drift the others. NULL semantics for insufficient history (a newly-listed coin at the 365d window) so the CLI reads "n/a" loud rather than zero. Non-materialized — the math is cheap (one indexed subquery per coin × window, then a self-cross-join on the per-coin returns), and live views avoid a refresh-policy job. `analytics` schema landed in this same migration per storage.md's convention for cross-source derived views. **Experiments module** (`src/genkei/experiments/relative_strength.py`): mirrors B-062's `protocol_revenue.py` separation between pure functions and lake helpers. `compute_return_pct(price_series, *, window_days)` and `compute_relative_strength(asset_series, peer_series, *, asset, peer, windows)` are dual implementations of the SQL view's math in Python — kept synced so any future refactor can verify view-vs-Python parity on synthetic series. `load_relative_strength(*, asset, peer, window_days, limit)` queries the view with optional filters, ordered by `relative_strength_pct DESC NULLS LAST` so the most-outperforming pairs surface first. **CLI** (`src/genkei/cli/relative_strength.py` → `genkei relative-strength`): default mode iterates every watchlist crypto vs BTC at the 30d window, sorted by relative_strength_pct DESC; `--ticker X --peer Y` shows all 5 default windows for that pair (the "SUI vs SOL across windows" shape); `--window N` narrows to one of the 5 supported windows and rejects others *loudly* rather than silently empty (the view only carries the 5 defaults); `--json` and `--limit` are the standard flags. Human-friendly tickers (BTC / SUI / ETH …) resolve via the `crypto:` watchlist; protocol-side coins (NAVX / SEND / CETUS / …) show their `coingecko_id` directly since they aren't in `crypto:`. **`genkei watchlist health` integration**: `PRIMARY_TABLES` gains an `analytics` source key pointing at the new view. The view appears under "Primary table liveness" with the standard OK/EMPTY semantics. Intentionally NOT added to `RECURRING_ENDPOINTS` — the view has no `meta.ingest_runs` row (computed live from `coingecko.market_data`), so the recurring-cron MISSING check doesn't apply to it. `ExpectationsRegistryTests.test_all_known_sources_have_primary_tables` pinned the new source key in CI. **22 new tests**: 14 pure-algorithm in `tests/experiments/test_relative_strength.py` (basic return math, negative return, empty series, insufficient history yields None not zero, lookback picks most-recent-at-or-before-target, zero lookback price safety, default-windows-emit-five-rows, one-sided history yields None relative-strength, and a synthetic SUI-vs-SOL 365d reproduction of the morning session's -22.8pp call); 8 CLI integration in `tests/cli/test_relative_strength.py` covering default mode, --ticker + --peer all-windows mode, explicit --window narrowing, --ticker-only defaults peer to BTC, unknown ticker / unknown peer / out-of-set window rejected loudly, JSON payload shape stable. Test count 574 → 596, ruff clean. **Live smoke against homelab (1,900-row view)**: every SUI-vs-SOL window matches the morning's manual computation exactly — 7d -3.7pp, 30d +15.6pp, 90d +14.0pp, 180d +12.0pp, 365d -22.8pp. **Sui-ecosystem signal sharpened**: the morning's bear case had +12pp on the 180d window (sector-correlation, "damage front-loaded" call). With B-091's protocol-token coingecko data now flowing, the relative-strength view will pick up SUI vs NAVX / CETUS / SUILEND / etc. cross-pairs over time — opens the "is SUI underperforming or outperforming its own ecosystem" angle for future sessions, which B-062's `revenue-divergence` doesn't directly address. **Decision-log integration**: `docs/research/decisions/2026-05-20-sui-position-assessment.md` implication #5 struck through and cross-referenced to this item. All four of the morning's filed implications (B-087 + B-088 + B-089 + B-090) are now either resolved or filed, plus the B-085 priority bump from #4 was applied earlier today.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260521_create_analytics_crypto_relative_strength.py` (129 lines, applied to homelab — alembic head 7e4d2a1f8b35 → b1c2d3e4f5a6). `src/genkei/experiments/relative_strength.py` (239 lines, dataclasses + pure functions + load helper). `src/genkei/cli/relative_strength.py` (233 lines, CLI command). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` (+7 lines, registers the command). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (+5 lines, adds analytics to PRIMARY_TABLES). `tests/experiments/test_relative_strength.py` (183 lines, 14 tests) + `tests/cli/test_relative_strength.py` (285 lines, 8 tests) + `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` (+1 expected source key). `docs/research/decisions/2026-05-20-sui-position-assessment.md` cross-reference. Live homelab `analytics.crypto_relative_strength` returns 1,900 rows; `genkei relative-strength --ticker SUI --peer SOL` reproduces all 5 of the morning session's relative-strength numbers exactly; `genkei watchlist health` surfaces the new source as OK.

### ~~B-085 — Investigate stablecoin historical-anchor sparsity in defillama.stablecoins~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-21 (branch stablecoin-sparity, commits `1ac87a6` + `daaf650`)
- **Outcome:** Closes the gap that blocked two consecutive research sessions (LINK 2026-05-17, SUI 2026-05-20) — both could only see today's stablecoin snapshot when trying to measure on-chain dry-powder over time. **Diagnosis:** DefiLlama splits stablecoin data across two endpoints with the same destination table (`defillama.stablecoins`) — the daily `/stablecoins` endpoint returns *current state only* (377 stablecoins with per-chain supply at `now()`); the per-asset `/stablecoin/{id}` endpoint returns multi-year historical timeseries per chain. The daily collector was running fine — writing one snapshot per run — but the historical-depth path (`--backfill --since YYYY-MM-DD --endpoint stablecoins`) had never been invoked in production. So the table had only the days since the collector started running (~11 days at LINK-session time, ~14 days at SUI-session time; queries against `ts::date IN (latest-30, latest-90, …)` returned zero matches because those dates literally had no rows). The collector/normalizer code path was already implemented (`_backfill_stablecoins()` in `ingest/defillama.py` calling `/stablecoin/{id}` for every known asset_id, and `normalize_stablecoin_history()` in `normalize/defillama.py` parsing the resulting `chainCirculating.<chain>.tokens` historical array into per-day rows) — it just needed to be invoked. **Operational fix (one-shot backfill against homelab):** ingest_run_id=96 walked all 301 known stablecoin asset_ids at 5 req/s (`https://stablecoins.llama.fi/stablecoin/{id}`), landing one raw blob per asset (~5 minutes wall clock). ingest_run_id=97 normalized those blobs through the existing path, populating `defillama.stablecoins` with per-(asset_id, chain, date) historical rows back to 2017-11-28. **Code change (preventive):** extended `src/genkei/ingest/defillama.py`'s module docstring with a "Daily-collector vs one-shot-backfill (B-085 runbook)" section that codifies the dual-mode for the three endpoints (prices, protocols, stablecoins) where DefiLlama splits current-state from historical-depth. The runbook explains: (a) daily collector keeps current state fresh, (b) one-shot backfill via `--backfill --since YYYY-MM-DD --endpoint <name>` fills historical depth, (c) if a new asset first appears in the daily collector after the historical backfill ran, re-run the backfill for that endpoint with `--since` to land its history too. **Live verification** of the previously-failing query pattern:
  - **Row count:** 19,816 → **929,720** (47× increase)
  - **Distinct ts:** 16 → **3,112** (195× increase)
  - **History depth:** 11 days → **8.5 years** (earliest row 2017-11-28; latest 2026-05-21)
  - **The original failing query** (`SELECT … FROM defillama.stablecoins WHERE ts::date IN (current_date, current_date-30, -90, -180, -365, -730)`) now returns 6 anchors with real data: today $320.8B total stablecoins across 200 chains; 2 years ago $161.2B across 92 chains (visible market growth).

  **New signals that materially extend prior research sessions** (worth surfacing for `/reflect-decisions` whenever those sessions resolve):

  - **SUI chain stables: 1y ago $1.155B → today $597M = -48% YoY.** The SUI session noted only the today snapshot. The 48% decline confirms the bear thesis far more strongly than the session realized — capital is *leaving* Sui DeFi, not idle dry powder.
  - **Ethereum chain stables: 1y ago $124B → today $164B = +32% YoY** *despite* ETH chain TVL falling -28% YoY in the same window. The LINK session noted the 4:1 stable/TVL ratio but couldn't see the divergence over time. Capital is *arriving* on Ethereum while *not* deploying into DeFi — a more nuanced bear signal than the LINK session expressed.
  - **Total stablecoin float on tracked chains: 2024-05 $161B → 2025-05 $244B → 2026-05 $320B** — visible sectorwide growth that's now queryable for any future macro/regime classifier.

  **Why this didn't have a code change beyond the docstring:** the backfill infrastructure was already wired (B-019). The actual delta was operational + documentation: run the backfill once + add a runbook so the next person knows the dual-mode exists. Tests stay 599/599 (no new code paths, no behavior change), ruff clean.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/ingest/defillama.py` (+18-line "Daily-collector vs one-shot-backfill (B-085 runbook)" docstring section). `docs/research/decisions/2026-05-17-link-position-assessment.md` + `docs/research/decisions/2026-05-20-sui-position-assessment.md` (strike-through implication #4 in both, cross-referenced to B-085). Live homelab: ingest_run_id 96 (backfill collect — 301 asset histories) + 97 (backfill normalize — landed historical rows). `defillama.stablecoins`: 19,816 → 929,720 rows, 16 → 3,112 ts, 11d → 8.5y history depth. Verification queries above all return real data. Tests 599/599, ruff clean.

### ~~B-074 — Architecture diagram + expanded README~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-21 (branch arch-diagram, commits `5ee975a` + `d10e6b8`)
- **Outcome:** Onboarding-debt cleanup after 21 days and ~40 resolved items have shipped without docs catching up. **README** was the worse-stale of the two — it dated to the DeFiLlama-MVP era and referenced `scripts/collect_defillama.py` / `scripts/normalize_defillama.py` / `scripts/build_daily_report.py` (none of which exist anymore; R-019 moved everything to `src/genkei/` 11 days ago), plus knew nothing about the `genkei` CLI (10 typed subcommands + 1 stub), the experiments framework, the `/research` workflow, the decision log, the analytics derived view, the mission queue, or roughly the entire last 40 of the 47 resolved entries. Rewritten end-to-end with: (a) **"The loop" ASCII diagram** at the top showing External APIs → Postgres (raw_blobs + per-source tables + analytics views) → genkei CLI → Agent (/research, /reflect-decisions); (b) quickstart with commands that actually work (install + .env + alembic upgrade + 4 smoke commands); (c) "What's in the lake" table by source with coverage + row counts; (d) CLI surface table — one row per `genkei <cmd>` with "what it answers"; (e) research workflow explainer + decision-log contract pointer; (f) "How it runs" — three modes (scheduled cron, synchronous pairing, async mission queue); (g) repository-layout block matching the current `src/genkei/` shape; (h) conventions section + deeper-docs pointer table linking out to `architecture.md` / `storage.md` / `infrastructure.md` / `repo-layout.md` / `missions.md` / `research/README.md` / `prompts/research-methodology.md` / `prompts/reflect-on-decisions.md` / `backlog.md` / `resolved.md`. **architecture.md** was structurally fine but stuck at 2026-05-10. Eleven days of work (B-062, B-081, B-082, B-083, B-087, B-090, B-091, B-085 + the simplify + relative-strength branches) needed to land in the snapshot. Refreshed: the "Last updated" line + phase progress narrative; the high-level data-flow diagram (added CLI / experiments / agent layers that didn't exist when first drawn; enumerated current per-source schemas including the new analytics, onchain, protocol_fees, protocol_tvl, form4_* tables; added the analytics.crypto_relative_strength view); the `src/genkei/` tree (every module listed — was incomplete, missed sec_form4, coingecko, onchain_staking, revenue_divergence, relative_strength, _helpers, the experiments modules); the cli/ branch lists all 10 real subcommands instead of "1 shipped + 6 stubs"; the roadmap table (Phase 2 3/10 → 4/10 + 4 carve-outs; Phase 3 3/11 → 10/11; Phase 4 "not started" → "foundation laid"; Phase 5 "not started" → 3/11; Phase 6 "not started" → "started"); test count 125 → 599; backlog 58 → 46; resolved 24 → 47; deeper-docs table gained research/README + methodology + reflect prompts. **Append-only decision + gotcha log** preserved the convention (newest at the bottom): D-021 (cross-source derived signals: pure-function detector + Postgres view + CLI surface, always paired — captures the B-062 / B-090 pattern); D-022 (daily collector vs one-shot backfill is the canonical dual-mode for "current state + history" endpoints — codifies B-085); D-023 (`coingecko_id` on `ProtocolEntry` picked up by `load_coins()` union, decoupled from investment intent in `crypto:` — captures B-091); G-032 (ingest modules were reading legacy `config/watchlists.yml` for weeks after the simplify refactor); G-033 (DefiLlama's `/protocols` `.gecko_id` is NULL — use CoinGecko `/search` instead); G-034 (DefiLlama splits multi-product protocols into per-product slugs; verify live); G-035 (`deepbook` ≠ `deep`); G-036 (`Decimal (a*b)/c ≠ a*(b/c)` under default precision); G-037 (defillama `--backfill --since` is required by argparse but endpoint-specific). Acceptance criteria all met: (1) high-level diagram in `docs/architecture.md` ✓; (2) README explains data lake + CLI + agent end-to-end ✓; (3) per-component pointers to deeper docs ✓. No code changes — pure documentation work, tests stay 599/599, ruff clean.
- **Evidence:** `README.md` rewritten end-to-end (125 lines → 144 lines, +132/-91; replaced every legacy `scripts/` reference, added "the loop" ASCII diagram, source table, CLI table, workflow + conventions). `docs/architecture.md` (+127/-34 surgical refresh: Last updated line, high-level diagram, src/genkei/ tree, roadmap table, deeper-docs pointer table, test/backlog/resolved counts; append-only D-021/D-022/D-023 + G-032/G-033/G-034/G-035/G-036/G-037). Tests 599/599 still pass, ruff clean — pure docs work.

### ~~B-071 — Workflow-failure + ingest-staleness alerting~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-22 (branch workflow-failure, commits `1a957f2` + `bea52ce`)
- **Outcome:** Closes the "silent failures defeat the constantly-gathering goal" gap. Both halves of B-071's acceptance shipped as separate workflow files so they can be triggered, diagnosed, and disabled independently — neither carries dead code if one of them ever needs to be swapped for a different mechanism (Discord webhook, external paging service, etc.). **(1) `workflow-failure-alert.yml`** — a centralized `workflow_run` listener that watches Tests + DefiLlama Daily Brief + CoinGecko Daily + FRED Daily + SEC Daily. On `conclusion == 'failure'`, opens a `[workflow-failure] <name>` GitHub issue or, if one's already open, adds a comment with the new run details (branch, short SHA, triggering event, run URL, detection timestamp). Centralized vs per-workflow `if: failure()` step: one file to maintain, captures every workflow without per-workflow surgery, the issue-management logic only lives in one place. **Cancellations intentionally ignored** — most cancellations are deliberate (push during running job, manual cancel, or the `cancel-in-progress` concurrency setting on Claude Code Review which cancels every prior run on every push). Alerting on those would spam the issue tracker with non-actionable noise; the same pattern that wasted 4 of 5 arch-diagram code-review runs would have generated 4 useless alerts. Exact-title match on `gh issue list --json title,number --jq 'select(.title==$x)'` rather than GitHub's fuzzy `--search "in:title"`, so two workflows whose names share words (e.g., "Daily Brief" + "Daily") don't collapse onto the same issue. **(2) `ingest-staleness-check.yml`** — daily 14:00 UTC scheduled workflow on the self-hosted Beelink runner (needs lake access for `genkei watchlist health`). Runs `genkei watchlist health --json`, pipes through an inline Python parser that extracts non-OK rows (any tagged STALE / FAIL / MISSING / EMPTY — same vocabulary as the existing `_health_status_tag` function in `cli/watchlist.py`), opens one per-(source, endpoint) issue `[ingest-alert] <source> <endpoint> — <TAG>` per affected row. Repeated unhealthy days add comments to the existing issue rather than reopening; close-when-recovered is a manual step (auto-close after N consecutive OK runs is a possible follow-up but adds state-tracking complexity not worth the cost today). **Why the 14:00 UTC schedule** — fires *after* the morning ingest crons (DefiLlama 10:30 UTC, others in the 10-12 UTC window) so a freshly-stale source surfaces same-day rather than the next morning. **Python over jq for parsing** — the JSON shape has nested optional fields (some rows have `endpoint`, some have `table`; `age_hours` is sometimes null) that get fragile fast in jq; inline Python is 8 lines and explicit about every optional. Pairs with `workflow-failure-alert.yml`: that catches CI runs that *failed*; this catches sources that *silently stopped working* (a workflow that no longer triggers, a partial-success that didn't fail but didn't write rows, a primary table going EMPTY because its normalizer broke). Together they cover both kinds of silent breakage B-071 called out — the failure-alert is the canary for known failure modes, the staleness-check is the canary for unknown failure modes. **What this would have caught earlier:** today's "why does code review keep breaking" surprise was visible only because the user noticed the PR status manually — once this workflow ships to main, every Claude Code Review timeout would have auto-opened an issue (or, since cancellations are ignored, the *first* genuine timeout would). Same for FRED's recurring failures that surfaced via the LINK + SUI sessions' "FRED collector failed in the last 24h" notes — the staleness check would have caught those at 36h instead of waiting for a research session to surface them. **Not shipped (deferred follow-ups):** Discord/Slack notification surface (issues-only today; can add a `--notify-webhook` flag later if issues prove insufficient); auto-close on N consecutive OK days (adds state tracking; for now, the user closes manually); a `genkei` CLI subcommand wrapping the staleness check for local use (the workflow's Python snippet is the canonical implementation; a CLI version is a small extraction if needed).
- **Evidence:** `.github/workflows/workflow-failure-alert.yml` (89 lines, workflow_run listener for Tests + 4 daily ingest workflows; exact-title dedup; cancellation/success skipped). `.github/workflows/ingest-staleness-check.yml` (125 lines, daily 14:00 UTC scheduled on `self-hosted, beelink`; runs `genkei watchlist health --json`, parses non-OK rows inline, opens or comments on per-(source, endpoint) issues). Both YAML files validate via PyYAML. Tests 599/599 still pass, ruff clean — pure CI work, no Python code touched. Pairs with the arch-diagram branch's `claude-code-review.yml` timeout bump (15→30 min) shipped earlier today — that fix prevents the false-positive cancellations that would otherwise feed into this alerter's noise.

### ~~B-072 — Schema-drift detection~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-22 (branch schema-drift, commits `cf7c3a1` + `9961ba6`)
- **Outcome:** Closes the silent-failure trilogy started by B-071 (failure-alert + staleness-check). Those two catch runs that *fail* and sources that *stop running*; neither catches the third silent-breakage mode — a normalizer that *succeeds* with zero or wrong rows because the upstream API renamed/removed the field it reads. B-072 is the canary for that case. **New module** `src/genkei/common/schema_drift.py` (421 lines): `EndpointSchema` dataclass declaring load-bearing keys per (source, endpoint_kind), `SCHEMA_SPECS` tuple with 15 specs covering DefiLlama (protocols, prices_current, stablecoins, chain_tvl_history_<chain>, protocol_<slug>, protocol_fees_<slug>, protocol_revenue_<slug>, stablecoin_<id>), CoinGecko (coin_<id>, market_chart_<id>), FRED (series_<id> with the "seriess" typo per G-018, observations_<id>), and SEC (submissions_<cik>, companyfacts_<cik>). The pure `check_payload(payload, spec)` function emits one `DriftIssue` per violation across four kinds: `WRONG_TOP_LEVEL_TYPE`, `MISSING_REQUIRED_KEY`, `EMPTY_ARRAY`, `MISSING_NESTED_PATH`. The lake-loader `check_recent_blobs(max_age_hours=72)` picks the most recent raw_blob per endpoint_kind, runs the pure checker, and surfaces `NO_RECENT_SAMPLES` for any spec whose endpoint hasn't been hit recently. **Specs are intentionally narrow** — only the keys whose absence would actually break the normalizer. Adding non-load-bearing keys to a spec invites false positives (DefiLlama adds an optional `logo_v2` — irrelevant to us, should not be drift). When a normalizer starts depending on a new key, the spec gets updated in the same commit per CLAUDE.md's "extract canonical helpers" principle. **`endpoint_pattern_excludes`** captures shared-prefix endpoint families that collide under SQL LIKE — `protocol\\_%` would otherwise match `protocol_fees_<slug>` and `protocol_revenue_<slug>` because `%` is greedy. The protocol_<slug> spec excludes both explicitly. Caught by the very first live smoke against homelab, which flagged `protocol_revenue_deepbook-v3` as missing `chainTvls` — the detector caught its own pattern bug. Fixed in the first commit alongside the module itself. **CLI integration** (`src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py`, +149 lines): two surfaces — `genkei watchlist drift` for the focused view (just drift findings), and `genkei watchlist health` now folds drift rows in with `health_status: "DRIFT"`. The B-071 staleness alerter (`.github/workflows/ingest-staleness-check.yml`) filters on `health_status != "OK"`, so DRIFT rows flow through automatically and open GitHub issues — **no workflow update needed for B-072 to be auto-alerted**. The two pieces compose without coupling. `--skip-drift` on `health` for when you want the fast path; `--drift-max-age-hours` for tuning. **Defensive shim:** `_drift_rows` wraps `check_recent_blobs` in try/except so a checker error surfaces as one `CHECKER_ERROR` row rather than masking the entire health output. **23 unit tests** covering both the pure checker (all four issue kinds, array-sample cutoff, nested-path resolution, intermediate-non-dict, type mismatches) and the spec registry itself (every spec has required fields, endpoint_kinds are unique, all four expected sources covered — pinning the source set means adding a new ingester forces a corresponding spec). Realistic-shape tests use payload structures from live raw_blobs (`/protocols` array of slug-name-tvl objects; `/market_chart` three parallel arrays; FRED's `seriess` typo). **Live homelab smoke** against 72h of raw_blobs found exactly one genuine drift: `stablecoin_99` returns a stub `{id: ...}` payload missing `chainCirculating` / `symbol` / `name` / `pegType`. Either DeFiLlama is returning an error stub for a deleted asset or the asset has zero chain data — either way our normalizer silently skips it, and now we know. **Trilogy complete:** B-071 catches `failure` / `stale` / `missing` / `empty`; B-072 catches `drift`. Together they cover (CI run crashed), (CI ran but didn't trigger), (CI ran but didn't write rows), AND (CI wrote rows but they're the wrong shape). The unknown-unknowns space shrinks meaningfully — future ingester drift surfaces same-day instead of being noticed when a research session goes empty-handed.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/common/schema_drift.py` (new module — `EndpointSchema`, `DriftIssue`, `SCHEMA_SPECS`, `check_payload`, `check_recent_blobs`, helpers). `tests/common/test_schema_drift.py` (23 tests: pure checker × 9, spec registry × 3, realistic payload shapes × 6, array behaviors × 5). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (drift integrated into `health_cmd` + new `drift_cmd` subcommand + `_drift_rows` helper + `_health_status_tag` gains DRIFT vocabulary). Test count 599 → 622, ruff clean. Live homelab: `genkei watchlist drift` returns one DRIFT row (`defillama/stablecoin_<id>/stablecoin_99 — MISSING_REQUIRED_KEY 'chainCirculating'`); `genkei watchlist health` folds the same row into its output under a new "Schema drift" section.

### ~~B-065 — Watchlist scoring rubric~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-22 (branch watchlist-scoring, commits `a6cca4d` + `6499fd4` + `9e7d61f`)
- **Outcome:** First synthesis ship that combines the three existing single-source signals (B-060 insider clusters + B-062 revenue-divergence + B-090 relative-strength) plus TVL trend, volume momentum, and macro regime into a single per-asset composite. Design is deliberately *interpretable, not optimal*: each component contributes a small signed integer (-3..+3), composites sum unweighted, and every score carries a per-component breakdown so a surprising number can always be drilled into. Asymmetric by asset class — **equity** scores from `insider_flow` (Form 4 buy/sell clusters), `revenue_trend` (XBRL YoY), `filings_velocity` (8-K cadence; ≥5 in 30d signals restatement / executive-change risk), and the shared `macro_regime`; **crypto** scores from `relative_strength` (the B-090 view vs BTC at 30d), `tvl_trend` (chain TVL for L1s the coingecko_id maps to), `volume_momentum` (7d vs 30d avg), and `macro_regime`. Macro regime is computed once per scoring run from FRED DGS10 / BAMLH0A0HYM2 / VIXCLS / DTWEXBGS and shared across all assets — risk-on / mixed / risk-off labels. **`RUBRIC_VERSION = "v1"` is part of the `meta.signals` PK** — the hard rule (documented in `docs/scoring.md` and pinned by a unit test) is *never modify v1 in place*; a future formula ships as `v2`, scores coexist, and backtesting a rubric change becomes a SQL JOIN. New `meta.signals` table: `(asset, ts, rubric_version)` PK, JSONB `components`, FK to `meta.ingest_runs`, three indexes (`ts DESC` for today-snapshot, `(rubric_version, ts DESC)` for version-scoped backtests, `(sleeve, ts DESC)` for sleeve-filtered research-session reads), plain table not hypertable (volume is ~35 watchlist assets × 1 score/day = ~13k rows/year). Module `src/genkei/experiments/watchlist_scoring.py` follows the established pure-function + lake-loader + orchestrator separation (B-062 / B-090 / B-072 pattern): pure scorers per component, composers per asset class, `_load_macro_inputs` / `_load_equity_signals` / `_load_crypto_signals` lake loaders, `compute_today` / `persist_scores` / `load_latest_scores` orchestrators. Custom `_bulk_upsert_signals` rather than the generic `bulk_upsert` helper because of G-028 (bare dicts can't auto-adapt to JSONB — `json.dumps` required). CLI surface `genkei watchlist score` with five modes — default (compute + print sorted by composite DESC), `--persist` (write to `meta.signals` under a fresh `meta.ingest_runs` row of source `watchlist_scoring`, idempotent on PK), `--ticker` / `--sleeve` filters, `--since DATE` (read persisted history), `--no-breakdown` (compact view), `--json` (every mode, agent-readable). **Live smoke produced sensible scores** across all 35 watchlist assets: TSM +4 (matches the real B-079 buy cluster), JPM -1 (matches the May 15 4-officer sell cluster from B-060), ETH -2 (rel-strength + TVL falling — matches the LINK session's bear thesis), MSTR 0 (filings_velocity -2 catches 5+ 8-Ks in 30d), SUI flat (the 22.8pp-vs-SOL 365d underperformance from the 2026-05-20 session sits inside the rubric's ±10pp 30d band — by design, since the rubric reads 30d not 365d). 36 unit tests pin every component (5 insider flow, 6 revenue trend, 3 filings velocity, 6 relative strength, 3 TVL trend, 5 volume momentum, 4 macro regime, 3 composite, 1 RUBRIC_VERSION). Acceptance criteria all met: (1) rubric documented in `docs/scoring.md` ✓ — design principles, per-component tables, score interpretation table (-5/+5 thresholds tied to sleeve discipline), schema, CLI usage, evolution rule; (2) daily scores written to Postgres ✓ — `meta.signals` plus persistence path; (3) CLI access `genkei watchlist score` ✓ — five modes. **Catches that wouldn't have surfaced without live smoke:** (a) `WITH window AS (...)` is a syntax error — `window` is a reserved PostgreSQL keyword; CTEs renamed to `recent_volume`/`top_7`/`top_30` and a note added (G-038 candidate); (b) `bulk_upsert` doesn't `json.dumps()` dict values for JSONB columns — needed a custom upserter per G-028. **What v2 likely adds** (documented in `docs/scoring.md` "Rubric evolution"): a `protocol_revenue_divergence` component for crypto tokens whose `coingecko_id` maps to a watchlist protocol (only LINK qualifies under v1 today); dollar-weighted `insider_flow` (a 4-reporter $200M cluster ≠ a 4-reporter $5K cluster); a stablecoin-supply flow component for crypto-core. Persistence is on-demand today (`genkei watchlist score --persist`); a `watchlist-scoring-daily.yml` self-hosted-runner workflow is a small follow-up if daily automatic persistence becomes valuable.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260522_create_meta_signals.py` (new — meta.signals table + 3 indexes, FK to meta.ingest_runs, head b1c2d3e4f5a6 → 8c5d7e9f1a02; applied to homelab). `src/genkei/experiments/watchlist_scoring.py` (new — 7 pure scoring functions, 2 composers, 3 lake loaders, 4 orchestrators, custom JSONB upserter, RUBRIC_VERSION constant). `tests/experiments/test_watchlist_scoring.py` (36 unit tests across 8 component test classes + RubricVersionTests). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (+357 lines — `score_cmd` plus `_format_score_human` / `_score_value` / `_score_fields` / `_components_iter` / `_score_to_json_dict` helpers; module docstring now lists 5 subcommands list/health/drift/score/gaps). `docs/scoring.md` (new — design principles, per-component tables, score interpretation by sleeve, schema, CLI usage, rubric evolution rules). Test count 622 → 663, ruff clean. Live homelab: ingest_run 105 wrote 35 scored rows; `genkei watchlist score --ticker AAPL`, `--sleeve crypto-core`, `--persist`, `--since 2026-05-22` all return expected output.

### ~~B-070 — Confirm and document Postgres backup posture~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-22 (branch confirm-doc, commits `f23ac31` + `c4cdfe1` + `08b314f`)
- **Outcome:** First fund-grade-hygiene ship after the data lake crossed a meaningful threshold today (B-065 started persisting `meta.signals` — the asset that can't be re-derived if lost). Investigation confirmed zero automated backups exist on the homelab: no genkei-specific cron, no systemd timer, no existing dumps anywhere; the only `~/homelab/scripts/backup.sh` lists `postgres_dispatcher_pg_data` / `pubpilot_pg_data` / `postgres_data` but not `genkeicapital_postgres_data`. Ships a complete posture instead of just documenting the gap: **`infra/backups/backup_postgres.sh`** does nightly `pg_dump --format=custom` of the live container via `docker exec`, validates the TOC via `pg_restore --list`, applies tiered retention (7 daily + 4 weekly + 12 monthly) via hard-links so the same dump file is referenced from multiple tiers without doubling disk, and refuses to run if free disk is <3× live DB size (preflight). **`infra/backups/restore_postgres.sh`** is deliberately the *drill* script not "restore over production" — spins up a fresh isolated `genkei-restore-drill` container on port 5499 with a temp volume, runs the TimescaleDB-aware sequence (`CREATE EXTENSION timescaledb` → `timescaledb_pre_restore()` → `pg_restore --single-transaction --exit-on-error` → `timescaledb_post_restore()`), parity-checks row counts in 8 high-cardinality tables against the live container, and tears down on exit. Production-restore (DR scenario) is intentionally manual in `docs/backups.md` so the destructive intent stays explicit. **End-to-end restore drill performed against live homelab**: pg_dump took 73s (1.5 GB → 511 MB custom-format, 3.0× compression); pg_restore took 86s into a fresh container; 8/8 sampled tables matched live (sec.filings 213,713 = 213,713; sec.form4_transactions 192,323 = 192,323; meta.raw_blobs 39,524 = 39,524; sec.form4_normalized_filings 36,707 = 36,707; defillama.stablecoins 930,962 = 930,962; coingecko.market_data 7,056 = 7,056; meta.ingest_runs 104 = 104; meta.signals 35 = 35 — the brand-new B-065 rows survived the round-trip). TimescaleDB sanity: 9 hypertables + 1,094 chunks restored intact. Restored DB size 1,448 MB ≈ within 5% of live's 1,531 MB (expected — no index bloat). **`docs/backups.md`** ships as the policy doc: "what's at risk" table per asset class (highlights `meta.signals` as the irreplaceable one), strategy table, retention math, the off-site decision matrix (Cloudflare R2 / Backblaze B2 / rsync-to-Pi / S3 Glacier / USB drive — recommends R2 because `cloudflared` infra is already adjacent on the Beelink and the $0 egress means a full recovery isn't priced by panic; cost at current volume ~$0.05/month, ~$2.50/month at 50× growth), the manual install procedure on the Beelink (per `~/.claude/skills/server-info/` governance, I don't autonomously modify the homelab), and both restore runbooks (DR scenario A: production-restore, drill scenario B: isolated-container verification). Drill evidence table embedded with exact numbers from the 2026-05-22 run so future-me can compare. **Caveats surfaced during the drill** (also captured in the doc): (1) pg_dump emits warnings about circular FK constraints on TimescaleDB catalog tables `hypertable` / `chunk` / `continuous_agg` — expected, the `pre_restore()`/`post_restore()` sequence handles it without `--disable-triggers`; (2) the live container is actually pinned to `timescale/timescaledb:latest-pg16` not the documented `2.26.4-pg16` (compose file uses `:latest` — minor drift, surfaced as an open follow-up); (3) the postgres superuser is `genkei_capital` not `postgres` (scripts hardcode this — change `DB_USER` if the role ever moves). **What B-070 deliberately does NOT ship** (each captured as an open item in `docs/backups.md`): the off-site upload step (waits for the user's R2 vs B2 vs rsync decision); cron failure → GitHub-issue alerting via B-071's existing pipeline (would need webhook surface); pinning the container image away from `:latest-pg16`. The local-dump layer is installable today; off-site follows independently. **Cross-linked from `docs/infrastructure.md`** so anyone reading the natural entry point finds the backup posture without already knowing it exists. Acceptance criteria all met: (1) backup cadence verified ✓ — confirmed zero exists; (2) restore drill performed and documented ✓ — full evidence table in docs/backups.md with timing + parity numbers; (3) off-site copy strategy recommended ✓ — Cloudflare R2 documented as the recommendation; final target selection and implementation remain pending.
- **Evidence:** `infra/backups/backup_postgres.sh` (115 lines: nightly dump via `docker exec`, TOC validation, hard-link retention across daily/weekly/monthly tiers, disk preflight). `infra/backups/restore_postgres.sh` (147 lines: isolated drill container on port 5499, TimescaleDB-aware sequence, parity check against live, auto-teardown). `docs/backups.md` (213 lines: what's at risk, strategy, retention math, off-site decision matrix, install runbook, restore runbooks A + B, drill evidence table, caveats, open items). `docs/infrastructure.md` (+4 lines cross-link). Tests 663 → 663 still pass, ruff clean. Live homelab drill: pg_dump 73s, pg_restore 86s, 8/8 tables matched live, 1,094 hypertable chunks intact, drill container torn down cleanly.

### ~~B-059 — Experiment: macro regime classifier~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-22 (branch macro-classifier, commits `4d916df` + `5682ea1` + `28c948f`)
- **Outcome:** Phase 5 experiment that buckets every business day from 2006-present into one of five macro regime labels — `risk_on` / `risk_off` / `easing` / `tightening_stress` / `mixed` — derived from four FRED daily series (DGS10, BAMLH0A0HYM2, VIXCLS, DTWEXBGS). Priority-ordered classification (mutually exclusive labels), absolute thresholds (not z-scores) aligned with B-065's `score_macro_regime` for interpretability, and graceful degradation to `mixed` when fewer than 3 of 4 inputs have coverage on a given date. **Originally planned as B-058 (TVL drawdown early-warning)**; pivoted here in the same session after the B-058 kickoff investigation surfaced a foundational blocker — `coingecko.market_data` has only 377 days of history (CoinGecko Demo/Public is hard-capped at 365d and Pro-tier backfill is paid), so OOS validation of any price-drawdown classifier is infeasible until B-035 lands. B-059 was the right unblocked alternative because FRED data is fully backfilled (DGS10 to 1962, VIX to 1990, USD to 2006) and the experiment directly upgrades the simplest component of the watchlist scoring rubric (B-065 v2's `macro_regime`). **Storage**: `analytics.macro_regime_per_date` live view — anchored on DGS10's daily calendar with LATERAL joins back to `fred.observations` to forward-fill each input (uses the existing `(series_id, ts DESC)` index; full 5,096-row view materializes in <1s). Restricted to 2006+ because pre-2006 we have at most 2 of 4 inputs and the classifier would always emit `mixed` anyway — pre-2006 history can be added in a follow-up via the USD substitute. **First iteration of the view timed out** at the full DGS10 calendar (1962-present, ~33k rows × 7 lateral subqueries against unindexed CTE-materialized series); rewrote using direct LATERAL joins against `fred.observations` so the (series_id, ts DESC) index gets used + capped the calendar at 2006 — query runs sub-second. **Python equivalent in `src/genkei/experiments/macro_regime.py`** runs the same thresholds and same priority order so a row-by-row Python-vs-SQL parity check can verify both surfaces agree (parity is exercised during live smoke; not in CI because that would require a live DB). **19 unit tests** in `tests/experiments/test_macro_regime.py` cover all five regimes (`RiskOff/TighteningStress/Easing/RiskOn/Mixed` test classes), the priority collisions (tightening_stress > risk_off when both apply; easing loses to risk_off when VIX is also elevated), the available-input degradation contract (1-2 inputs → `mixed` regardless), and a `RegimeLabelsContractTests` class that pins the label set itself + exhaustively checks the classifier never emits an unknown label over a grid of VIX × HY × DGS10-change inputs. The Lehman 2008 sanity test pins the real 2008-10-15 inputs (VIX 69.25, no HY pre-2023, DGS10 +0.52pp 30d) → `risk_off` via VIX alone. **CLI** (`genkei macro-regime`, +178 lines) ships five modes: default (latest day + one-line breakdown of the 4 inputs that drove the label), `--since/--until` for ranged history (table view), `--summary` collapses to a regime-distribution count table with shares, `--limit` for fixed row counts, `--json` for agent consumption (every mode). Default-no-args returns the single most recent row so `genkei macro-regime` is the fast lookup; ranged usage is explicit. **Live homelab sanity** matches every intuition: full 5,096-day distribution is 17.1% risk_off / 9.2% risk_on / 1.0% easing / 0.1% tightening_stress / 72.7% mixed — the ~17% risk_off rate roughly matches the cumulative footprint of 2008-09 H2, 2010 sovereign debt scare, 2011 US downgrade, 2015-16 China deval, 2018 Q4, 2020 COVID, 2022 hiking + Russia, 2024-08 yen carry unwind, 2025 tariff war. Targeted windows: 2008-09 to 2008-12 (Lehman) → 75 risk_off / 0 risk_on / 8 mixed (90% risk_off, exactly right); 2022-03 to 2022-10 (Fed hiking) → 107 risk_off / 3 easing / 59 mixed (63% risk_off, 1.8% easing — the brief easing windows match the mid-2022 "Fed pivot" head-fakes); 2024-08-05 (yen unwind) → risk_off with VIX 38.57. Today 2026-05-13 → `risk_on` (HY 2.82, VIX 17.87, USD weakening -0.95 over 30d). **Acceptance criteria met**: (1) regime labels per date ✓ — emitted by both SQL view and Python classifier; (2) materialized as a Postgres view for downstream queries ✓ — `analytics.macro_regime_per_date`. **Doc** `docs/experiments/macro-regime.md` (116 lines) establishes the convention for the rest of Phase 5 experiment writeups (B-056/057/058/061): what this experiment answers (descriptive labels for join-able context vs the forward-prediction question that's blocked on B-035/B-039), inputs + coverage table, priority-ordered regime definitions table, threshold rationale (why absolute not z-scored), historical distribution + windowed sanity checks (2008/2020/2022/2024/2021), consumers (B-065 v2, /research sessions, B-064, B-066), limitations (HY OAS pre-2023 gap, absolute thresholds underweight regime-relative shifts, no equity prices yet, no forward-return validation), and open follow-ups (BAMLH0A0HYM2 backfill to 1996, B-035 + B-039 unblock regime-conditional return study, B-064 + B-066 depend on this view existing). **Catches that wouldn't have surfaced without live smoke**: (a) initial view used inner CTEs that materialized the entire deduped series per fred.observations row → unindexed → full DGS10 calendar (33k rows) timed out; fixed by direct LATERAL joins + 2006+ calendar restriction. (b) Ruff auto-fix replaced `Optional[X]` with `X | None` everywhere; needed to add `from __future__ import annotations` for the venv's Python 3.9 to evaluate the modern union syntax at dataclass definition time (matches the existing experiments-module convention; relative_strength.py has the same header). **What B-059 deliberately does NOT ship** (each documented in `docs/experiments/macro-regime.md` "Open follow-ups"): backfill BAMLH0A0HYM2 to 1996 (closes pre-2023 HY gap); regime-conditional return validation (depends on B-035 long-history crypto prices + B-039 equity prices); B-066 CLI alias (the `genkei macro-regime` surface already covers B-066's `genkei macro --regime` acceptance — B-066 can merge or alias). The macro-regime view is now the canonical context-column for any future research-session SQL query that needs to condition on macro state without re-synthesizing it by hand.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260522_create_analytics_macro_regime.py` (new — view `analytics.macro_regime_per_date`, head 8c5d7e9f1a02 → c8e2f3a4d501; applied to homelab; restricted to 2006+ calendar after the LATERAL-join rewrite). `src/genkei/experiments/macro_regime.py` (new — `RegimeInputs`/`RegimeResult` dataclasses, pure `classify(inputs)` function, `load_regimes(since, until, limit)` lake loader, `summarize(results)`, `REGIME_LABELS` constant; `from __future__ import annotations`). `tests/experiments/test_macro_regime.py` (19 tests across 8 test classes: RiskOff × 3, TighteningStress × 2, Easing × 3, RiskOn × 3, Mixed × 1, AvailableInputDegradation × 3, Summarize × 2, RegimeLabelsContract × 2). `src/genkei/cli/macro_regime.py` (new — 5-mode CLI: latest / ranged history / summary distribution / limit / json). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` (+7 lines registering the new command). `docs/experiments/macro-regime.md` (new — 116 lines, establishes the experiments-writeup convention). `docs/backlog.md` (+2 lines marking B-058 as blocked on B-035 — the pivot context). Test count 663 → 682, ruff clean. Live homelab: `genkei macro-regime` returns today as risk_on; `genkei macro-regime --summary --since 2008-09-01 --until 2008-12-31` → 90% risk_off / 0% risk_on across Lehman quarter; `genkei macro-regime --summary --since 2022-03-01 --until 2022-10-31` → 63% risk_off / 1.8% easing across the hiking cycle.

### ~~B-035 — Binance public market-data ingester~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-23 (branch binance-market-data, commits `68d792f` + `8be290b` + `1286b1f` + `8a8bbcf` + `55b9c49` + `f345cf4` + `33e705d` + `2ee905a` + `bd41ff1` + `1265581`)
- **Outcome:** Phase 2 free-data ingester that finally breaks the 365-day ceiling on `coingecko.market_data` — landed 14,331 daily OHLCV rows across 7 watchlist coins with BTC-USD going back to 2015-07-19 (10.8y) and ETH-USD to 2016-05-17 (10y). **Pivoted from Binance to Coinbase** during the kickoff after live API tests confirmed Binance.com geo-blocks US IPs ("Service unavailable from a restricted location") — the homelab Beelink runner and any GitHub-hosted US runner hit the same wall. Binance.US was the next candidate but doesn't list PYTH (one of our secondary tactical alts) and has shorter history (Binance.US founded 2019). **Coinbase Exchange's public candles API** ([api.exchange.coinbase.com](https://api.exchange.coinbase.com)) was the right pivot: covers all 7 watchlist coins, no auth, US-accessible, longest free history for BTC/ETH. The B-035 acceptance criteria all hold under Coinbase: (1) no API key ✓; (2) backfill what's free ✓ — full per-product history from listing date on the free tier; (3) tables aligned with kline structure ✓ — `coinbase.candles` with (product, ts, open, high, low, close, volume_base). Backlog name stays as "Binance" for traceability; shipped implementation is Coinbase. **Storage**: `coinbase.candles` TimescaleDB hypertable, 30-day chunks, compression > 30d segmented by product (the dominant access pattern is per-product history). PK `(product, ts)`. `volume_base` is base-asset volume (BTC for BTC-USD); USD volume requires a close-price multiply at query time rather than baking an opinion into the table. No `market_cap` column — that's an aggregator concept, `coingecko.market_data.market_cap_usd` is the right source for it. **Ingester** (`src/genkei/ingest/coinbase.py`, 423 lines) ships two modes: default daily collector (7-day lookback per product, idempotent upserts overwrite the latest close-price revisions Coinbase publishes within 24h of each daily close, ~7 calls / <2s); `--backfill --since YYYY-MM-DD` walks 280-day chunks per product because Coinbase enforces a hard 300-candle cap per call (returns an error dict, not partial data, if exceeded). Empty windows (pre-listing dates) are silently accepted as zero rows so backfills don't fail when SUI-USD doesn't exist in 2020. Rate-limited at 5 req/sec (Coinbase ceiling is 10 — leaves room for daily+backfill overlap). **Error responses use HTTP 200** with `{"message": "..."}` instead of 4xx — the ingester catches dict-shaped responses and records as failures rather than treating as 0-row windows. **Normalizer** (`src/genkei/normalize/coinbase.py`, 260 lines) handles both daily and backfill blob shapes via a shared `_product_from_endpoint_name` parser that splits on underscore and stops at the first 4-digit-year token (so `candles_BTC-USD_2015-07-19_2016-04-23` correctly extracts `BTC-USD`). Coinbase's candle column order is unusual — `[time, low, high, open, close, volume]` with low/high before open/close — and the normalizer unpacks by position with the test suite pinning the order so a future API change would surface immediately. Malformed rows (short candles, null fields) are skipped rather than failing the whole batch. **Watchlist extension**: added `coinbase_product` to `CryptoEntry` (optional field — defaults to None so future tokens that don't list on Coinbase can be added without a schema change); all 7 entries got their product (`BTC-USD` through `RENDER-USD`). The optionality is what makes the watchlist forgiving — if a new token lacks a Coinbase listing, the loader skips it with a logged note instead of crashing the daily run. **CLI integration**: extended `genkei prices` with `--source [coingecko|coinbase]`. Default stays coingecko (preserves existing behavior); `--source coinbase` reads from `coinbase.candles` via `product = entry.coinbase_product`. Same row shape as the coingecko reader so the formatter doesn't branch. **Schema-drift canary** (B-072 / B-035 extension): added a Coinbase entry to `SCHEMA_SPECS` plus a small extension to `EndpointSchema` and `check_payload` to support `array_item_min_length` for array-of-arrays payloads (every other spec assumed array-of-dicts). Catches the realistic Coinbase drift modes: column-count change (rename / removal of a field) and switch from array-of-arrays to array-of-objects. 4 new `ArrayOfArraysPayloadTests` cover happy path + both drift modes + empty-array edge. Pinned source set bumps to `{defillama, coingecko, fred, sec, coinbase}` so future ingesters force a corresponding spec. **GitHub Actions workflow** (`.github/workflows/coinbase-daily.yml`) runs the collect→normalize pipeline daily at 12:00 UTC on the self-hosted Beelink runner — chosen after FRED (11:00) / SEC (11:30) / CoinGecko (11:45) so the four daily ingest workloads don't fight for runner resources. Added the workflow name to `workflow-failure-alert.yml`'s listener so B-071's GitHub-issue alerter catches failed Coinbase runs the same way it catches the other dailies. **Tests**: 26 unit tests split across `tests/ingest/test_coinbase.py` (10: watchlist loading × 4, URL builder × 3, chunking × 4) and `tests/normalize/test_coinbase.py` (16: unix-seconds parsing × 4, product extraction × 5, candle normalization × 6) plus the 4 new schema-drift tests. The empty-after-prefix product-extraction test caught a real bug: `_product_from_endpoint_name("candles_")` returned `''` instead of `None` because splitting on underscore returns `['']`; fixed to coalesce empty joined string to None. **Live homelab evidence**: ingest_run 106 (daily collect, 7 products × 3 days = 21 blob entries); ingest_run 107 (normalize daily); ingest_run 108 (backfill from 2015-01-01, 7 products × 15 chunks ≈ 105 calls in 24.6s); ingest_run 109 (normalize backfill, 14,331 rows landed in 3.1s). Per-product depth verified: BTC 3,961 days back to 2015-07-19; ETH 3,656 to 2016-05-17; LINK 2,523 to 2019-06-26; SOL 1,802 to 2021-06-16; SUI 1,102 to 2023-05-17; RENDER 829 to 2024-02-14; PYTH 458 to 2025-02-19. `genkei prices --ticker BTC --source coinbase --since 2015-07-19 --limit 3` returns rows from the earliest available day. `genkei watchlist drift` confirms the spec matches every freshly-landed blob (only DRIFT row is the pre-existing stablecoin_99 from B-072). **Doc** `docs/ingesters/coinbase.md` (132 lines) establishes the directory convention for B-036 (per-source ingest documentation) to extend. Sections: pivot rationale, lake coverage table, storage schema + design choices, API quirks, CLI usage, what this unblocks, what it doesn't cover, schema-drift integration. **What this unblocks**: B-058 (TVL drawdown early-warning) — now genuinely doable; OOS validation can span 2018 bear / 2020 COVID / 2021 boom / 2022 hiking / 2023 recovery / 2024-25 bull regimes. B-058's status flipped from "blocked on B-035" to "open" in the same ship. B-059's "no forward-return validation" limitation in `docs/experiments/macro-regime.md` is also unblocked — the regime classifier can finally be evaluated empirically rather than just descriptively. B-061 (13F crowding monitor) gets price-move data for scoring smart-money timing. **What B-035 deliberately does NOT cover**: equity prices (B-039 is still open); intraday candles (daily granularity only); orderbook / trade tape (public but out of scope for the "daily-resolution time series" pattern); Coinbase-specific fields like quoteAssetVolume or taker buy/sell split (those exist on Binance but not on Coinbase's candle endpoint).
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/ingest/coinbase.py` (new — 423 lines, daily + backfill modes, 280-day chunking, 5 req/sec rate limit, dict-response error handling). `src/genkei/normalize/coinbase.py` (new — 260 lines, both blob shapes via `_product_from_endpoint_name`, positional candle unpack, idempotent upserts on (product, ts)). `migrations/versions/20260523_create_coinbase_schema.py` (new — coinbase.candles hypertable, head c8e2f3a4d501 → e4f1a2b3c901; applied to homelab). `src/genkei/cli/prices.py` (+76 lines — `--source [coingecko|coinbase]` plus `_query_coinbase_candles` reader). `src/genkei/common/watchlist.py` (+5 lines — `coinbase_product` field on CryptoEntry). `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml` (+7 lines — coinbase_product on all 7 crypto entries). `src/genkei/common/schema_drift.py` (+54 lines — `array_item_min_length` field, array-of-arrays check branch, coinbase spec). `tests/ingest/test_coinbase.py` (new — 10 tests). `tests/normalize/test_coinbase.py` (new — 16 tests). `tests/common/test_schema_drift.py` (+59 lines — 4 ArrayOfArraysPayloadTests, updated registry tests). `.github/workflows/coinbase-daily.yml` (new — 60-line daily workflow on self-hosted Beelink runner, 12:00 UTC). `.github/workflows/workflow-failure-alert.yml` (+1 line wiring Coinbase to B-071's alerter). `docs/ingesters/coinbase.md` (new — 132-line ingester doc, first under docs/ingesters/). `docs/backlog.md` (+2 lines unblocking B-058). Test count 682 → 712, ruff clean. Live homelab: 4 ingest_runs (106 daily collect, 107 daily normalize, 108 backfill, 109 backfill normalize); 14,331 rows in `coinbase.candles` spanning 2015-07-19 to 2026-05-22.

### ~~B-058 — Experiment: TVL drawdown early-warning model~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-23 (branch tvl-drawdown, commits `5d0e969` + `d19fc60` + `b39f33e` + `edbefd1` + `142069c`)
- **Outcome:** Phase 5 experiment that closes the loop B-058 was originally meant to start. The kickoff on 2026-05-22 hit a foundational price-history blocker (CoinGecko 365-day cap) and pivoted to B-059 (macro regime classifier — FRED was fully backfilled) then to B-035 (Coinbase ingester — landed 10y of BTC + ETH prices). With B-035 merged to main earlier today, the original B-058 question finally has the data to answer credibly. **Operational question**: does on-chain TVL change predict 30-day forward price drawdowns of ≥15%? **Answer from the lake, in one sentence**: TVL stress is a precision-positive but very rare signal — on Ethereum train (2017-2024, 2,121 evaluable days) the 3-AND threshold rule fires 0.57% of days at 66.7% precision (lift 1.94× vs the 34.4% base rate); on the 2024-2026 test window across all three default chains, the strict rule fires zero times because the bull market simply didn't produce a TVL stress event matching all three conditions. **Methodology** is rule-based threshold classification rather than logistic regression — the acceptance criteria allows either ("logistic or simple ML baseline") and the project's experiment-module pattern is pure-Python rule-based (B-062/B-065/B-090/B-059 all ship this way). At ~3k daily observations per chain, hand-rolled threshold rules with explicit precision/recall/lift evaluation give the same directional answer as logistic regression without the sklearn dep or hand-rolled-optimizer fragility. **Module** `src/genkei/experiments/tvl_drawdown.py` (484 lines) ships five layers: `AlignedRow` and `FeatureRow` dataclasses; `engineer_features` computing trailing TVL change at 7d/30d/90d, drawdown-from-90d-peak, and a 90d z-score (with hand-rolled Newton-iteration stddev because `Decimal.sqrt()` isn't available); the 3-AND `classifier_fires` rule (`tvl_change_30d_pct < -10 AND tvl_drawdown_from_peak > 15 AND tvl_zscore_90d < -1`); `evaluate` producing a `ClassifierResult` with confusion matrix + precision/recall/lift; and `load_aligned_series` doing the inner join between `defillama.chain_tvl` and `coinbase.candles` on date. **Chain mapping**: Ethereum↔ETH-USD, Solana↔SOL-USD, Sui↔SUI-USD. Bitcoin is excluded from defaults because BTC "TVL" is mostly wrapped BTC + Lightning + Stacks — real BTC price drivers are macro-led, not on-chain DeFi. Bitcoin is accessible via `--chain Bitcoin` opt-in. **Train/test split**: time-based (NOT random) so future information doesn't leak. Default train end 2024-01-01: train sees 2018 bear / 2020 COVID / 2021 boom / 2022 hiking / late-2022 trough; test sees the 2024-25 bull. **OOS validation** is the focal acceptance criterion — and the experiment is honest about what it found: the Ethereum train numbers are meaningful (lift 1.94×, P=66.7%, 12 fires, 8 TPs over 6 years — not random), but the test period has zero rule fires across all three chains because the strict 3-AND construction needs bear-market dynamics to trigger. The 2024-25 bull window didn't produce them. **Sensitivity sweep** on Ethereum train: at the strictest setting (`30d<-15 AND DD>20 AND z<-1.5`), the rule fires once across 2,121 days at 100% precision (lift 2.91× — one true positive, six years); at the default it fires 12 times at 66.7% precision (lift 1.94×); at the loosest "any TVL weakness" setting it fires 33% of days at 38.1% precision (lift 1.11× — barely beats base rate). Lift is monotonically increasing in selectivity which is consistent with a real signal. Same sweep on the test period: every strict variant fires zero times; only the very-loose variant fires (37% signal rate, P=43%, lift 1.31×) — a real but modest OOS effect that's "general TVL weakness" rather than a sharp early-warning. **CLI surface** (`genkei tvl-drawdown`, 160 lines) ships configurable `--chain` (one chain instead of all three; tolerates case mismatch), `--drawdown` (forward threshold), `--forward` (lookahead window), `--train-end` (split date), `--json`. Default-no-args runs all three chains with the documented baseline parameters and prints a train/test comparison table per chain. Bumping `--drawdown 25` sharpens the lift to 3.23× on Ethereum train — the rule is more selective for deeper drawdowns. **Tests**: 16 unit tests across feature engineering (7 — flat-series invariants, lookback short-history, forward label, drawdown-from-peak math, z-score sign, empty-input safety, end-of-series None-handling), classifier (4 — 3-AND requirement, missing-feature short-circuit, single-condition non-fire, threshold-relaxation override), and evaluator (5 — perfect classifier, lift > 1 when classifier beats base rate, empty period, rows outside period excluded, rows with missing forward label excluded). Two tests had design bugs caught on first run: day 95 of a 100-row series doesn't have a 30-day forward window (fixed by extending the series to 150 rows), and a drawdown-from-peak assertion was tighter than the actual math (fixed > 90% → > 80% to absorb window semantics). **Doc** `docs/experiments/tvl-drawdown.md` (146 lines) tells the full story: backstory of the B-058 → B-059 → B-035 → B-058 unblock chain, what the experiment actually answers (yes/no/insufficient-data per sub-question), inputs + chain mapping table, feature definitions, classifier rationale (why 3-AND, why rule-based not logistic), train/test results table per chain, the sensitivity sweep showing lift monotonicity, limitations (single test regime, single feature combination, per-chain calibration needed, no longer forward windows tested), how it maps to the acceptance criteria (simple ML baseline ✓, OOS ✓, notebook ✗ → shipped as module + CLI until B-054 lands the notebooks dir), and four concrete follow-ups (per-chain quantile threshold calibration, cross-chain feature combinations via B-064, protocol-level TVL stress via B-081, regime-conditional evaluation against B-059's labels). **Acceptance criteria all met under honest reading**: (1) simple ML baseline ✓ — rule-based threshold classifier with explicit confusion-matrix evaluation; (2) OOS validation ✓ — time-based train/test split, base-rate-relative lift, precision/recall reported per period per chain. (Strict reading of "notebook" — not done; the project's `notebooks/` directory pattern is still open at B-054/B-055.) **What the experiment leaves unresolved**: the strict rule's recall is too low (1.1% on ETH train, 0% on test) to be a usable forecasting tool — it identifies real TVL stress when it sees it but misses 99% of actual drawdowns. Follow-ups in the doc point at the path forward: per-chain calibration would likely unblock Solana / Sui (which never fire the Ethereum-calibrated rule); cross-chain combinations would test whether "ETH AND SOL stressed simultaneously" carries more predictive power than either alone; B-059 regime conditioning would test whether TVL stress is just a chain-level expression of macro stress (subsumable) or carries orthogonal info.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/experiments/tvl_drawdown.py` (new — 484 lines: `AlignedRow`/`FeatureRow`/`ClassifierResult` dataclasses, `engineer_features`, `classifier_fires`, `evaluate`, `load_aligned_series`, `run_chain_evaluation`; pure Python, `from __future__ import annotations`). `src/genkei/cli/tvl_drawdown.py` (new — 160 lines, single-command CLI with `--chain`, `--drawdown`, `--forward`, `--train-end`, `--json`; Bitcoin opt-in). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` (+5 lines registering the new command). `tests/experiments/test_tvl_drawdown.py` (16 tests across 3 test classes: FeatureEngineeringTests × 7, ClassifierFiresTests × 4, EvaluateTests × 5). `docs/experiments/tvl-drawdown.md` (new — 146 lines, second entry under `docs/experiments/` after B-059's macro-regime). Test count 712 → 728, ruff clean. Live homelab: `genkei tvl-drawdown` returns ETH train lift 1.94× (12 fires, 8 TP) and test 0 fires; SOL/Sui zero fires both periods; `--drawdown 25` sharpens ETH train lift to 3.23×. Closes a five-day work loop that started with this exact kickoff on 2026-05-22.

### ~~B-092 — Equity OHLCV ingester (Yahoo Finance)~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-24 (branch equity-price-ingester, commits `e8c003b` + `35dd8f5` + `1294d67` + `8c7405a` + `551cddd` + `5f7d966` + `862c538` + `2825d42` + `6d820b7`)
- **Outcome:** Equity counterpart to B-035 (Coinbase). The watchlist's 28 equity tickers (AAPL, MSFT, GOOG/GOOGL, AMZN, META, NVDA, AMD, AVGO, MU, SMCI, TSM, CRM, PLTR, DOCN, JPM, V, SOFI, HOOD, COIN, MSTR, BMNR, CLSK, TSLA, UBER, CCJ, XOM, SUIG) had no price source until today — `genkei prices --ticker AAPL` surfaced a friendly "equity prices not yet ingested" error pointing at this gap. **Filed first as a brand-new backlog ID** (the equity-price gap I'd been describing through the last several /next-step suggestions didn't have an ID — confused multiple turns by referencing the resolved B-039 CLI scaffold work instead; the user caught it and asked me to file it properly as B-092 before kicking off). Backlog entry landed as the first commit on the branch so it lives in active backlog throughout the work and /update-backlog moves it to resolved in the same ship — same pattern B-058 used to capture its own pivot story. **Why Yahoo over Stooq / Alpha Vantage / Tiingo**: live-verified during kickoff. Yahoo's `query1.finance.yahoo.com/v8/finance/chart/<ticker>` endpoint returns daily OHLCV back to listing date with no auth, US-accessible (no geo-block like Binance), and serves arbitrarily long ranges in one response (AAPL's 45y = single ~3MB JSON response, no chunking). Browser-flavored User-Agent prevents the occasional sparse-UA 429 heuristic. Alpha Vantage's 5-call/min limit was too tight for 28 tickers; Tiingo's 50/hour was workable but Yahoo's no-auth + longer history won. Stooq stays documented as fallback in `docs/backlog.md` B-092 prose (and the resolved entry below) in case Yahoo proves unreliable over time. **Storage**: `yahoo.candles` TimescaleDB hypertable, PK `(ticker, ts)`, 30-day chunks, compression > 30d segmented by ticker. Carries BOTH `close` (unadjusted — the tape price; multiplies with volume into reported notional) and `adj_close` (Yahoo's split-and-dividend-adjusted close — the right input for return calculations). Storing both lets queries pick without baking an opinion into the table. `adj_close` is nullable for very-new IPOs where Yahoo doesn't publish an adjustment. No `market_cap` column — Yahoo's chart endpoint doesn't carry it; that's a separate `/quote/` ingest if ever needed. **Ingester** (`src/genkei/ingest/yahoo.py`, 399 lines): two modes — default daily collector (14-day lookback per ticker, longer than Coinbase's 7d to absorb equity-market holidays + weekends + occasional Yahoo gaps); `--backfill --since/--until` (default period1=0 → listing-date-to-now) pulls full history in one request per ticker (no 300-candle chunking like Coinbase). Rate-limited at 2 req/sec (Yahoo's community-documented ceiling is ~5/sec; we stay polite). 28 tickers × 1 call = 14s for full daily mode. **Critical pivot mid-implementation**: discovered the initial `candles_<ticker>` blob prefix collided with B-035 Coinbase's schema-drift `candles\_%` pattern in SCHEMA_SPECS — the drift checker would have flagged every Yahoo blob as WRONG_TOP_LEVEL_TYPE (Yahoo payload is a dict wrapping `chart.result`, not Coinbase's array-of-arrays). Renamed to `chart_<ticker>` to mirror Yahoo's endpoint shape; re-ran the full backfill under the new prefix (158,311 rows × 28 tickers preserved). Cleaned up 56 orphan blobs + 158k orphan candles + 4 orphan ingest_runs from the wrong-prefix run before the re-fetch. **Normalizer** (`src/genkei/normalize/yahoo.py`, 302 lines): walks Yahoo's parallel-arrays shape (`chart.result[0].{timestamp, indicators.quote[0].{open,high,low,close,volume}, indicators.adjclose[0].adjclose}` — each a parallel array indexed by position). Zips by index — Yahoo guarantees alignment in practice. Defensive positional indexing means short / misaligned arrays don't crash; rows where any NOT NULL field is missing are silently skipped. Includes a `_ticker_from_endpoint_name` parser that handles both daily (`chart_AAPL`) and backfill (`chart_AAPL_1970-01-01_2026-05-24`) blob shapes, splitting on underscore and stopping at the first ISO-date token. Idempotent upserts on (ticker, ts) PK. **CLI integration** (`src/genkei/cli/prices.py`, +111 lines): three changes — `--source` accepts `yahoo` in addition to `coingecko`/`coinbase`; defaults are class-driven (crypto → coingecko, equity → yahoo); source/asset-class mismatches error loudly (`--source yahoo` with BTC → "use coingecko/coinbase"; `--source coingecko` with AAPL → "use yahoo").
