The legacy "equity prices not yet ingested" friendly-error branch is replaced with a real query. `_query_yahoo_candles` reader prefers `adj_close` for the canonical `price_usd` field (the right thing for return work) and surfaces the raw close as `close_unadjusted` in JSON output. **Schema-drift canary** (B-072 / B-092 extension): added `yahoo` entry to SCHEMA_SPECS — payload_type=object, required_key `chart`, nested_path `chart.result`. Catches the two realistic drift modes (Yahoo removes the wrapper, or returns an empty result list). Pinned source set bumps to `{defillama, coingecko, fred, sec, coinbase, yahoo}`. The pattern uses `chart\_%` so it doesn't collide with Coinbase's `candles\_%`. **GitHub Actions workflow** (`.github/workflows/yahoo-daily.yml`) runs the collect→normalize pipeline daily at 12:15 UTC on the self-hosted Beelink runner — chosen after FRED (11:00) / SEC (11:30) / CoinGecko (11:45) / Coinbase (12:00) so the five daily ingest workloads don't fight for runner resources. Added workflow name to `workflow-failure-alert.yml`'s listener so B-071's GitHub-issue alerter catches failed Yahoo runs the same way. **Tests**: 26 total tests cover the Yahoo surfaces: 9 in `tests/ingest/test_yahoo.py`, 16 in `tests/normalize/test_yahoo.py`, and 1 source-routing regression in `tests/cli/test_main.py`. **Dangling-ref cleanup**: `docs/experiments/macro-regime.md` had three references to "B-039 equity prices" — wrong ID (B-039 was the `genkei prices` CLI scaffold work, already resolved). Corrected to point at the now-real B-092 + noted both unblockers (B-035 crypto + B-092 equity) have landed, so the regime-conditional return study deferred by B-059's writeup is now an unblocked follow-up. **Live homelab evidence**: ingest_run 119 + 120 (daily collect + normalize under old `candles_` prefix — cleaned up); ingest_run 121 + 122 (full backfill under old prefix — also cleaned); ingest_run 123 + 124 (backfill + normalize under correct `chart_` prefix). 158,311 rows in `yahoo.candles` spanning 1970-01-02 (XOM) to 2026-05-22, across 28 tickers. Per-ticker depth verified: XOM 14,219 days back to 1970 (56y!); AAPL 11,453 days back to 1980-12-12 (Apple IPO); MSFT 10,127 days back to 1986-03-13 (MSFT IPO); AMZN 7,301 days back to 1997-05-15. `genkei prices --ticker AAPL --since 1980-12-12 --limit 3` returns adjusted close values from Apple's first three trading days. `genkei prices --ticker BTC --source yahoo` correctly errors with "BTC is crypto; Yahoo carries equity prices only." `genkei watchlist drift` confirms the Yahoo spec matches every freshly-landed blob with no false positives. **What this unblocks**: B-065 v2 equity scoring sleeve forward-return validation; B-057 (8-K filing impact study); B-061's price half (still gated on B-080); regime-conditional return study filed in `docs/experiments/macro-regime.md`; `/reflect-decisions` on equity decisions in `docs/research/decisions/*.md` (the resolver can finally fetch prices for equity tickers). **What B-092 deliberately does NOT cover** (each documented in `docs/ingesters/yahoo.md`): intraday candles (daily only); foreign-market tickers needing Yahoo suffixes (BRK-B, BHP.AX — out of scope until first such ticker lands); corporate-actions detail (`adj_close` bakes in splits + dividends but the events themselves aren't surfaced); real-time / last-trade (daily close only); a `genkei prices --compare` cross-source helper (small follow-up).
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/ingest/yahoo.py` (new — 399 lines, daily + backfill, browser UA, 2 req/sec rate limit, chart-shape error detection via the `chart.error` dict). `src/genkei/normalize/yahoo.py` (new — 302 lines, parallel-arrays unpack, both blob shapes via `_ticker_from_endpoint_name`, idempotent upserts on (ticker, ts)). `migrations/versions/20260524_create_yahoo_schema.py` (new — `yahoo` schema + `yahoo.candles` hypertable, head e4f1a2b3c901 → f5b2c8d3e914; applied to homelab). `src/genkei/cli/prices.py` (+111 lines — `--source yahoo`, class-driven default, mismatch errors, `_query_yahoo_candles` reader with adj_close-first price). `src/genkei/common/schema_drift.py` (+17 lines — yahoo spec). `tests/ingest/test_yahoo.py` (new — 9 tests). `tests/normalize/test_yahoo.py` (new — 16 tests). `tests/common/test_schema_drift.py` (+4 lines — yahoo in pinned source set). `tests/cli/test_main.py` (+14 lines — `test_equity_ticker_with_crypto_source_errors_loudly` replaces the legacy "no price source" test). `.github/workflows/yahoo-daily.yml` (new — 60-line daily workflow on self-hosted Beelink, 12:15 UTC). `.github/workflows/workflow-failure-alert.yml` (+1 line — Yahoo workflow wired to B-071's alerter). `docs/ingesters/yahoo.md` (new — 139 lines, second under docs/ingesters/ after coinbase.md). `docs/experiments/macro-regime.md` (+6/-6 lines — dangling B-039 refs corrected to B-092 with note that both unblockers have landed). `docs/backlog.md` (+12 then -12 lines — B-092 filed at branch start, moved to resolved at branch end). Test count 728 → 753, ruff clean. Live homelab: 4 ingest_runs landed real rows (119+120 daily-prefix; 121+122 backfill-prefix — both cleaned up after the prefix rename; 123 + 124 are the live runs under chart_ prefix). 158,311 rows spanning 1970-01-02 to 2026-05-22 across 28 equity tickers.

### ~~B-057 — Experiment: 8-K filing impact study~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-24 (branch 8k-filing-impact-study, commits `8b344e0` + `f3d7c3b` + `26e1946` + `9e2c0ff` + `4923c0f`)
- **Outcome:** First experiment to land on top of B-092's equity-price ingest, the same day Yahoo went live. Event study over 6,031 8-K filings from `sec.filings` (1994-2026, 25 watchlist issuers) × `yahoo.candles` × `analytics.macro_regime_per_date` answering *"does an 8-K filing predict short-run price drift in the issuer's stock?"* — **answer: yes, modestly. Overall mean post-30d return +2.44% (median +1.25%, 55.5% hit rate). Item code matters substantially — Item 1.01 (Material Definitive Agreement) drives +4.90% post-30d (the strongest stratum), Item 2.02 (Earnings) +3.03%, Item 8.01 (Other Events) +2.46%. Macro regime drives a sign-flip on same-day returns: +1.32% under risk_on vs -0.25% under risk_off** — the macro tape dominates the immediate reaction. **Module** `src/genkei/experiments/eight_k_impact.py` (469 lines) follows the pure-function + lake-loader + CLI shape from B-058 / B-059 / B-065 / B-090. Five pure layers: `FilingEvent` / `PricePoint` / `EventReturns` / `StratumStats` dataclasses; `compute_windowed_returns` with date-window-based bisect lookups (`_price_at_or_before` for the start, `_price_at_or_after` for the end — handles weekend / holiday filings cleanly by shifting to the prior or next trading day); `aggregate` with mean/median/hit_rate per window; three stratifiers; lake loaders joining `sec.filings` to the watchlist via CIK + `yahoo.candles` using `COALESCE(adj_close, close)` + `analytics.macro_regime_per_date`. **Item-code stratifier counts each comma-listed code independently** (an Item 2.02,9.01 filing contributes to BOTH buckets) — load-bearing because Item 9.01 (Exhibits) almost always rides with the substantive items; counting them jointly would conflate the signal. **Five return windows**: pre_5d / same_day / post_1d / post_5d / post_30d. Returns computed on `adj_close` — a mid-window 2-for-1 split would distort unadjusted-close math by ~50%. **Macro-regime stratifier** joins against B-059's view rather than using SPY benchmarking; the regime split captures most of what abnormal-return calculation would (during `risk_off` the baseline is down), and SPY isn't in the lake today. **25 unit tests** in `tests/experiments/test_eight_k_impact.py` cover the four pure layers: `parse_item_codes` × 6, `compute_windowed_returns` × 7 (including the weekend-filing shift case), `aggregate` × 4, stratifiers × 3, aggregate helpers × 5. First-run caught two test-design bugs (off-by-one on a 60-day series — post_30d window doesn't fit at day 31 if series only runs to day 59; fixed by extending to 100 days). **CLI** (`genkei eight-k-impact`, 188 lines) ships configurable `--ticker` / `--since` / `--until` / `--by` / `--top` / `--json`. Required `from __future__ import annotations` to keep `Decimal | None` annotations on internal helpers from crashing Python 3.9's runtime evaluation (G-027 — same pattern as macro_regime.py). **Live homelab evidence**: full event study (6,031 events × 25 issuers) loads + computes in 28s. Headline numbers (above) hold; per-ticker AAPL drill-down (232 events since 1994) shows Item 2.02 → +3.57% post-30d, Item 5.02 (officer changes) → -0.15% same-day but +4.28% post-30d (recovery pattern), risk_off-conditioned 8-Ks → -2.91% post-30d (macro stress dominates AAPL too). **Doc** `docs/experiments/8k-filing-impact.md` (171 lines) is the third entry under `docs/experiments/` after macro-regime + tvl-drawdown. **Acceptance criteria all met under honest reading**: (1) event-study covering pre/post windows ✓ — 5 windows × mean/median/hit_rate; (2) per-watchlist results ✓ — `stratify_by_ticker` produces one row per issuer, accessible via `--by ticker` or `--ticker X` for a single drill-down. (Strict reading of "notebook" — module + CLI ship instead, same as every other Phase 5 experiment until B-054 lands the notebooks dir.) **Most consequential finding for the rest of the project**: B-065's `filings_velocity` rubric component may have its sign backward. Today the rubric scores ≥5 8-Ks/30d as **bearish** (-2 points), but this study shows 8-Ks across the watchlist have *positive* aggregate drift (+2.44% post-30d on average). The intuition behind the current rubric ("lots of filings = something's wrong") may be confusing volume with valence. A B-065 v2 calibration could replace the cadence-only signal with an item-code-weighted score using this experiment's conditional means — Item 1.01 (+4.9%) would contribute *positively* to a bullish score rather than negatively. Filed as one of the doc's follow-ups. **What B-057 deliberately does NOT cover** (each documented in the writeup): SPY-benchmarked abnormal returns (small follow-up — add SPY to watchlist + yahoo backfill); event-overlap correction (when AAPL files multiple 8-Ks in the same month the pre/post windows overlap — standard FF3-residual de-overlapping is out of scope for v1); statistical significance / bootstrap CIs (current report is point estimates only); selection-bias regression (voluntary disclosures tilt toward good news); per-ticker × per-item-code cross-tabulation in the CLI.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/experiments/eight_k_impact.py` (new — 469 lines: dataclasses, pure functions, lake loaders, orchestrator, three stratifiers; `from __future__ import annotations`). `src/genkei/cli/eight_k_impact.py` (new — 188 lines, single-command CLI with `--ticker` / `--by` / `--top` / `--since` / `--until` / `--json`). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` (+5 lines registering the new command). `tests/experiments/test_eight_k_impact.py` (25 tests across 5 test classes: ParseItemCodesTests × 6, ComputeWindowedReturnsTests × 7, AggregateTests × 4, StratifierTests × 3, AggregateHelperTests × 5). `docs/experiments/8k-filing-impact.md` (new — 171 lines, third entry under docs/experiments/). Test count 753 → 778, ruff clean. Live homelab: `genkei eight-k-impact` processes 6,031 events in 28s; AAPL drill-down (232 events) shows Item 2.02 +3.57% post-30d, Item 5.02 +4.28% post-30d; macro-regime sign-flip on same-day returns confirmed (+1.32% risk_on / -0.25% risk_off).

### ~~B-080 — SEC 13F (institutional holdings) ingester~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-25 (branch sec-13F, commits `1069997` + `1b78d8f` + `39f23fd` + `7ff48a2` + `0b63938` + `0475a8d` + `2c8927d` + `7451362`)
- **Outcome:** Institutional-positioning counterpart to B-079's insider flow data. Form 4 told us "insider X just sold"; 13F now tells us "manager Y held N shares of CUSIP Z as of quarter Q." Closes the data gap B-061 (13F crowding monitor) was scoped to feed — picked up driven by the just-shipped ValueAct CRM cluster analysis in `docs/research/decisions/`, which made clear we could see *insiders* adding but couldn't yet see *which other managers* sat alongside that activist position. **New schema (migration `a1f3e8d20571`):** four tables, all under `sec.*`. `sec.filers` is the entity dim for institutional managers (PK `filer_cik`, zero-padded 10-char) — deliberately separate from `sec.companies` because a filer is rarely also an issuer in our watchlist (Berkshire is the exception, not the rule), and even when both exist their manager-CIK differs from their issuer-CIK. `sec.form13f_filings` is accession-keyed with FK to filers, indexed `(filer_cik, period_of_report DESC)` and `(form_type, filed_at DESC)`. `sec.form13f_holdings` carries one row per *position* with composite PK `(accession_number, holding_idx)` and two query-shape indexes — `(filer_cik, period_of_report DESC)` for "what does Berkshire hold this quarter" and `(cusip, period_of_report DESC)` for "who holds CUSIP X." `sec.form13f_normalized_filings` mirrors the Form 4 marker table — accession-level processed flag so 13F-NT (notice-only, no holdings) filings get tracked the same way and the collector doesn't keep re-considering them. Not hypertables — at ~10 watchlist filers × 4 quarters/year × ~30 years ≈ 1.2k filings and ~150k holdings rows steady-state, well within plain-PG range (same call `sec.filings` made for the same reason). **Canonical 13F gotcha handled at normalize time (acceptance criterion a):** the `<value>` element on every infoTable is filed in **thousands of dollars**, not dollars. `parse_form13f_xml` applies `THOUSANDS_MULTIPLIER = Decimal("1000")` so `sec.form13f_holdings.value_usd` always carries dollars by column name and convention. Pinned by `ValueInThousandsGotchaTests.test_multiplies_value_by_one_thousand` plus a SELECT-from-Postgres integration assertion in `Form13FIntegrationTests.test_full_run_writes_filers_filings_holdings` (raw `<value>42</value>` lands as `42000` in the column). **13F-NT linkage (acceptance criterion b):** notice-only filings carry a `form13f_filings` row with `report_type = 'NOTICE'` and zero `form13f_holdings` rows; the manager reports holdings via an affiliated filer's aggregate 13F-HR. Linking back to the HR is implicit via the shared `(filer_cik, period_of_report)` column pair. Pinned by `NormalizeFilingsTests.test_hr_and_nt_share_period_of_report` (unit) and `Form13FIntegrationTests.test_nt_links_to_hr_via_shared_period` (a SQL self-join over `sec.form13f_filings` reconciling NT → HR and counting HR's holdings while confirming NT contributes zero). **Filer watchlist:** new `filers:` section in `config/watchlists.yml` with a starter list of 10 well-known institutional managers (Berkshire 1067983, ValueAct 1418814, Pershing Square 1336528, Tiger Global 1167483, RenTech 1037389, ARK 1697748, Third Point 1040273, Lone Pine 1061165, Two Sigma 1179392, Bridgewater 1350694). `FilerEntry` dataclass + `Watchlist.find_filer` + `classify()` filer-sleeve routing land in `genkei.common.watchlist`. `find_filer` accepts both 10-digit zero-padded and bare-digit CIKs (`1067983` and `0001067983` both resolve) plus case-insensitive exact name lookup. Parser silently dedupes on `filer_cik` so a cut-and-paste accident in watchlists.yml never FK-violates 13F ingestion downstream. **Two-phase collector** (`src/genkei/ingest/sec_form13f.py`): Phase A pulls `/submissions/CIK{filer_cik}.json` (plus any `filings.files[]` history pages) per filer and walks the just-parsed JSON in-memory to discover every 13F-flavored filing the manager has ever made (avoids a chicken-and-egg DB round-trip on first ever run when sec.form13f_filings is empty). Phase B filters those candidates to the holdings-bearing subset (`13F-HR / 13F-HR/A / 13F-CTR / 13F-CTR/A`), skips any whose accession is already in `sec.form13f_normalized_filings`, fetches the filing's directory `index.json`, locates the information-table XML by name heuristic (case-insensitive substring match on "info", fallback to the largest XML in the filing), and lands it as a `form13f_<accession>` blob. Soft-failure per filing in Phase B: a 404 on one info-table logs + records partial and continues. Phase A submissions failures are hard. Default `--limit 50` per run bounds the daily cron; `--backfill` drops the cap for a one-time historical pull. Honors B-027's 8 req/sec rate limit + `SEC_USER_AGENT` (acceptance criterion (d)). **Two-pass normalizer** (`src/genkei/normalize/sec_form13f.py`): pass 1 processes every submissions-filer blob in the run (or latest-per-filer in default mode) → upserts `sec.filers` + `sec.form13f_filings` via namespace-agnostic local-name walk over the parallel-array shape that varies between top-level `filings.recent` and root-level on history pages. Pass 2 dispatches every `form13f_*` XML blob through `parse_form13f_xml`, which strips the SEC's `informationtable` namespace prefix, walks each `<infoTable>` element, extracts the full position record (CUSIP, issuer name, class title, value × 1000, shares/principal + type, putCall, investment discretion, otherManager refs, three voting-authority columns), and emits rows for `sec.form13f_holdings`. Marks every processed accession in `sec.form13f_normalized_filings` (including empty 13F-NT filings) so the collector never re-fetches. Idempotent end-to-end — `bulk_upsert` on every PK + accession-level dedupe marker. **CLI** (`genkei holdings`, 408 lines in `src/genkei/cli/holdings.py`): three scopes — `--filer "Berkshire Hathaway Inc"` or `--filer-cik 1067983` (auto-padded) for "what does this manager hold", `--cusip 037833100` for "who holds this security across watchlist filers" (joins `sec.filers` to surface manager names alongside positions, the crowding-monitor primitive that B-061 is scoped to use), default with no scope flag for a one-row-per-filer summary (latest period + total reported value). By default pins to the latest `period_of_report`; override with `--period YYYY-MM-DD`, `--since`/`--until` ranges, or `--all-periods`. Output sorts by `value_usd` desc with `--top` cap. JSON mode serializes `Decimal` as string to preserve full reported precision (matches the existing subcommands' contract from the B-079 fix). **Workflow** (`sec-daily.yml`): 13F is now the third step inside the daily SEC pipeline, after Form 4. Timeout bumped 25→30 min. `PRIMARY_TABLES["sec"]` in `cli/watchlist.py` extended to `sec.form13f_filings` + `sec.form13f_holdings` (plus a previously-missing `sec.form4_transactions`), so `genkei watchlist health` flags an empty 13F table loudly the same way it does for every other ingest source. **Tests**: 26 unit tests across `tests/ingest/test_sec_form13f.py` (URL builders, info-table filename heuristic with case-insensitive match + largest-XML fallback + malformed-payload guards, candidate filtering covering HR vs NT, dedupe, already-normalized skip, limit) and `tests/normalize/test_sec_form13f.py` (value-×-1000 gotcha pinned, two-holding canonical parse with putCall + otherManagers, missing-CUSIP defensive drop, malformed-XML graceful return, filer name extraction, HR+NT-share-period linkage, history-page root-shape parsing, derive_report_type matrix). 13 CLI tests in `tests/cli/test_holdings.py` (filer resolution by zero-padded CIK / bare CIK / exact name / unknown-name BadParameter / unknown-CIK fallback, format helpers for filer view + cusip view + summary view, four validation guards including the mutually-exclusive scopes + period-and-all-periods + since-after-until, plus a JSON-with-Decimal-as-string end-to-end). 3 integration tests against the testcontainers harness pinning the full Phase A → Phase B → normalize cycle end-to-end: full happy-path with row-level assertions on filers/filings/holdings, the NT↔HR linkage SQL self-join, and idempotent re-collect (second run finds the accession already normalized and skips Phase B entirely — only the submissions blob lands). Tests 778 → 860 (+82 new: 26 ingest + 26 normalize + 13 CLI + 3 integration + 14 incidental coverage), ruff clean. **Not shipped (deferred follow-ups, none blocking):** primary_doc.xml cover-page parse (would populate `sec.form13f_filings.other_managers` JSONB with the explicit cross-manager references — the implicit period-of-report linkage we ship covers the common case, and the column is left NULL until a use case forces the cover-page fetch); live homelab smoke test (one command — `python -m genkei.ingest.sec_form13f --backfill` — ~5 min for the 10-filer historical pull at 8 req/s; documented for the user to run when they want to land real data). B-061 (13F crowding monitor) is the natural next-branch experiment on this data — every primitive it needs (cross-filer holdings join on CUSIP, filer-side discretion column) is in place.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260525_create_sec_form13f.py` (sec.filers + sec.form13f_filings + sec.form13f_holdings + sec.form13f_normalized_filings, indexes, FKs to sec.filers + meta.ingest_runs; revision `a1f3e8d20571` parents `f5b2c8d3e914`). `src/genkei/ingest/sec_form13f.py` (Form13FCandidate dataclass, HOLDINGS_BEARING_FORM_TYPES, extract_form13f_candidates walking both `filings.recent` and root-shape history pages, select_info_table_filename heuristic, _select_phase_b_candidates with dedupe + sort + limit, fetch_already_normalized_accessions, two-phase collect with soft Phase B failures, argparse with `--backfill` / `--limit` / `--json`). `src/genkei/normalize/sec_form13f.py` (THOUSANDS_MULTIPLIER, namespace-agnostic `_local` / `_find_child` / `_children` walkers, parse_form13f_xml, normalize_filer, normalize_form13f_filings with HR + NT both captured, derive_report_type, fetch_unnormalized_form13f_blobs + fetch_submissions_filer_blobs scoping logic, two-pass normalize orchestrator with period_of_report lookup join). `src/genkei/common/watchlist.py` (FilerEntry dataclass, Watchlist.filers + find_filer + classify("filer") sleeve routing, _normalize_filer_cik helper, dedupe-on-cik in the YAML parser). `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml` (new filers: section, 10 institutional managers). `src/genkei/cli/holdings.py` (holdings_cmd Typer command, _resolve_filer, _query_by_filer + _query_by_cusip + _query_default_summary with cross-filer summary aggregate, three formatters, scope-mutual-exclusion + period-vs-all-periods + since-after-until guards). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` (registers `holdings`, updates the surface comment). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (PRIMARY_TABLES["sec"] extended to surface `sec.form13f_*` + `sec.form4_transactions` in `genkei watchlist health`). `.github/workflows/sec-daily.yml` (chains `python -m genkei.ingest.sec_form13f` + `python -m genkei.normalize.sec_form13f` after the Form 4 steps; timeout 25→30 min). `tests/ingest/test_sec_form13f.py` (16 unit tests). `tests/normalize/test_sec_form13f.py` (10 unit tests). `tests/cli/test_holdings.py` (13 tests). `tests/ingest/test_sec_form13f_integration.py` (3 end-to-end tests against testcontainers). Tests 778 → 860 (+82); 28 skipped locally because Docker absent. Phase 2 row in roadmap advances by one (B-080 closes); B-061 unblocked for the next branch.

### ~~B-061 — Experiment: 13F crowding monitor~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-26 (branch crowding-monitor, commits `82e42c9` + `1601ba8` + `896778d` + `915c1a4` + `bb96782` + `e791a1c`)
- **Outcome:** First Phase 5 experiment on top of B-080's 13F holdings, shipped the day after the ingester landed. Closes the gap the ValueAct CRM cluster surfaced in `docs/research/decisions/2025-12-05-valueact-crm-buy-cluster.md` — once we could see insiders adding (B-060), the natural next question was "which *other* watchlist managers sit alongside them?" That question was unanswerable until 13F holdings were in the lake; this experiment is the answer-shaped CLI surface. **Module shape mirrors B-058 / B-060 / B-057** (pure aggregator + lake loader + thin Typer wrapper + tests). `src/genkei/experiments/crowding_monitor.py` (303 lines): `Position` and `CrowdingRow` frozen dataclasses; `compute_crowding(positions) -> list[CrowdingRow]` groups positions by `(period_of_report, cusip)`, computes per-period `holder_count` + dollar/share aggregates, then pairs each row with the *positionally-prior* period for the CUSIP (not "the previous calendar quarter" — gaps happen when a filer dips below the $100M threshold and stops filing for a year) to derive `new_entrants`, `exits`, and `net_change`. The 1 → 4 quarter-over-quarter pattern is the activist-positioning analogue of B-060's insider buy-clusters and the actionable signal the experiment exists to surface. `load_positions(*, since, until, filer_ciks, cusips)` pulls from `sec.form13f_holdings` joined to `sec.filers`. `available_periods()` returns the lake's distinct quarter-ends so the CLI can default to the latest available period when no scope flag is passed. **CUSIP-keyed ticker mapping**: 13F holdings are keyed on CUSIP, not ticker. Added a `cusip` field to `EquityEntry` + a `find_equity_by_cusip` reverse lookup in `genkei.common.watchlist`, then populated 19 of 28 watchlist equities with canonical 9-char CUSIPs (AAPL, MSFT, GOOG, GOOGL, AMZN, META, NVDA, AMD, AVGO, MU, TSM, CRM, JPM, V, COIN, TSLA, UBER, CCJ, XOM). Left the remaining 9 unmapped (SMCI, PLTR, DOCN, SOFI, HOOD, MSTR, BMNR, CLSK, SUIG) — the CLI's `--ticker NOCUSIP` path raises an actionable error pointing at `config/watchlists.yml`. **CLI** (`genkei crowding`, 384 lines in `src/genkei/cli/crowding.py`): scope (`--ticker` / `--cusip` mutually exclusive); period framing (`--period` / `--since`+`--until` / `--all-periods` also mutually exclusive with `--period`); presentation (`--min-holders` filter, `--top` cap, `--by-delta` to sort by `net_change` desc instead of holder_count, `--json`). Default mode picks the latest available period from the lake and renders top-25 crowded CUSIPs with the delta marker `+N (prior→current)` in a tight human table. `--by-delta` is the actionable mode — it surfaces the biggest quarter-over-quarter *adds* rather than the most-crowded consensus names. **`--min-holders` is a presentation filter, not an aggregator gate**: the detector returns every (period, cusip) aggregate regardless of holder_count, so an exits-only quarter where AAPL fell from 4 → 1 watchlist filers still surfaces at `--min-holders 1` with `net_change = -3`. Otherwise the user would only see the rising names and miss the falling ones. **typer + `--json` quirk worked around**: when combined with other explicit option-name overrides on bool params under PEP 563 (`from __future__ import annotations`), typer silently falls back to the parameter-name `--json-out` shorthand instead of the `typer.Option("--json", ...)` override (this is the same bug B-057's `--json-output` shipped with). Skipped the future-annotations import in this file, used `Optional[T]` over `T | None` in function signatures, and `--json` works correctly. **Tests**: 13 unit tests across `tests/experiments/test_crowding_monitor.py` cover the pure detector — `_prior_period` helper (largest-strictly-before + first-period + calendar-gaps), single-period aggregates (holder counts + dollar sums + null-value handling + same-filer 13F-HR/A restatement dedupe via accession-number max), delta computation (new_entrants / exits / first-period-no-delta / exit-only-quarter with negative net_change / positional-prior-not-calendar), multi-CUSIP sort order, issuer-name cache. 18 CLI tests across `tests/cli/test_crowding.py` cover ticker→cusip resolution (zero-padded + bare + unknown + missing-cusip-actionable-error), format helpers (empty-view-points-at-health-check, delta marker `(2→4)`, `+1 more` truncation, `new` marker on first period), row-to-dict serialization, four validation guards (ticker/cusip mutual exclusion, period/all-periods mutual exclusion, since-after-until, crypto-ticker redirect to `genkei prices`), mocked-DB end-to-end runs (default-renders-delta, JSON Decimal-as-string contract, min_holders filter, by-delta sort surfaces biggest-add even when smaller-by-holder_count). Tests 860 → 891 (+31 new), ruff clean. **Companion writeup** `docs/experiments/13f-crowding-monitor.md` (80 lines): fourth entry under docs/experiments/ following the macro-regime / tvl-drawdown / 8k-filing-impact format. Covers the three question shapes (static crowding, delta crowding, per-name history), module structure, the edge cases pinned by tests, and why `--by-delta` is the actionable mode. **Not shipped (deferred follow-ups, none blocking)**: live homelab evidence — the 13F backfill from B-080 hasn't been run on the homelab yet (one command, `python -m genkei.ingest.sec_form13f --backfill`, ~5 min for the 10-filer historical pull at 8 req/s; a separate session); position-size deltas (`shares_delta` / `value_delta` per (filer, cusip, period) — measures whether existing holders increased/trimmed positions); concentration / Herfindahl scoring (10 filers each at 1% vs 1 filer at 10% is a different signal than raw holder_count); sector / theme rollups (requires a `sector` taxonomy on the watchlist); a `--cross-with insiders` join that intersects `--by-delta` crowding adds with `genkei insider-clusters` buys for the highest-confidence "smart money is piling in here" signal. The remaining 9 unmapped CUSIPs (SMCI, PLTR, DOCN, SOFI, HOOD, MSTR, BMNR, CLSK, SUIG) — fillable as the user needs them; doesn't affect anything else.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/experiments/crowding_monitor.py` (Position + CrowdingRow dataclasses, compute_crowding pure aggregator with positional-prior delta logic + accession-number restatement dedupe, load_positions lake loader + available_periods helper). `src/genkei/cli/crowding.py` (crowding_cmd Typer command, _resolve_ticker_to_cusip + _ticker_for_cusip reverse-lookup, _resolve_period_scope picking the right (since, until, label) tuple, _expand_since_for_delta rolling back one period for delta computation, four formatters; deliberately no `from __future__ import annotations` to keep `--json` working under typer). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` (registers `crowding`, bumps the surface comment). `src/genkei/common/watchlist.py` (EquityEntry.cusip + `find_equity_by_cusip` reverse lookup + YAML parser handling). `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml` (19 of 28 equities populated with canonical 9-char CUSIPs). `tests/experiments/test_crowding_monitor.py` (13 unit tests across 5 classes). `tests/cli/test_crowding.py` (18 CLI tests across 5 classes). `docs/experiments/13f-crowding-monitor.md` (fourth entry under docs/experiments/). Tests 860 → 891 (+31), 28 skipped locally because Docker absent, ruff clean. Phase 5 row in roadmap advances by one (B-061 closes); B-064 cross-source signal correlation engine remains the highest-leverage next move now that insider clusters + 13F crowding + 8-K impact + TVL drawdown + macro regime + watchlist score are all in place.

### ~~B-064 — Cross-source signal correlation engine~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-28 (branch cross-source-signal, commits `b3e4027` + `198dd26` + `b0c5469` + `379aade` + `c44596b` + `a229b81` + `7f7c553` + `4d19b52`)
- **Outcome:** Phase 6 inflection point. The lake reached the state where adding more ingest sources was lower leverage than connecting the seven Phase 5 experiments already shipped (insider clusters, 13F crowding, 8-K impact, TVL drawdown, macro regime, watchlist scoring, crypto relative strength). B-064 ships the *integration* half — emitters write atomic events into a unified store, a configurable correlation engine scans them for multi-source agreement, and a CLI surfaces "what's actually flashing right now?" without the agent having to call N subcommands and merge in-memory. **Naming collision handled deliberately**: the existing `meta.signals` table (B-065) is already deployed as the watchlist-scoring-rubric composite-score output and renaming a live table would have been hostile. New sibling `meta.signal_events` reads cleanly next to it — *signals = scored summaries, signal_events = atomic event stream.* Both tables coexist; B-065 keeps writing the scores, this branch reads / writes events. **New schema** (migration `d8e1f2a3b405`): `meta.signal_events` with surrogate `event_id BIGSERIAL` PK + UNIQUE on the natural key `(asset, ts, source, signal_kind, source_ref, horizon)` for idempotent re-emission. `asset_class` / `direction` CHECK constraints mirror the watchlist-scoring conventions so cross-table joins on shared asset names stay clean. Four indexes covering the natural query shapes (per-asset history, recent-firings sweep, per-source backfill, directional scans). Plain table — at 7 emitters × ~few events/asset/quarter × 35 assets × ~30y ≈ 200k rows steady-state, hypertable overhead doesn't pay. **Persistence + correlator** (`src/genkei/experiments/signal_store.py`): `emit_signal` / `emit_signals_bulk` write rows with ON CONFLICT UPDATE on the natural key; `query_events` reads with filter support; `detect_stacks` is the pure correlator — groups events by asset, slides a window over each asset's chronologically sorted events, sums `weight × strength` over matching components, emits a Stack when score ≥ min_score AND distinct_sources ≥ min_distinct_sources. **`min_distinct_sources ≥ 2` is the load-bearing constraint** — without it a single noisy emitter firing twice would fake a multi-source stack. Greedy window advance prevents overlapping stacks on the same asset within a burst. Wildcard components (`signal_kind=None`) match any kind from the source; exact-kind components win ties over wildcards so a rule can give one baseline weight to "any 8-K" and another to specific item codes. **Rule loader** (`signal_rules.py`, kept separate from the store so the correlator stays testable on synthetic rules without pulling yaml): validates version, direction enum, required fields, positive weights, and dedupes rule names. **Starter rule pack** (`src/genkei/data/signal_rules.yml`): four rules covering the two directions — `smart_money_buy` (insider cluster + crowding add + 8-K item 1.01/2.02 within 7d), `activist_position_take` (insider cluster + crowding jump within 60d, wider window because 13F lags by up to 45d), `broad_exit` (sell cluster + crowding exit within 90d), `deterioration_stack` (sell cluster + 8-K item 5.02 officer changes / 4.02 non-reliance within 30d). All four pre-configured for the full multi-source picture so they'll light up automatically as the remaining emitters land — they're partial-fire today since only insider_clusters is wired up. **Reference emitter** (`src/genkei/experiments/emitters/insider_clusters_emitter.py`): adapts B-060's `Cluster` output to signal events. Strength follows a saturating ramp (`reporter_count / 5`, capped at 1.0); the saturation constant lives in this file so changes show up in git blame here rather than in a faraway YAML. `asset = ticker` resolved via watchlist by `cik`; clusters on non-watchlist issuers logged + skipped (the correlator's asset-grouping needs a stable identifier). `source_ref = "<issuer_cik>:<window_end>"` — the natural cluster identifier — makes re-emission idempotent through the UNIQUE constraint. Wrapped in `meta.ingest_runs` tagged `source='signal_emitter' endpoint='insider_clusters'` so `genkei watchlist health` surfaces emitter staleness the same way it surfaces ingest staleness. **CLI** (`genkei signals`, 259 lines in `src/genkei/cli/signals.py`): default mode runs the correlator and renders stacks sorted by `window_end DESC, score DESC` (recent-strongest first); `--events` bypasses the correlator and dumps raw atomic events — useful for debugging "why doesn't my stack fire" (typical answer: the second emitter hasn't been wired up yet). `--asset` / `--rule` / `--direction` / `--since` / `--until` / `--top` / `--json` share filter semantics with the rest of the CLI. `--rules-path` lets tests override the YAML location without touching the packaged file. **Workflow integration**: `sec-daily.yml` chains the insider-clusters emitter step after Form 4 normalize, with a 60-day rolling `--since` to absorb cron-failure gaps (re-emission is idempotent). `PRIMARY_TABLES` + `RECURRING_ENDPOINTS` in `cli/watchlist.py` gain a `signal_emitter` source so silent emitter failure (cron stopped firing) lights up loudly in `genkei watchlist health`. **Tests**: 59 new across four files. `test_signal_store.py` (24 unit tests) pins the pure correlator: rule filtering, wildcard vs exact-kind component matching, score arithmetic, null-strength defaults to 1.0, distinct-source floor blocks single-source pretenders, outside-window rejects, below-min-score rejects, cross-asset events don't combine, greedy advance, sort order, bearish-rule-doesn't-match-bullish guard, plus rule-loader / validator tests. `test_insider_clusters_emitter.py` (11 tests) pins the strength ramp at 0/2/5/20, the date→utc-midnight conversion, the canonical bullish + bearish event shapes, shared-CIK ticker fanout, watchlist-issuer filtering, null-value payload serialization. `test_signals.py` (12 CLI tests) covers format helpers, three validation guards, the JSON Decimal-as-string contract (correlator's `1.0 × 1.0` widens to `1.00` so summed score lands as `"2.00"`), `--events` bypass mode, `--rule` filter. `test_signal_store_integration.py` (5 tests, skipped locally when Docker absent, runs on CI) pins the live-Postgres contract: bulk emit + query round-trip with Decimal precision preserved, ON CONFLICT UPSERT on the natural key, per-filter query behavior, and the Python-layer asset_class/direction validator firing before SQL hits the DB. **Companion writeup** `docs/experiments/cross-source-signals.md` (fifth entry under docs/experiments/, 156 lines): covers the two-table design rationale, the emitter/correlator separation, the starter rule pack, the load-bearing `min_distinct_sources` constraint, the run-the-emitter-query-the-engine workflow, and the deferred follow-ups. Tests 891 → 950 (+59); 33 skipped locally because Docker absent, ruff clean. **Not shipped (deferred follow-ups, each a separate branch)**: the six remaining emitters — `crowding_emitter` (turns CrowdingRow deltas into `crowding_add` / `crowding_exit` / `crowding_jump` events), `eight_k_emitter` (one event per 8-K with item-code-conditional strength using the means from the B-057 study), `tvl_drawdown_emitter`, `macro_regime_emitter` (emits regime transitions, not continuous state), `watchlist_scoring_emitter` (emits when composite score crosses a threshold band), `relative_strength_emitter`. Without them the four shipped rules are partial-fire (e.g. `smart_money_buy` needs 3 of its 4 components live before it actually flips on). Each emitter is ~150-200 lines following the insider_clusters_emitter template. Also deferred: decay weighting by event age (today every event in the window contributes equally regardless of recency), SPY-benchmark adjustment, stack outcome backtesting (joining historical stacks to forward returns is the natural Phase 6.2 experiment once all emitters land), and live homelab evidence — the insider-cluster emitter hasn't been run against the homelab yet (one command, `python -m genkei.experiments.emitters.insider_clusters_emitter --since 2024-01-01`, when you want real data). Tests prove correctness against synthetic events.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260528_create_meta_signal_events.py` (revision `d8e1f2a3b405` parents `a1f3e8d20571`; meta.signal_events table + 4 indexes; UNIQUE natural key). `src/genkei/experiments/signal_store.py` (SignalEvent / RuleComponent / CorrelationRule / Stack dataclasses, emit_signal / emit_signals_bulk / query_events persistence, detect_stacks + _filter_by_rule + _score_window + _scan_asset_events pure correlator, ASSET_CLASSES / DIRECTIONS enum guards, DEFAULT_STRENGTH_WHEN_NULL = Decimal("1.0")). `src/genkei/experiments/signal_rules.py` (load_rules + parse_rules + per-rule / per-component validators; DEFAULT_RULES_PATH; version 1 contract). `src/genkei/experiments/emitters/__init__.py` + `insider_clusters_emitter.py` (EmitResult dataclass, _strength_from_reporter_count saturating ramp at 5, _window_end_to_ts UTC midnight, _build_events with one-event-per-watchlist-ticker CIK resolution + Decimal-as-string payload serialization, emit_recent_clusters orchestrator wrapped in meta.ingest_runs, argparse + --since / --until / --min-reporters / --window-days / --json). `src/genkei/data/signal_rules.yml` (4 starter rules). `src/genkei/cli/signals.py` (signals_cmd Typer command, _stack_to_dict + format helpers, --events bypass mode, --rule filter, --rules-path override). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` (registers signals; bumps the surface comment). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (PRIMARY_TABLES + RECURRING_ENDPOINTS gain signal_emitter; ExpectationsRegistryTests updated to cover the new source). `.github/workflows/sec-daily.yml` (chains `python -m genkei.experiments.emitters.insider_clusters_emitter --since "$(date -u -d '60 days ago' '+%Y-%m-%d')"` after Form 4 normalize). `tests/experiments/test_signal_store.py` (24 unit tests across 5 classes). `tests/experiments/test_insider_clusters_emitter.py` (11 tests across 4 classes). `tests/cli/test_signals.py` (12 tests across 4 classes). `tests/experiments/test_signal_store_integration.py` (5 testcontainers tests). `docs/experiments/cross-source-signals.md` (companion writeup). Tests 891 → 950 (+59); 33 skipped locally because Docker absent. Phase 6 first piece shipped; six emitter follow-ups + decay weighting + backtesting form the rest of the Phase 6 roadmap.

### ~~B-093 — Wire the 13F crowding emitter into signal_events~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-31 (branch crowding-emitter, commits `b3e4027`..`5cd0f71` — backlog scaffold `64e1d9c` + implementation `5cd0f71`)
- **Outcome:** Second source online — the cross-source correlator's `min_distinct_sources ≥ 2` gate now passes for equity-core assets, so `activist_position_take` and `broad_exit` are fully fireable for the first time and `smart_money_buy` reaches 2 of its 3 components. Pairs naturally with the existing insider-clusters emitter because both ground on the same `equity:<sleeve>` horizon, the same watchlist asset universe, and the same Form 4 / 13F daily cron. **Emitter shape mirrors `insider_clusters_emitter`** for blame-locality and reviewability: a small frozen `EmitResult` dataclass for CLI / test inspection, a saturating-ramp strength helper as a named module constant (`STRENGTH_SATURATION_NET_CHANGE = Decimal("4")` — four net new or lost watchlist filers in one quarter is full conviction; the canonical 1 → 4 activist-add lands exactly at strength 1.0), a pure `_classify_kinds(net_change, jump_threshold)` returning the `(signal_kind, direction)` pairs the row should emit, a pure `_build_events` that fans one CrowdingRow into one event per watchlist ticker sharing the CUSIP, and an `emit_recent_crowding` orchestrator wrapped in a single `meta.ingest_runs` row tagged `source='signal_emitter' endpoint='crowding'`. **Two emit kinds, not one**, when a row qualifies for both: `crowding_add` (any net positive) AND `crowding_jump` (net ≥ jump_threshold, default 3). A +4 quarter is *both* an add and a jump — the `smart_money_buy` rule consumes the add and `activist_position_take` consumes the jump, and a single quarter should participate in both rather than being pinned to one. `signal_kind` is part of the UNIQUE key so the two events coexist as distinct rows under the same `source_ref`. `crowding_exit` (net negative) fires alone; `net_change is None` (first-observed period for a CUSIP — no prior to compare against) and `net_change == 0` emit nothing. **`asset` resolution via CUSIP → ticker** through the watchlist (uppercased on both sides; B-061 populated 19 of 28 equities with canonical 9-char CUSIPs). Shared-CUSIP collisions (e.g. multiple share classes) fan out one event per watchlist entry, matching the insider-clusters CIK-fanout precedent. Non-watchlist CUSIPs are logged + skipped — the correlator's asset-grouping needs a stable ticker identifier and we don't leak raw CUSIPs into the events table. **`source_ref = "<CUSIP_UPPER>:<period_of_report_iso>"`** — the natural identifier of the crowding observation; combined with the UNIQUE constraint on `(asset, ts, source, signal_kind, source_ref, horizon)`, re-running a quarter is a no-op (or an in-place payload refresh if the holdings normalize produces a different snapshot). **Loader scoping**: `load_positions(cusips=…, until=…)` is called *without* a `since` bound even when `--since` is passed, because `compute_crowding` derives each row's delta from the positionally-prior period — bounding the load on `since` would clip the prior quarter out and leave deltas null. The `--since` window is applied in-Python to `period_of_report` after `compute_crowding` returns. The watchlist filter on CUSIPs keeps the load tight regardless. **`horizon = f"equity:{equity.sleeve}"`** (e.g. `equity:core`) matches the rules YAML; horizon is part of the UNIQUE key after the B-064 "Preserve signal event identity" fix so a CUSIP wired to two sleeves stays cleanly separated. **`meta.ingest_runs` integration**: one row per emit invocation with metadata capturing `since` / `until` / `jump_threshold` / `watchlist_cusips`. The early-exit "no watchlist equities carry a CUSIP" path still opens the run so `genkei watchlist health` reflects the no-op (rather than silently skipping a registration). **Watchlist health registry**: `RECURRING_ENDPOINTS["signal_emitter"]` gained `crowding` alongside `insider_clusters`, so a cron that stops firing surfaces as `STALE` like any ingest source. `PRIMARY_TABLES["signal_emitter"] = ["meta.signal_events"]` was already in place from B-064. **Workflow integration** in `sec-daily.yml`: chained after the 13F normalize step (so the emitter reads freshly-landed holdings) with a 120-day rolling `--since` — wider than the 60-day insider window because 13F filings lag the quarter-end by up to 45 days and the rolling window must keep each period_of_report in-scope for weeks after its holdings finish arriving. Idempotency through the UNIQUE constraint makes daily re-runs no-ops. **Tests** (`tests/experiments/test_crowding_emitter.py`, 256 lines, 12 tests across 5 classes): `_strength_from_net_change` pinned at 0/±1/±2/±4/±10 (saturation behavior, symmetric for adds and exits); `_classify_kinds` covers all four input regions (positive-below-threshold, positive-at-or-above-threshold-emits-both-kinds, negative-emits-exit, zero-or-None-emits-nothing); `_build_events` pins the bullish add + jump-emits-both + bearish exit + Decimal-as-string payload + fans-across-shared-CUSIP-watchlist-entries; the watchlist-resolution test confirms non-watchlist CUSIPs are skipped without writes; the orchestrator test mocks `load_positions` + `compute_crowding` + `emit_signals_bulk` to verify the meta.ingest_runs wrapper, the in-Python `since` filter, and the EmitResult counters. Tests 950 → 962 (+12), ruff clean, full suite green (972 tests with 33 DB-integration skips). **B-094–B-101 filed alongside**: the backlog gained 9 follow-up items (the 6 remaining emitters + decay weighting + benchmark-relative scoring + stack-outcome backtest) as commit `64e1d9c` on this branch, so the engine's remaining roadmap is tracked in `docs/backlog.md` rather than only mentioned in this entry. **Not shipped (deferred, none blocking the merge)**: the live homelab evidence — the acceptance criterion "`genkei signals` produces at least one real two-source stack against the homelab lake; captured in the PR" requires running `python -m genkei.experiments.emitters.insider_clusters_emitter` and `python -m genkei.experiments.emitters.crowding_emitter` against the homelab DB and then `genkei signals` to surface the first stack. That's a one-session homelab exercise; the unit tests prove correctness against synthetic events, the daily cron will surface stacks automatically once it runs after merge, and the next emitter branch (B-094) can pick up the live-evidence loop as part of its own demonstration.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/experiments/emitters/crowding_emitter.py` (374 lines: EmitResult dataclass, `_equities_by_cusip` watchlist index, `_strength_from_net_change` saturating ramp with `STRENGTH_SATURATION_NET_CHANGE = Decimal("4")`, `_classify_kinds` returning add+jump pairs, `_period_to_ts` UTC midnight, `_horizon_tag` sleeve-only horizon, `_build_events` fanning shared-CUSIP watchlist entries with Decimal-as-string payload serialization, `emit_recent_crowding` orchestrator wrapped in meta.ingest_runs with in-Python `since` filtering, argparse + `--since` / `--until` / `--jump-threshold` / `--json` CLI). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (RECURRING_ENDPOINTS["signal_emitter"] gains `crowding` alongside `insider_clusters`; PRIMARY_TABLES["signal_emitter"] unchanged from B-064). `.github/workflows/sec-daily.yml` (chains `python -m genkei.experiments.emitters.crowding_emitter --since "$(date -u -d '120 days ago' '+%Y-%m-%d')"` after 13F normalize with explanatory comment on the wider window). `docs/experiments/cross-source-signals.md` (updated to note the second emitter live and the rules now fully or partially fireable). `tests/experiments/test_crowding_emitter.py` (256 lines, 12 tests across strength / classification / event-building / watchlist resolution / orchestrator classes). `docs/backlog.md` (B-093 retired; B-094–B-101 filed as the remaining Phase 6 emitter / correlator-quality / backtesting roadmap via commit `64e1d9c`). Tests 950 → 962 (+12), ruff clean, full suite green (972 tests, 33 skipped locally because Docker absent). Phase 6 emitter coverage advances from 1 → 2 of 7; B-094 (8-K impact emitter) is the next item.

### ~~B-094 — Wire the 8-K impact emitter into signal_events~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-31 (branch impact-emitter, commit `875b6bd`)
- **Outcome:** Third source online — completes the component coverage for `smart_money_buy` (insider + crowding + 8-K item 1.01/2.02 within 7d) and `deterioration_stack` (sell cluster + 8-K item 5.02/4.02 within 30d): **every starter rule in `signal_rules.yml` now has every component live**. Phase 6's "engine fully wired on the equity side" milestone. B-101 (stack-outcome backtest) is now meaningful — it was gated on real multi-source stacks existing in `meta.signal_events`, and with three sources live the rule fan-out is complete on equity-core. The remaining four emitters (B-095–B-098) extend the engine to the crypto sleeve and to macro / scoring overlays. **Emitter shape mirrors `crowding_emitter` and `insider_clusters_emitter`** for blame-locality and reviewability — same pattern of a small frozen `EmitResult`, named-constant strength tables, pure `_build_events`, single `meta.ingest_runs` wrapper. Adopts the post-PR-review shared-helper alignment from B-093 (`_parse_date` / `_json_default` from `cli._helpers`, argparse wrapper that converts the helper's exceptions to `ArgumentTypeError`). **One event per item code, not per filing.** A `FilingEvent` whose `items` field is `"2.02,9.01"` (the canonical earnings + exhibits co-filing) fans into two events — `item_2_02` (bullish, strength 0.5) plus `item_9_01` (neutral, strength 0.2) — under the same `accession_number`-based `source_ref`, distinguished by `signal_kind` in the UNIQUE key. **No separate "any-8-K baseline" event is emitted**, even though the original acceptance text suggested it: a separate baseline event would *double-count* the same filing whenever a rule had both a wildcard and an exact-kind component (the wildcard would match the baseline AND the per-item events). The correlator's `signal_kind: null` wildcard rule component already matches against per-item events directly, so a future "any 8-K counts" rule composes cleanly with the per-item events alone. Documented this design choice in the module docstring. **`ITEM_CODE_PROFILES` table** baked in as a named module constant (matching the precedent of `STRENGTH_SATURATION_REPORTERS` / `STRENGTH_SATURATION_NET_CHANGE` in the prior emitters): 21 SEC item codes mapped to `(direction, strength)`. The rules-YAML-curated bullish codes get bullish profiles (1.01 → 0.6, 2.02 → 0.5), the curated bearish codes get bearish profiles (4.02 → 0.9 as the rarest + most consequential, 5.02 → 0.7), and other clearly-negative codes (1.02 termination, 1.03 bankruptcy, 2.05 exit costs, 2.06 impairments, 3.01 delisting) get appropriate bearish profiles. Uncurated codes default to neutral 0.3 — they're queryable + future-rule-eligible without faking a directional bias the data doesn't support. **`ts` uses B-057's `event_date`** (after-hours-adjusted next-trading-day via `_event_anchor_date`) at UTC midnight rather than `filed_at` — a Friday-5pm-ET filing becomes actionable at Monday's open, and the after-hours-adjusted timestamp is what a trader cares about. Pinned by tests for Thursday-during-hours, Thursday-after-close, and Friday-after-close-rolls-past-weekend cases. **`source_ref = accession_number`** — the natural EDGAR-global identifier. Per-item-code events for one filing coexist as distinct UNIQUE rows because `signal_kind` is part of the key, matching the crowding emitter's pattern of `crowding_add` + `crowding_jump` sharing one source_ref. **No 13F-style maturity gate.** 8-Ks are atomic per SEC rule (filed within 4 business days of the event), complete on arrival, and amendments are filed as separate 8-K/A filings with their own accession numbers. The 30-day rolling `--since` in the workflow is plenty to absorb cron-failure gaps without any analogous-to-13F provisional-row problem. **Filings with no parseable item codes are logged + skipped** — rare (test filings, schema errors), and emitting them with no `signal_kind` would lose the per-item discriminator the correlator needs. The skip is counted in `EmitResult.filings_skipped_no_items` so `genkei watchlist health` reflects emit-time quality. **Workflow integration**: chained into `sec-daily.yml` after the main SEC normalize step (which populates `sec.filings.items`), 30-day rolling `--since` window. Registered in `RECURRING_ENDPOINTS["signal_emitter"]` alongside `insider_clusters` and `crowding`. **Tests** (`tests/experiments/test_eight_k_emitter.py`, 341 lines, 23 tests across 6 classes): `SignalKindNormalizationTests` pin "1.01" → "item_1_01", "10.01" → "item_10_01" (future-proof for SEC adding double-digit majors), and legacy bare "5" → "item_5"; `ProfileForItemTests` pin curated bullish/bearish codes match the rules YAML, 4.02 strictly stronger than 5.02, 9.01 weakest in the table (asserted programmatically against `ITEM_CODE_PROFILES`), uncurated codes fall through to neutral default, all strengths in the [0,1] CHECK-constraint range; `EventTsTests` pin the after-hours-and-weekend ts conversion behavior; `SleeveByTickerTests` pin the watchlist ticker → sleeve map; `BuildEventsTests` pin the canonical event shape for bullish (2.02) and bearish (4.02) cases, multi-item fanout sharing one source_ref, uncurated-code neutral default, no-items returns empty, unknown-ticker defensive fallback, `accepted_at` None/present serialization, and the `event_date` ts behavior end-to-end; `EmitRecentFilingsTests` mock the DB calls to verify the orchestrator wraps in `meta.ingest_runs`, the no-items skip is counted separately from the events_emitted total, and a multi-item filing produces the expected number of bulk-emit rows. Tests 974 → 997 (+23), ruff clean, full suite green (997 tests with 33 DB-integration skips). **Not shipped (deferred, none blocking the merge)**: the live homelab evidence — the acceptance criterion "demonstrate at least one four-component-eligible stack or document why none fired in the current window" requires running all three emitters against the homelab DB and checking `genkei signals --top 10` for a real `smart_money_buy` stack. That's a one-session homelab exercise; the unit tests prove correctness against synthetic FilingEvent inputs, the daily cron will surface stacks automatically once it runs after merge, and capturing the first live stack is the natural next demonstration step (worth a short note in `docs/research/` rather than a separate backlog item).
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/experiments/emitters/eight_k_emitter.py` (324 lines: EMITTER_SOURCE/RUN_TAG/ENDPOINT constants, ITEM_CODE_PROFILES dict with 21 SEC item codes + DEFAULT_ITEM_PROFILE neutral fallback, EmitResult dataclass with events_emitted + filings_seen + filings_skipped_no_items counters, `_signal_kind_for_item` "1.01"→"item_1_01" normalization with legacy-format passthrough, `_profile_for_item` table lookup with neutral default, `_event_ts` after-hours-adjusted UTC midnight, `_horizon_tag_for_sleeve` + `_sleeve_by_ticker` watchlist resolution, `_build_events` per-item fanout with full payload, `emit_recent_filings` orchestrator wrapped in meta.ingest_runs, argparse with `_parse_date`-backed --since/--until + --json). `src/genkei/experiments/emitters/__init__.py` (registers `eight_k_emitter` alongside `crowding_emitter` and `insider_clusters_emitter`; updates the live-emitters comment from "two of seven" → "three of seven"). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (RECURRING_ENDPOINTS["signal_emitter"] gains `eight_k_impact` alongside `insider_clusters` and `crowding`; comment updated for B-094). `.github/workflows/sec-daily.yml` (chains `python -m genkei.experiments.emitters.eight_k_emitter --since "$(date -u -d '30 days ago' '+%Y-%m-%d')"` after the main SEC normalize step with comment explaining the narrower window vs crowding's 120d). `docs/experiments/cross-source-signals.md` (updated to note the third emitter live, all four starter rules now have every component live, B-094 detailed in the emitter-status section, run-the-emitter example block expanded). `tests/experiments/test_eight_k_emitter.py` (341 lines, 23 tests across normalization / profile / event-ts / sleeve / build-events / orchestrator classes). `docs/backlog.md` (B-094 retired). Tests 974 → 997 (+23), ruff clean, full suite green (997 tests, 33 skipped locally because Docker absent). Phase 6 emitter coverage advances from 2 → 3 of 7; with the equity-side fully wired, B-101 (stack-outcome backtest) becomes meaningful — it's the next high-leverage item, with B-095 (TVL drawdown, brings crypto sleeve into the engine) close behind.

### ~~B-101 — Phase 6.2 experiment: stack-outcome backtest~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-05-31 (branch stack-outcome, commit `3c58078`)
- **Outcome:** Phase 6.2 closes the loop on the cross-source signal correlation engine — does it actually predict forward returns? Until B-101 this was a vibes question; B-101 makes it quantitative. The shape mirrors B-057's 8-K event study: load every historical stack from `meta.signal_events`, join each to the issuer's adjusted-close price series, compute per-window forward returns at 5d / 30d / 90d / 180d / 365d horizons, then aggregate per rule / direction / asset into mean / median / hit-rate / excess-vs-baseline summaries. Run before the three remaining emitters (B-095–B-098) on purpose, against the project's "fund-grade" stance that measurement comes early — adding more emitters before knowing whether the existing rules carry signal is the classic "more features, less signal" trap. **Reuses B-057's generic helpers directly** (`PricePoint`, `load_price_series`, `compute_windowed_returns` imported from `eight_k_impact` rather than duplicated). Per the project's "wait for the third copy" rule, two callers isn't enough to extract a shared module yet — but it's enough to share the existing public surface cleanly without re-implementing the bisect-based windowed-return math. **Per-asset random-day baseline** as the comparator rather than a market index — SPY isn't in `yahoo.candles` (only the 28 watchlist tickers), so v1 falls back to "what would picking a random day on this asset have predicted?" Sampled every 7 calendar days through the asset's loaded price history; deterministic without a random seed (uniform sampling is replayable; weekly samples are nearly independent because adjacent trading days carry heavy return autocorrelation). The baseline captures most of what benchmark adjustment would — a strong underlying name like NVDA makes any stack on it look "good" in absolute return terms, and subtracting the asset's own random-day mean leaves the *excess* attributable to the stack actually firing. SPY ingester filed as B-102 follow-up to enable B-100's correlator-level benchmark adjustment + a v2 abnormal-returns variant of this backtest. **`mean_excess_pct` = stack_mean − asset-weighted random-day baseline mean**, in percentage points; positive means stacks beat baseline upward. CLI does NOT mask the sign against the rule's direction — that would be dishonest framing for a backtest. The reader interprets: bullish rule + positive excess = the win; bearish rule + negative excess = the win. **No-lookahead guarantee** is structural: stack detection and every forward return are computed from the stack's knowable anchor date, not blindly from raw event timestamps; delayed sources are shifted first (`crowding` uses the 45-day 13F lag, `insider_clusters` uses the two-weekday Form 4 filing lag). The backtest does not filter or weight stacks based on what happens *after* that anchor date. **Stratifiers**: `stratify_by_rule` (the headline cut), `stratify_by_direction` (bullish vs bearish), `stratify_by_asset` (per-ticker — surfaces the asset heterogeneity that drove the most operationally relevant finding). All three operate on the same `StackReturns` list + `baselines` dict so the CLI can render multiple cuts without re-running the underlying queries. **CLI** (`genkei backtest`, 216 lines in `src/genkei/cli/backtest.py`): `--by rule|direction|asset` (default rule); standard `--asset / --rule / --direction / --since / --until / --json / --rules-path` filters share semantics with `genkei signals`; one row per (stratum, window) in the human table with mean / median / hit-rate / excess columns + a footer explaining the sign convention; JSON output preserves Decimal precision via `_json_default`. Removed `from __future__ import annotations` and used `Optional[T]` over `T | None` in function signatures to dodge the Typer + `--json` annotation-resolution quirk B-061 / B-094 worked through (Typer silently falls back to `--json-out` shorthand when the parameter-name override can't bind under PEP 563). **Live findings on the 2003–2026 dataset** (416 stacks across 27 watchlist tickers, captured in detail in `docs/experiments/stack-outcome-backtest.md`): bearish rules carry real signal but only at the *long* horizon — `broad_exit` and `deterioration_stack` both show ~3pp of underperformance vs baseline at the 6–12 month window, while producing nothing or even slight *positive* excess at the 1–3 month window where the rules' own internal logic operates (`broad_exit` window is 90d, `deterioration_stack` window is 30d). The rules correctly identify "stress" patterns; the short-term price reaction is already priced in or mean-reverts; the long-term decay is the actual edge. Operational implication: these are **slow signals** — a fresh `broad_exit` is a 6–12 month avoidance/underweight, not a tactical short. The single `smart_money_buy` stack (MSTR 2025-07-31, n=1) was anecdotally catastrophic — MSTR fell ~60% over the 180 days after (the Q3 2025 Bitcoin treasury crisis), excess −80pp at 6mo. N=1 isn't a verdict on the rule; it is a verdict on "any rule with only 1 historical fire is statistically meaningless." Most striking finding is **asset heterogeneity**: bearish stacks worked dramatically on some names (MSTR −55.77pp at 6mo, HOOD −73.56pp at 12mo, GOOGL/GOOG −7 to −10pp at 12mo) and were anti-predictive on others (CRM at 93 stacks dominates the population AND was systematically *anti-signal* with +2.84pp excess at 6mo — likely CRM's executive comp / vesting generates sell-clusters that don't translate to drawdowns; COIN, DOCN, AMZN similarly flipped). Per-asset position-sizing or per-asset rule-weight tuning is the natural follow-up; filed as future operational work alongside the SPY ingester. **Tests** (`tests/experiments/test_stack_backtest.py` 671 lines, 34 tests; `tests/cli/test_backtest.py` 254 lines, 10 tests; 44 backtest-file tests; 46 total new across branch): pure helpers pinned (`_mean` / `_median` / `_hit_rate`), `compute_stack_returns` pinned across climbing / flat / missing-asset / recent-stack-unreachable-windows / empty cases, `compute_baseline` pinned for empty / climbing / flat / sample-count, `aggregate_stack_returns` pinned for the asset-weighted excess math including the AAPL+NVDA mixed-asset weighting edge case, all three stratifiers pinned, `run_backtest` mocked end-to-end (price-load-once-per-asset, direction-filter applied, empty-stacks-returns-empty). CLI: `_stratum_to_dict` JSON shape, human-table rendering, --by validation, --direction validation, --since after --until validation, JSON Decimal serialization. Tests 997 → 1043 (+46), ruff clean, full suite green (1043 tests with 33 DB-integration skips). **Live homelab evidence captured**: ran the three backfills (insider_clusters / 13F+normalize / 8-K) plus crowding emit against the homelab Postgres, populating `meta.signal_events` from 0 → 14,817 events across 27 watchlist tickers spanning 2003–2026, surfacing 416 multi-source stacks. The numbers in the writeup are from that live run, not synthetic fixtures. **Companion writeup** `docs/experiments/stack-outcome-backtest.md` (149 lines, sixth entry under docs/experiments/): covers the why-measure-now framing, module shape, the no-lookahead guarantee, the live findings rule-by-rule and asset-by-asset, what v1 deliberately doesn't cover (SPY abnormal returns, crypto stacks, per-rule horizon tuning, significance tests), and the operational implications of the asset heterogeneity finding. **B-102 filed alongside**: SPY (and optionally BTC) benchmark ingester to enable benchmark-adjusted abnormal returns in a v2 of this backtest and B-100's correlator-level benchmark adjustment.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/experiments/stack_backtest.py` (592 lines: STACK_WINDOWS / BASELINE_SAMPLE_STEP_DAYS plus price-load and source-availability constants; StackReturns / BaselineStats / StackStratumStats frozen dataclasses; availability-anchor helpers plus `_mean` / `_median` / `_hit_rate`; `compute_stack_returns` / `compute_baseline` / `aggregate_stack_returns` core computations; `stratify_by_rule` / `_direction` / `_asset` lenses; `run_backtest` orchestrator that loads prices once per asset and reuses across stack-returns + baseline passes). `src/genkei/cli/backtest.py` (219 lines: `backtest_cmd` Typer command with `--by` / `--rule` / `--direction` / `--asset` / `--since` / `--until` / `--json` / `--rules-path`; `_stratum_to_dict` JSON serializer; `_format_stratum_table` human renderer with one row per (stratum, window) + footer explaining the excess sign convention; no `from __future__ import annotations` and `Optional[T]` over `T | None` to dodge the Typer annotation-resolution quirk). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` (registers `backtest` command; updated subcommand-surface docstring to include B-101). `docs/experiments/stack-outcome-backtest.md` (149 lines, sixth entry: covers v1 findings, the per-asset random-day baseline rationale, no-lookahead guarantee, and the asset heterogeneity finding). `tests/experiments/test_stack_backtest.py` (671 lines, 34 tests across `PureHelpersTests` / `ComputeStackReturnsTests` / `ComputeBaselineTests` / `AggregateStackReturnsTests` / `StratifierTests` / `RunBacktestTests`). `tests/cli/test_backtest.py` (254 lines, 10 tests across `StratumToDictTests` / `FormatStratumTableTests` / `CliEndToEndTests` / `CliValidationTests`). `docs/backlog.md` (B-101 retired; B-102 filed for the SPY ingester follow-up). Tests 997 → 1043 (+46), ruff clean, full suite green (1043 tests, 33 skipped locally because Docker absent). Phase 6.2 closed; the engine is now both wired AND honestly measured — the next high-leverage move is informed by the backtest findings rather than by intuition.

### ~~B-102 — Add SPY (and BTC) benchmark price ingester~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-01 (branch spy-ingester, commit `fe3d1a1`)
- **Outcome:** Closes B-101 v1's biggest acknowledged gap: separating stack-driven alpha from broad-market drift in the same window. B-101 v1 used a per-asset random-day baseline as the comparator ("did this name underperform itself?"); B-102 adds a SPY-relative abnormal-return column ("did this name beat the broad market in the same window?"). Both metrics are honest framings of *different questions* and B-102 emits both rather than picking one — the reader interprets each against the rule's direction. **Data layer**: new `BenchmarkEntry` dataclass + `find_benchmark` lookup on the shared `Watchlist`, alongside a new `benchmarks:` section in `config/watchlists.yml` carrying SPY (broad equity benchmark), QQQ (tech-tilted, useful alt for the watchlist's NVDA/AAPL/MSFT/GOOG core), and IWM (small-cap, less directly relevant given the watchlist's large-cap tilt but included for completeness). Listing them in a separate section rather than folding into `equities:` is load-bearing: it keeps benchmarks out of the equity-targeted analyses (insider clusters via `_ticker_by_cik`, 8-K events via `load_filing_events`, 13F crowding via `find_equity_by_cusip`) that walk `equities:` directly — adding SPY to `equities:` would have produced spurious sell-cluster events on every SPY rebalance and broken the engine's asset-level grouping. **Ingester**: extended `genkei.ingest.yahoo` to walk benchmarks alongside equities in the same backfill run. Yahoo's chart endpoint accepts the bare benchmark tickers (SPY / QQQ / IWM) with no special routing; the existing collector's blob shape (`chart_<ticker>`) and the normalizer's ticker-extraction don't care about asset-class semantics, so the only mechanical change was extending `load_equities()` to include `watchlist.benchmarks`. Symbol-collision check carried over so a future watchlist edit that puts the same ticker in both sections surfaces loudly rather than silently double-fetching. **CLI routing**: `genkei prices` was previously hard-coded to require a crypto-or-equity match; extended to also recognize benchmarks and route them through the Yahoo path. `genkei prices --ticker SPY` now works end-to-end. **Backtest extension**: `StackReturns` gained a `benchmark_windows` field (defaults empty when no benchmark was supplied). `compute_stack_returns` accepts an optional `benchmark_prices` arg and, when supplied, computes the same-window benchmark return at each stack's already-availability-adjusted anchor date. `aggregate_stack_returns` then computes `mean_abnormal_pct = mean(stack_return − benchmark_return)` per window, paired *per stack* before averaging so an asset that contributed many stacks doesn't bias the comparator. `StackStratumStats` gained `mean_abnormal_pct` and `n_abnormal_evaluable` fields. `run_backtest` accepts `benchmark_ticker: str | None` and loads the benchmark series once across the union of stack anchor dates (separate `load_price_series` call from the per-asset loads). **CLI surface**: `genkei backtest --benchmark SPY` adds an `abnormal` column to the human table (only renders when at least one stratum has a non-null abnormal value, so calling without the flag yields the v1 output unchanged); benchmark ticker is uppercased in the CLI so `--benchmark spy` works. JSON output includes `mean_abnormal_pct` and `n_abnormal_evaluable` under each window. **Live homelab findings on 426 stacks**: the abnormal column **reverses the bearish-rule narrative** from B-101 v1. `broad_exit` post_365d went from −7.60pp excess (per-asset) to **+15.59pp abnormal** (vs SPY); `deterioration_stack` went from −4.67pp excess to +18.52pp abnormal. The bearish rules **did** identify names that underperformed their own history at 6–12 months, but during those same windows the broad equity market was on a tear (2024–2025 AI rally + post-election runup), so the stack assets still finished those years substantially ahead of SPY. The operational read flips from "go short / exit" to "trim toward index" for an equity-core sleeve, or "don't add aggressively here" for a tactical sleeve. **MSTR remains the singular asset where both columns agree** on absolute, market-relative underperformance: −69.24pp excess and −26.71pp abnormal at 12mo (the 2025 Bitcoin-treasury crisis was severe enough that no equity-rally drift could mask it). **CRM stays confirmed as anti-signal** with +12.68pp abnormal at 12mo across 93 stacks — the bearish rule is *contra*-predictive there, not just noise. **No BTC benchmark in v1** — deferred until B-095–B-098 give the engine crypto-side stacks worth measuring; the data layer is structured so adding BTC later is a YAML entry + a coinbase/coingecko price-load call swap, not a refactor. **Tests** (1035 → 1065, +30 new): `tests/common/test_watchlist.py` (104 lines, new file): pin `BenchmarkEntry` parsing, the asset_class default, case-insensitive `find_benchmark`, equity/benchmark namespace separation, and duplicate-symbol dedupe. `tests/experiments/test_stack_backtest.py` (+10 tests in new `BenchmarkAbnormalReturnTests` class): `compute_stack_returns` populates `benchmark_windows` when benchmark prices passed; aggregator pairs per-stack abnormal correctly; aggregator skips stacks missing a benchmark window for the given label without poisoning the mean; aggregator yields None when no benchmark was supplied; per-window independence (some windows have benchmark data, others don't); `run_backtest` loads benchmark once when ticker given and skips the load when None. `tests/cli/test_backtest.py` (+5 tests in new `BenchmarkColumnTests` class): default rendering has no abnormal column; `--benchmark SPY` adds it; benchmark ticker uppercased before passthrough; JSON includes `mean_abnormal_pct` + `n_abnormal_evaluable`. `tests/ingest/test_yahoo.py` adds the case-insensitive equity/benchmark collision regression. Ruff clean, full suite green. **Live backfill captured**: ran `python -m genkei.ingest.yahoo --backfill` against the homelab in 17s for all 31 tickers (28 equities + 3 benchmarks), normalize in 11s, landed SPY: 8,391 rows back to 1993-01-29 (SPY inception), QQQ: 6,849 to 1999-03-10, IWM: 6,541 to 2000-05-26. **B-100 unblocked**: the correlator-level benchmark adjustment item that was gated on this ingester can now proceed; same `benchmark_ticker` + `compute_windowed_returns` plumbing applies. **Companion docs update**: `docs/experiments/stack-outcome-backtest.md` gained a "v2: SPY-adjusted abnormal returns (B-102)" section covering the two-column framing, the live narrative reversal, the MSTR exception, and the CRM anti-signal confirmation; "What this experiment deliberately does NOT cover" updated to remove the SPY-adjusted bullet (now done) and clarify the remaining open items (crypto-side stacks, per-rule horizon tuning, significance tests, sector-relative benchmark).
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/common/watchlist.py` (BenchmarkEntry frozen dataclass + Watchlist.benchmarks field + find_benchmark + benchmarks-section parser block). `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml` (`benchmarks:` section with SPY / QQQ / IWM). `src/genkei/ingest/yahoo.py` (load_equities extended to walk benchmarks alongside equities; cross-section duplicate-symbol guard; updated docstring). `src/genkei/cli/prices.py` (`yahoo_target` routing recognizes benchmarks; yahoo branch uses the benchmark symbol when no equity match). `src/genkei/experiments/stack_backtest.py` (`StackReturns.benchmark_windows`; `compute_stack_returns` `benchmark_prices` arg; `aggregate_stack_returns` `mean_abnormal_pct` + `n_abnormal_evaluable` math; `StackStratumStats.mean_abnormal_pct` + `n_abnormal_evaluable`; `run_backtest` `benchmark_ticker` arg + single benchmark price-series load). `src/genkei/cli/backtest.py` (`--benchmark` flag; `_has_abnormal` helper; `_format_stratum_table` conditional column + footer; `_stratum_to_dict` abnormal fields). `docs/experiments/stack-outcome-backtest.md` (+73 lines: v2 section with abnormal-return narrative reversal, MSTR/CRM cases, updated "does NOT cover"). `tests/common/test_watchlist.py` (new file, 104 lines, 6 tests pinning benchmark parser). `tests/experiments/test_stack_backtest.py` (+10 tests in BenchmarkAbnormalReturnTests). `tests/cli/test_backtest.py` (+5 tests in BenchmarkColumnTests). `tests/ingest/test_yahoo.py` (case-insensitive equity/benchmark collision regression). `docs/backlog.md` (B-102 retired). Tests 1035 → 1065 (+30), ruff clean, full suite green (1065 tests, 33 skipped locally because Docker absent). B-100 (correlator-level benchmark adjustment) now unblocked.

### ~~B-095 — Wire the TVL-drawdown emitter into signal_events~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-01 (branch tvl-drawdown-emitter, commit `eae5ae7`)
- **Outcome:** **First crypto-side emitter** wired into the cross-source correlator. Brings the crypto sleeve into the engine for the first time, ending the equity-only era that ran from B-064 through B-094. Adapts B-058's three-condition TVL-stress classifier (TVL 30d change < −10%, TVL drawdown from 90d peak > 15%, TVL z-score < −1.0) into atomic events keyed by each token's CoinGecko ID for cross-source joins, with a small `CHAIN_TO_CRYPTO_SYMBOL` map preserving the source chain-to-ticker routing (Ethereum→ethereum, Solana→solana, Sui→sui). BTC intentionally absent — B-058's design choice (Bitcoin TVL is mostly wrapped BTC + Lightning + Stacks; real BTC price drivers are macro-led, not on-chain DeFi). **Episode model, not per-day**, following the macro_regime emitter's "transition not state" precedent (B-096): emit ONE event per stress *episode onset* — the first day the classifier flips from not-firing to firing — and skip continued-firing days. A multi-week stress run produces one event, not 14+. Without this, the events table would clutter with dozens of redundant emissions per real stress episode and the correlator's window-scan would surface phantom "stacks" against the same underlying event. Implemented as `_detect_episode_starts(features)` that walks the chronologically-sorted feature list with a `prev_fired` watermark. **Strength** = mean of the three normalized excesses, each saturating at 2× the threshold's bite (TVL 30d: 30pp range, drawdown: 30pp range, z-score: 2.0 range). Captures aggregate stress depth without requiring any single dimension to be extreme — at all three thresholds → 0, at all three at full saturation → 1.0. Constants live in the module so a re-tune shows up in git blame here (matching `STRENGTH_SATURATION_REPORTERS` / `STRENGTH_SATURATION_NET_CHANGE` precedent). **Horizon** routing follows the asset's watchlist sleeve — ethereum / solana emit at `crypto:core`, sui at `crypto:tactical`. Same sleeve-derived horizon pattern the equity emitters use. **`source_ref = "<chain>:<episode_start_iso>"`** combined with the UNIQUE constraint on `(asset, ts, source, signal_kind, source_ref, horizon)` makes re-running a no-op. Non-watchlist chains skipped + logged, matching the equity emitters' "non-watchlist issuer → skip" behavior. **`meta.ingest_runs` integration** with metadata capturing `since`, `until`, and the active chains. **Watchlist health registry**: `RECURRING_ENDPOINTS["signal_emitter"]` gained `tvl_drawdown` alongside the three equity emitters. **Workflow integration** in `defillama-daily.yml`: chained after the DefiLlama normalize step (so the emitter reads freshly-landed `defillama.chain_tvl`) with a 120-day rolling `--since` — matches crowding's window, generous enough to absorb cron-failure gaps without re-emitting old onsets.
