The emitter loads the full available aligned series regardless of `--since` (the feature engineer needs ≥90 days of trailing history to compute the rolling-peak / z-score features, and the episode-onset detector needs the prior day's state). `--since` is applied in-Python to the *emission* window after features + onsets are computed. **Pre-staged `crypto_tvl_stress_combo` rule** added to `signal_rules.yml` scoped to `crypto:core`: pairs `tvl_drawdown_stress` with the future `relative_strength/laggard_crossing` (B-098) within a 30-day window. Until B-098 lands, the rule won't fire a stack but TVL events still land in `meta.signal_events` for inspection — same partial-fire pattern the equity-core rules had between B-064 and B-093. **Live homelab backfill against 2017-present data produced three historical episodes**, all on ETH, all clustered in the 2018 ICO-bubble crash: 2018-08-05 (strength 0.78, TVL change −98% / drawdown 99% / z-score −1.68), 2018-12-13 (strength 0.69, TVL change −48% / drawdown 66% / z-score −1.11), 2018-12-29 (strength 0.75, TVL change −67% / drawdown 82% / z-score −1.49). These are the canonical "TVL collapsed while price is in a 90d drawdown and z-score is unusually low" patterns the classifier was designed to catch. **SOL and SUI haven't fired** — both chains' TVL has mostly grown over their available history (SOL from 2021-03, SUI from 2023-05). The three-condition AND is selective enough that years can pass without a fire, which is correct for a "real stress episode" signal — and which is also why this rule will likely produce sparse stacks once B-098 lands (a crypto-side stack will need a *real* coordination of TVL stress + relative-strength weakness, not noise). **Tests** (`tests/experiments/test_tvl_drawdown_emitter.py`, 515 lines, 26 tests across 7 classes): `_normalized_excess` pinned across both bound directions (sign +1 for upper, −1 for lower) at none-value, below-threshold, at-threshold, partial-excess, full-saturation, over-saturation. `_strength_from_features` pinned at thresholds → 0, canonical mid-stress → 0.5, full saturation → 1.0, one-condition-only → 1/3, plus pins on the saturation constants AND on B-058's threshold constants so a future B-058 retune doesn't silently drift this emitter's strength meaning. `_detect_episode_starts` pinned for quiet series (no onsets), single-episode-multi-day (one onset at first firing day), multiple episodes with quiet days between (one onset per episode), and the first-row-firing edge case. `_build_event` pins the canonical bearish event shape (asset, asset_class, horizon, source, signal_kind, direction, ts, source_ref, payload contents including thresholds), source_ref uses chain-not-asset (so multiple chains pinning the same asset would stay distinguishable), and BTC excluded from `CHAIN_TO_CRYPTO_SYMBOL` per B-058 design. Orchestrator test mocks `db.ingest_run` + `load_aligned_series` + `engineer_features` + `emit_signals_bulk` to verify per-chain dispatch, episode detection, and the `chains_skipped_no_data` / `chains_skipped_no_watchlist` counters. Tests 1065 → 1095 (+30), ruff clean, full suite green (1095 tests, 33 DB-integration skips). **Companion docs update** in `cross-source-signals.md`: emitter status section updated to "four of seven", new `tvl_drawdown` paragraph covering the episode model, the strength derivation, the horizon routing, and the live 2018-crash findings; references list and "remaining emitters" count updated. **B-098 unblocked**: the relative-strength emitter follow-up is the natural next item — once it lands, the `crypto_tvl_stress_combo` rule becomes fireable and the engine produces its first real crypto stack. Filed in B-064's follow-up section of the backlog. **Not shipped (deferred follow-ups)**: BTC benchmark for stack_backtest's crypto-side abnormal returns (filed under B-102's deferred items; needs a coinbase/coingecko price-load path); sleeve-specific tuning (the `crypto_tvl_stress_combo` rule today is scoped to `crypto:core` only — a future `crypto_tvl_stress_combo_tactical` could pair `tvl_drawdown` events on SUI with relative-strength on the tactical sleeve with possibly looser thresholds).
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/experiments/emitters/tvl_drawdown_emitter.py` (301 lines: EMITTER_SOURCE/RUN_TAG/ENDPOINT constants, `CHAIN_TO_CRYPTO_SYMBOL` map, three named saturation-range constants, `EmitResult` dataclass with episodes_emitted + chains_skipped_no_data + chains_skipped_no_watchlist counters, `_feature_ts` UTC-midnight conversion, `_horizon_for_symbol` watchlist-sleeve lookup, `_normalized_excess` sign-aware threshold-excess helper, `_strength_from_features` mean-of-three aggregator, `_detect_episode_starts` chronological transition walker, `_build_event` per-onset event builder with thresholds in payload, `emit_recent_drawdown_stress` orchestrator wrapped in meta.ingest_runs with in-Python `since` filtering, argparse with `_parse_date`-backed --since/--until + --json). `src/genkei/experiments/emitters/__init__.py` (registers `tvl_drawdown_emitter`; updated module docstring to call out the equity vs crypto split). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (RECURRING_ENDPOINTS["signal_emitter"] gains `tvl_drawdown` alongside the three equity emitters). `src/genkei/data/signal_rules.yml` (pre-staged `crypto_tvl_stress_combo` rule pairing `tvl_drawdown` + `relative_strength` on `crypto:core`). `.github/workflows/defillama-daily.yml` (chains the emitter after normalize with explanatory comment on the rolling window). `docs/experiments/cross-source-signals.md` (emitter status updated to 4 of 7; new tvl_drawdown paragraph with live 2018-crash findings; references list extended). `tests/experiments/test_tvl_drawdown_emitter.py` (515 lines, 26 tests across NormalizedExcess / StrengthFromFeatures / FeatureTs / HorizonForSymbol / DetectEpisodeStarts / BuildEvent / EmitOrchestrator classes). `docs/backlog.md` (B-095 retired). Tests 1065 → 1095 (+30), ruff clean, full suite green. Phase 6 emitter coverage advances from 3 → 4 of 7; B-098 (relative-strength emitter) is the natural next item — pair with this to unlock the engine's first crypto stack.

### ~~B-098 — Wire the relative-strength emitter into signal_events~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-01 (branch relative-strength-emitter, commit `b9419b8`)
- **Outcome:** **Second crypto-side source live** — the pair that finally closes the engine's crypto-side `min_distinct_sources ≥ 2` gate. Same inflection B-093 played for equity-core after B-064; **the engine now fires real crypto stacks**. For each watchlist crypto asset (ethereum / solana / chainlink / sui / pyth-network / render-token), walks daily and computes the trailing 30-day return vs BTC (the fixed crypto-market benchmark, crypto's analog of SPY for equity rules). Detects state crossings using a three-state machine (`laggard ≤ −15pp` / `neutral` / `leader ≥ +15pp`). Same episode-onset model as `tvl_drawdown_emitter`: emit ONE event per state-onset (transition from neutral or opposite-state INTO laggard / leader) and skip continued in-state days. Transitions back into neutral are silent — the "stress lifted" implicit signal is captured by the next opposite-direction crossing. State machine implemented as `_detect_crossings(daily, asset)` with a `prev_state` watermark, mirroring the `prev_fired` pattern in `tvl_drawdown_emitter`. **Strength** = `min(abs(rel_strength_pct) / STRENGTH_SATURATION_PP, 1.0)` where saturation magnitude lives in a named module constant. **Saturation point was tuned from live data**: first pass used 30pp (matching tvl_drawdown's 30pp ranges), but the live backfill against 2016-present coinbase data showed a real ETH 2018 stress episode landed at score 1.36 — just below the rule's `min_score=1.5` (laggard strength 0.58 + TVL strength 0.78). Crypto rel-strength magnitudes during real episodes cluster in the 15-25pp range (crypto routinely sees ±20%+ moves), so a 30pp saturation discounts threshold-edge crossings excessively. Re-tuning to 20pp gives ±15pp threshold-edge → strength 0.75 and ±20pp saturation → strength 1.0, which lets a real stress episode actually fire a stack while still gating noise out (sub-threshold crossings still don't emit at all). The retune is documented in the module's strength-constant docstring so a future tuner sees the reasoning rather than re-discovering it. **Direction** = `bearish` for `laggard_crossing`, `bullish` for `leader_crossing`. Symmetric emission costs almost nothing extra and gives future bullish-direction crypto rules (none today) immediate event coverage. **Source / signal_kind** match the pre-staged `crypto_tvl_stress_combo` rule in `signal_rules.yml`: `source='relative_strength'`, `signal_kind='laggard_crossing'`. **`source_ref = "<coingecko_id>:BTC:30d:<crossing_iso>"`** — natural identifier of the crossing; combined with the UNIQUE key on `(asset, ts, source, signal_kind, source_ref, horizon)` makes re-runs no-ops and lets a future emitter run with a different peer / window without colliding. **Horizon** routing follows the asset's watchlist sleeve (ethereum/solana/chainlink at `crypto:core`, sui/pyth-network/render-token at `crypto:tactical`). Same sleeve-derived horizon pattern the other crypto-side emitter uses. **BTC is the fixed peer**, excluded from the asset universe — its rel-strength vs itself is zero by definition; same exclusion principle as `tvl_drawdown_emitter`'s BTC exclusion (different reason — there BTC's TVL is mostly wrapped + Lightning + Stacks; here BTC IS the benchmark). **Loader**: pulls full available coinbase.candles series via `_load_price_series(product)` regardless of `--since` (the rel-strength window needs ≥30 days of trailing history; the crossing detector needs the prior day's state). `--since` is applied in-Python to the emission window after crossings are detected, matching the tvl_drawdown / crowding emitter precedent. **Workflow integration**: chained into `coinbase-daily.yml` after the normalize step (so the emitter reads freshly-landed `coinbase.candles`) with a 90-day rolling `--since` window — narrower than tvl_drawdown's 120d because crypto rel-strength crossings are more frequent (487 historical vs 3 TVL-stress episodes) and the rolling window only needs to absorb cron-failure gaps. Registered in `RECURRING_ENDPOINTS["signal_emitter"]` alongside the other four emitters. **Live homelab backfill against 2016-present data** produced 487 crossings across all six crypto watchlist assets: ETH 76 laggard + 75 leader, LINK 80 laggard + 47 leader, SOL 42 laggard + 31 leader, SUI 36 laggard + 23 leader, RENDER 27 laggard + 22 leader, PYTH 17 laggard + 11 leader. Distribution is reasonable: each asset sees a few crossings per year, consistent with the threshold being narrow enough that real episodes register but wide enough that minor noise doesn't fire daily. **THE ENGINE'S FIRST REAL CRYPTO STACK FIRES**: 2018-08-04 ethereum bearish `crypto_tvl_stress_combo` with score 1.65, combining the 2018-07-16 ethereum `laggard_crossing` (ETH down ~17pp vs BTC over the trailing 30 days, strength 0.87) with the 2018-08-04 `tvl_drawdown_stress` (strength 0.78). This is the canonical ETH ICO-crash signal — on-chain demand contraction AND price losing relative leadership at the same time. The other two ETH TVL stress episodes (2018-12-13 and 2018-12-29) don't pair into stacks because the nearest ETH laggard crossings landed 33-49 days later — just past the rule's 30-day window. That's correct behavior, not a bug: the rule requires *coincident* stress, not eventual stress. **Tests** (`tests/experiments/test_relative_strength_emitter.py`, 474 lines, 29 tests across 8 classes): `_state_for` pinned for None / below-laggard / above-leader / between-thresholds. `_strength_from_rel_strength` pinned at threshold edge (0.75), saturation point (1.0), above saturation (1.0 clamp), below-threshold magnitude (0.5 at -10pp), plus pins on the named constants AND on the canonical peer config so a future re-tune doesn't silently drift the meaning. `_date_ts` pinned for UTC midnight conversion. `compute_daily_relative_strength` pinned on empty, flat (zero rel-strength), asset-outperforming, peer-outperforming. `_detect_crossings` pinned for no-crossings-in-neutral, single-laggard-onset, laggard-then-leader sequence, neutral-only emits nothing, first-row-firing edge case, return-to-neutral-does-not-emit. `_build_event` pins the bearish laggard event shape (asset, asset_class, horizon, source, signal_kind, direction, ts, source_ref, payload with thresholds), the bullish leader event direction, and the source_ref including peer+window so a future emitter run can vary either without collision. `_crypto_assets` pins BTC exclusion and sleeve passthrough. `EmitOrchestratorTests` mocks `db.ingest_run` + `_load_price_series` + `compute_daily_relative_strength` + `emit_signals_bulk` to verify per-asset dispatch, the no-peer-data short-circuit, the per-asset-no-data counter, and the laggard+leader symmetric emission. Tests 1091 → 1120 (+29), ruff clean, full suite green (1120 tests, 33 DB-integration skips). **Companion docs update** in `cross-source-signals.md`: emitter status updated to "five of seven", new `relative_strength` paragraph covering the state machine, the strength derivation, the live-data-tuned saturation, and the engine's first crypto stack finding; references list extended; "remaining emitters" count reduced from three (B-096/B-097/B-098) to two (B-096/B-097). **Phase 6 milestone achieved**: the engine is now wired for both equity AND crypto sleeves, with all currently-staged rules fireable. Stack-outcome backtesting on crypto stacks is the natural follow-up — needs a BTC benchmark in coinbase.candles (B-102 deferred this) to enable `--benchmark BTC` on `genkei backtest` for crypto-asset stacks; with only one crypto stack today the backtest would be n=1 anyway, but the framework is ready.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/experiments/emitters/relative_strength_emitter.py` (484 lines: EMITTER_SOURCE/RUN_TAG/ENDPOINT constants, PEER_PRODUCT/PEER_SYMBOL fixed-benchmark constants, WINDOW_DAYS=30 matching the pre-staged rule, LAGGARD/LEADER threshold constants (±15pp), STRENGTH_SATURATION_PP=20 with extensive docstring on the live-data tuning, `Crossing` and `EmitResult` frozen dataclasses, `_state_for` three-state classifier, `_strength_from_rel_strength` saturating-ramp helper, `_date_ts` UTC-midnight conversion, `_load_price_series` coinbase.candles loader, `compute_daily_relative_strength` per-day rel-strength walker, `_detect_crossings` state-machine onset detector, `_build_event` per-crossing event builder with thresholds in payload, `_crypto_assets` watchlist filter (excludes BTC peer), `emit_recent_crossings` orchestrator wrapped in meta.ingest_runs with in-Python `since` filtering + the empty-watchlist and no-peer-data short-circuits, argparse with `_parse_date`-backed --since/--until + --json). `src/genkei/experiments/emitters/__init__.py` (registers `relative_strength_emitter`; updated module docstring to call out the equity+crypto-fully-wired milestone). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (RECURRING_ENDPOINTS["signal_emitter"] gains `relative_strength` alongside the other four emitters with comment noting it closes the crypto-stack min_distinct_sources gate). `.github/workflows/coinbase-daily.yml` (chains the emitter after normalize with explanatory comment on the 90-day rolling window vs tvl_drawdown's 120d). `docs/experiments/cross-source-signals.md` (emitter status updated to 5 of 7; new relative_strength paragraph with state machine + live tuning + first-crypto-stack finding; run-the-emitter example block extended; references list extended; remaining-emitters count reduced). `tests/experiments/test_relative_strength_emitter.py` (474 lines, 29 tests across StateFor / StrengthFromRelStrength / DateTs / ComputeDailyRelativeStrength / DetectCrossings / BuildEvent / CryptoAssets / EmitOrchestrator classes). `docs/backlog.md` (B-098 retired). Tests 1091 → 1120 (+29), ruff clean, full suite green (1120 tests, 33 skipped locally because Docker absent). Phase 6 emitter coverage advances from 4 → 5 of 7; engine now wired for both equity AND crypto sleeves with all currently-staged rules fireable. Next high-leverage items: B-103 (per-asset rule tuning informed by backtest findings, especially the CRM anti-signal pattern), B-100 (live correlator-side benchmark adjustment), B-096/B-097 (macro and watchlist-scoring overlay emitters).

### ~~B-100 — Correlator: SPY/BTC benchmark adjustment on stack scores~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-01 (branch correlator-benchmark-adjust, commit `8aff6c4`)
- **Outcome:** **Live counterpart to B-102's backtest abnormal-return column.** `genkei signals` now shows a `vs_bench` column by default that reports `asset_return_pct − benchmark_return_pct` over each stack's own window (`window_start → window_end`). Equity stacks compare vs SPY (from `yahoo.candles`); crypto stacks compare vs BTC (from `coinbase.candles`). Routing is automatic by `asset_class`; per-class overrides via `--equity-benchmark` / `--crypto-benchmark`; `--no-benchmark` suppresses the column entirely. **Design choice: presentation layer, not correlator-score mutation.** The acceptance criterion explicitly left room to modify `detect_stacks`' score directly. We chose to leave the correlator's math invariant and surface the benchmark context as an *additional* column. Two reasons documented in `signal_benchmark.py`'s docstring: (1) the B-102 backtest already proved the honest read is "show both raw and benchmark-adjusted; let the reader weigh against the rule direction" — collapsing them into one score would lose the framing; (2) asset-class-aware routing means equity and crypto stacks get different comparators (SPY vs BTC), and a score mutation would have to pick or average them, losing per-class explicitness. **New module `src/genkei/experiments/signal_benchmark.py`** (375 lines): `StackBenchmarkContext` frozen dataclass parallel-indexed to the input stack list (so callers don't have to mutate the immutable `Stack`); `compute_stack_benchmark_contexts(stacks, *, equity_benchmark, crypto_benchmark)` orchestrator that buckets stacks by `(asset_class, ticker)` and loads each unique series exactly once across the union of stack windows; `compute_window_return_pct(prices, start, end)` helper using bisect-based close-on-or-before semantics matching B-057's price-anchor pattern; per-asset-class loaders `_load_yahoo_series` (queries `yahoo.candles` for equity-side assets and SPY/QQQ/IWM benchmarks) and `_load_coinbase_series` (queries `coinbase.candles` for crypto-side assets and BTC benchmark via watchlist `coinbase_product` routing). The `ASSET_CLASS_BENCHMARK_SOURCES` dict in the module exports the asset-class → loader-source mapping so a future asset class lands a single entry rather than threading through the orchestrator. **Window math**: returns are computed as `close_on_or_before(window_end) - close_on_or_before(window_start)`, percentage points. Zero-width windows (`window_start == window_end`, all events on one day) yield `None` rather than silently `0` so the column reads honestly. **No-lookahead**: every input is observable at `window_end` by construction — the stack itself can't be detected until then. Same guarantee the B-101/B-102 backtest's anchor-date logic provides. **CLI surface**: `signals_cmd` gains `--benchmark/--no-benchmark` toggle (defaults ON), `--equity-benchmark SPY`, `--crypto-benchmark BTC`. Renderer adds a `vs_bench` column when benchmark contexts are available with a footer explaining the sign convention (positive = asset out-performed in window; reader interprets against rule direction). JSON mode embeds a `benchmark` sub-object on each stack with `ticker`, `asset_return_pct`, `benchmark_return_pct`, `abnormal_pct`. **Live findings on the homelab top-10 stacks**: the column instantly reframes the bearish stacks in exactly the way B-102's backtest predicted at scale. AMD `deterioration_stack` (2026-02-17) at `vs_bench` −20.02pp is the only top-10 stack with meaningful market-relative weakness — the genuine bearish signal. AMZN `broad_exit` (2026-05-03) at +29.81pp, HOOD `deterioration_stack` (2026-04-15) at +14.33pp, AVGO `deterioration_stack` (2026-04-09) at +14.31pp, GOOG/GOOGL all positive — all "trim toward index" cases where the stack fired during a window the asset still beat SPY. NVDA at −1.32pp and HOOD (2026-03-04) at −5.11pp show marginal underperformance. The framing makes the operational read at decision time the same one B-102 produced retrospectively: bearish stacks are *not* shorts; they're rebalancing signals on the equity-core sleeve. **Tests** (`tests/experiments/test_signal_benchmark.py` 480 lines, 27 tests; `tests/cli/test_signals.py` +4 tests in new `BenchmarkColumnTests`; total +32 across the branch): `compute_window_return_pct` pinned for empty / zero-width / negative-window / basic-positive / basic-negative / start-before-series / start-price-zero / close-on-or-before semantics. `_benchmark_for` pinned for equity / crypto / unknown class plus the documented-defaults constants pin. `_dt_to_date` aware + naive datetime handling. `compute_stack_benchmark_contexts` mocked across equity-only / crypto-only / unknown-class short-circuit / load-once-per-ticker dedup / mixed-asset-class routing / benchmark override propagation. CLI: default-renders-vs_bench, `--no-benchmark` suppresses (and asserts the compute is NOT called), per-class overrides propagate to `compute_stack_benchmark_contexts`, JSON includes the benchmark sub-object. The three pre-existing `EndToEndTests` tests (which only patch `query_events`) got `--no-benchmark` to keep them DB-free without losing their original intent. Tests 1119 → 1151 (+32), ruff clean, full suite green (1151 tests, 33 DB-integration skips). **Companion docs update** in `cross-source-signals.md`: new "Live benchmark-adjusted column (B-100)" subsection covering the design choice (presentation-layer vs score mutation), the per-asset-class routing, the live findings showing the bearish-stack reframing, and the cross-reference to B-102's backtest framing; references list gains `signal_benchmark.py`. **Not shipped (deferred follow-ups)**: smart per-rule benchmark selection (could let a rule declare its own preferred comparator — e.g. tech-only rules vs QQQ — instead of relying on the per-class default); the column doesn't load benchmark data when running with `--events` (the raw-event dump bypasses the correlator entirely); BTC-benchmark backtest for crypto-side stacks (still gated on `genkei backtest`'s crypto-side support — n=1 stack today means backtest is anecdotal anyway, file when populated). **Phase 6 milestone**: the engine is now wired (5 of 7 emitters), measured (B-101 backtest), and benchmark-adjusted at decision time (B-100). Remaining items reshape what's high-leverage: B-103 (per-asset rule tuning for the CRM anti-signal), B-096 (macro regime overlay), B-097 (watchlist scoring crossings), or pivoting to actually use the engine via `/research`.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/experiments/signal_benchmark.py` (new file, 375 lines: `StackBenchmarkContext` frozen dataclass; `DEFAULT_EQUITY_BENCHMARK`/`DEFAULT_CRYPTO_BENCHMARK` constants; `ASSET_CLASS_BENCHMARK_SOURCES` future-extension map; `_dt_to_date` UTC normalizer; `compute_window_return_pct` bisect-based close-on-or-before helper; `_load_yahoo_series` / `_load_coinbase_series` per-source loaders; `_coinbase_product_lookup` + `_coinbase_product_for` watchlist product mapping; `_load_asset_series` / `_load_benchmark_series` per-class routers; `compute_stack_benchmark_contexts` orchestrator with load-once-per-ticker dedup; `_benchmark_for` class → ticker resolver). `src/genkei/cli/signals.py` (`--benchmark/--no-benchmark` toggle defaulting ON; `--equity-benchmark` / `--crypto-benchmark` per-class overrides; `_stack_to_dict` accepts optional `benchmark` arg producing nested benchmark sub-object; `_format_stacks_human` conditional `vs_bench` column + footer explaining sign convention; orchestrator threads `compute_stack_benchmark_contexts` after `detect_stacks` when benchmark enabled). `docs/experiments/cross-source-signals.md` (new "Live benchmark-adjusted column" subsection with design choice, per-asset-class routing, live findings, and B-102 cross-reference; references list extended). `tests/experiments/test_signal_benchmark.py` (new file, 480 lines, 27 tests across `ComputeWindowReturnPct` / `BenchmarkFor` / `CoinbaseProductFor` / `DtToDate` / `ComputeStackBenchmarkContexts` / `StackBenchmarkContextDataclass` / `LoaderSql` classes). `tests/cli/test_signals.py` (new `BenchmarkColumnTests` class with 4 tests pinning default-on rendering, `--no-benchmark` suppression, per-class override propagation, and JSON benchmark sub-object; three existing `EndToEndTests` tests adjusted with `--no-benchmark` to keep them DB-free). `docs/backlog.md` (B-100 retired). Tests 1119 → 1151 (+32), ruff clean, full suite green (1151 tests, 33 skipped locally because Docker absent). Phase 6 closes another loop: the engine now produces benchmark-adjusted reads at decision time, not only post-hoc in the backtest.

### ~~B-031 — CFTC Commitments of Traders ingester~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-02 (branch cftc-cot, commits `8f3dc8b` + `acd2896` + `56394d4` + `6d7c1a6` + `e088bc5` + `2f94441` + `51878ae` + `f0d4fd0` + `019104c`)
- **Outcome:** Institutional-positioning fact stream the 2026-06-02 ETH / SOL / SUI research sessions repeatedly named as the single most valuable missing input on crypto-core stack reads. COT directly answers "are Asset Managers and Leveraged Funds net-long or net-short BTC / ETH futures?" — and weekly Tuesday-snapshot / Friday-release cadence is fast enough to inform position-sizing decisions, slow enough to avoid noise. **Free public Socrata API** at `publicreporting.cftc.gov/resource/` — no key, no auth, no rate-limit headaches. Two datasets routed by report type: `gpe5-46if` (Traders in Financial Futures — BTC, ETH, ES; has the Asset Manager / Leveraged Funds breakdown) and `72hh-3qpy` (Disaggregated — GC, CL; has the Managed Money / Swap Dealer / Producer Merchant breakdown). **New schema** `cftc.cot_reports` (migration `c9a4e7b21f08`): plain table (not hypertable) keyed on `(report_date, market_code, trader_category)` with `report_type` discriminator (`tff` / `disaggregated` / `legacy`) CHECK-enforced; `long_positions` / `short_positions` / `spreading_positions` as `BIGINT` (CFTC publishes whole contracts, no decimal component); volume estimate ~80k rows steady-state at 5 markets × 5 categories × weekly × 30 years stays well within plain-PG range, same call `sec.form13f_holdings` (B-080) made. Two query-shape indexes: `(market_code, report_date DESC)` for per-market time series and `(trader_category, report_date DESC)` for cross-market category sweeps. **Watchlist gains `cot_markets:`** — typed `CotMarketEntry` (`code` / `symbol` / `name` / `report_type` / `sleeve` / `rationale`) with v1 seed covering the 5 markets the research sessions cited as load-bearing: BTC (`133741`), ETH (`146021`), ES (`13874A`, S&P 500 — macro-spine equity comparator), GC (`088691`, gold — risk-off / inflation-hedge cross-check), CL (`067411`, WTI crude — energy / inflation context for the macro-regime label). Adding a new market is a watchlist edit, zero code changes. **New collector `src/genkei/ingest/cftc.py`** (463 lines): per-market URL builder applies the Socrata `$where=cftc_contract_market_code='...'` filter plus an optional `report_date_as_yyyy_mm_dd >= 'YYYY-MM-DD'` clause for incremental cutoff, with `$limit=50000` sized well above any single market's realistic per-history row count (~1500 rows for 30y weekly) to avoid pagination logic v1. Each upstream weekly row fans out into 5 trader-category rows via the per-report-type `_CategoryFields` table mapping Socrata column triples (long / short / spread) to canonical `trader_category` values — TFF has the `dealer_intermediary` / `asset_manager` / `leveraged_funds` / `other_reportables` / `non_reportable` breakdown; Disaggregated has `producer_merchant` / `swap_dealer` / `managed_money` / `other_reportables` / `non_reportable`. The mapping captures the actual upstream column quirks, including the documented double-underscore in `swap__positions_spread_all` (pinned in unit tests so a CFTC schema rename would surface loudly rather than silently landing as NULL). **Incremental mode** picks up from `max(report_date)` per market and re-fetches the latest week itself (upserts are idempotent on the natural-key PK so a duplicate write is harmless); **backfill mode** pulls everything. **Skip semantics**: a row where both long and short are missing for a category is dropped (would land as net=NULL — meaningless); rows with no parseable `report_date_as_yyyy_mm_dd` are dropped; a non-array payload raises (Socrata contract changed). **CLI `genkei cot`**: slice by `--market` (symbol or CFTC code), `--trader-category` with caller-friendly aliases (`hedge_funds` / `lev_money` / `leveraged_funds` all resolve to the same canonical column; same for `asset_mgr` / `asset_manager` / `institutional`, etc.), `--since` / `--until` window. Computes `net_position` (long − short) in the output so the headline "are leveraged funds net-long or net-short" query is one command. `--list-markets` enumerates configured watchlist markets; `--json` for the agent. Subtle Python 3.9 / Typer interaction discovered + documented inline: `from __future__ import annotations` combined with `Annotated[bool, typer.Option("--json", ...)]` causes Typer to lose the custom option name and auto-derive `--json-out` from the Python identifier instead — same latent bug already lives in `eight_k_impact.py` and renders as `--json-output` there; worth a separate fix. **Wired into watchlist health + drift canary**: `PRIMARY_TABLES` gains `cftc.cot_reports`; `RECURRING_ENDPOINTS` adds `cftc/collect`; `schema_drift.SCHEMA_SPECS` adds a spec for the `cot_<report_type>_<market_code>` raw-blob pattern keyed on the two load-bearing upstream fields (`report_date_as_yyyy_mm_dd`, `cftc_contract_market_code`). **CI**: new `cftc-weekly.yml` daily 22:00 UTC cron on the self-hosted Beelink runner. Actual CFTC publication is Friday ~20:30 UTC; the daily cron is cheap insurance — on non-Friday days the upsert is a no-op because no new `report_date` exists. `workflow_dispatch` for manual backfill kicks. **Tests** (`tests/ingest/test_cftc.py` 268 lines, 28 tests; `tests/common/test_watchlist.py` +6 tests in new `CotMarketsParserTests`): URL construction (TFF vs Disaggregated dataset routing; `$where` filter shape with + without `--since`); `_coerce_int` (string digits / float strings / blank / garbage / native int / bool rejection); `_parse_report_date` (Socrata floating timestamp + bare date fallback); `_categories_for` (per-report-type category set + unsupported-type raise); the spec's canary `parse_market_rows` cases — fan-out to 5 categories, the leveraged-funds long/short/spread/net math the spec calls out, asset-manager column routing, non-reportable null-spread, the double-underscore swap-spread column, multi-week fan-out, skip-rows-missing-data, non-array-raises, watchlist-name fallback. Cross-source registry pins in `test_schema_drift` and `test_watchlist_cmd` updated to include `cftc`. **Tests 1151 → 1185 (+34), ruff clean, full suite green** (1185 tests, 33 DB-integration skips). **Backfill not yet run** — the GH Actions workflow will fire the first incremental on the next 22:00 UTC tick; for a kick-start backfill the user can run `gh workflow run cftc-weekly.yml` or `python3 -m genkei.ingest.cftc --backfill` on the homelab terminal. **Phase 1 of the institutional-flow cohort** (B-031 / B-104 / B-105 / B-106): COT gives the weekly *who's-long-short* breakdown across the curated 5 markets. B-104 (CME daily OI) will be the high-frequency / lower-detail companion on the same crypto markets; B-105 (Farside spot ETF flow) closes the spot side; B-106 (Etherscan whale flow) closes the on-chain side. Today's research decisions can already start consulting `genkei cot --market BTC --trader-category leveraged_funds --since 2024-01-01` after the first backfill.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260602_create_cftc_cot_reports.py` (new file: `cftc` schema; `cot_reports` table with `(report_date, market_code, trader_category)` PK; `report_type` CHECK in `('tff','disaggregated','legacy')`; market_code + category indexes). `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml` (new `cot_markets:` section with 5 entries). `src/genkei/common/watchlist.py` (new `CotMarketEntry` frozen dataclass; `Watchlist.cot_markets` list; `Watchlist.find_cot_market(symbol_or_code)` lookup; YAML parsing path with int-code stringification, unknown-`report_type` drop, duplicate-code dedupe). `src/genkei/ingest/cftc.py` (new file, 463 lines: `TFF_DATASET_ID` / `DISAGGREGATED_DATASET_ID` constants; `TFF_CATEGORIES` / `DISAGGREGATED_CATEGORIES` mappings; `build_market_url` Socrata URL builder; `_coerce_int` / `_parse_report_date` helpers; `parse_market_rows` row-fan-out; `latest_report_date_for_market` resume helper; `collect` orchestrator with backfill / incremental / since modes and partial-failure recording via `db.record_partial_endpoints`). `src/genkei/cli/cot.py` (new file, 327 lines: `_CATEGORY_ALIASES` alias→canonical table; `_resolve_category` / `_resolve_market` / `_query_cot` / `_format_human` / `_format_markets_human` / `_fmt` helpers; `cot_cmd` Typer command with `--market`, `--trader-category`, `--since` / `--until`, `--limit`, `--json`, `--list-markets`, `--config`). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` (cot module import; `genkei cot` command registration; surface-listing docstring entry). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (`PRIMARY_TABLES["cftc"] = ["cftc.cot_reports"]`; `RECURRING_ENDPOINTS["cftc"] = ["collect"]`). `src/genkei/common/schema_drift.py` (new `EndpointSchema` for `cot_<report_type>_<market_code>` raw blobs). `.github/workflows/cftc-weekly.yml` (new daily 22:00 UTC cron on `[self-hosted, beelink]`). `tests/ingest/test_cftc.py` (new file, 268 lines, 28 tests). `tests/common/test_watchlist.py` (new `CotMarketsParserTests` class with 6 tests). `tests/common/test_schema_drift.py` + `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` (registry-pin sets extended with `cftc`). `docs/backlog.md` (B-031 retired). Tests 1151 → 1185 (+34), ruff clean, full suite green (1185 tests, 33 DB-integration skips). Phase 7 institutional-flow cohort: first pillar landed; B-104 / B-105 / B-106 unblocked.

### ~~B-105 — Spot ETF activity tracker (Yahoo-pivoted from Farside)~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-03 (branch spot-etf-flow, commits `2c84932` + `2a6b770` + `84f2d3f` + `39ed8e6` + `9318c9a` + `d36e05d`)
- **Outcome:** Closes the *magnitude* half of the institutional-flow gap the 2026-06-02 ETH / SOL / SUI research sessions named, via a forced pivot from the original Farside-scrape spec. **Discovery + pivot (the bulk of this branch's value):** kickoff probes confirmed both Farside (`farside.co.uk/btc/`) and SoSoValue (the spec's named fallback) now return HTTP 403 + Cloudflare interactive JS challenge (`"Just a moment..."`) to any scripted request regardless of User-Agent / headers. Both publishers serve daily ETF flow data behind enterprise Cloudflare bot-protection that requires real browser JS execution to pass. Headless-browser bypass was rejected (1GB binary, perpetual cat-and-mouse with Cloudflare updates, against the spec's "polite scraping" principle); paid alternatives (Polygon, Coin Metrics, Kaiko) rejected per CLAUDE.md's free/open-sources stance. **Pivot to Yahoo Finance** — verified live against IBIT that Yahoo's chart endpoint (the same one B-092 / B-102 already use for SPY/QQQ/IWM benchmarks) serves daily OHLCV cleanly for every US-listed spot crypto ETF. Yahoo's `quoteSummary` endpoint (which would give shares-outstanding for true net-flow computation) requires a cookie/crumb auth dance and was not adopted — the auth fragility wasn't worth the complexity for v1. **Honest scope reduction:** v1 lands daily OHLCV per ETF and the CLI delivers `sum(volume x close) = daily-dollar-volume per asset` as a *magnitude proxy* for institutional activity. NOT signed net flow. The output column is explicitly labeled `dollar_volume_usd_mm` (not `net_flow_usd_mm`) and the human-format renderer carries a footer making that explicit so a future researcher doesn't misread the column as creations-minus-redemptions. True signed net flow filed separately as **B-107** with an SEC-EDGAR-investigation-required scope. **Watchlist gains `etf_tickers:`** — typed `EtfTickerEntry` (`ticker` / `name` / `asset` / `issuer` / `sleeve` / `launch_date` / `rationale`) with v1 seed covering 22 US spot crypto ETFs: 12 BTC ETFs (IBIT, FBTC, BITB, ARKB, BTCO, EZBC, BRRR, HODL, BTCW, GBTC, DEFI, BTC) and 10 ETH ETFs (ETHA, ETHB, FETH, ETHW, TETH, ETHV, QETH, EZET, ETHE, ETH; the original cohort launched 2024-07-23, with ETHB listed on Nasdaq on 2026-03-12). The original cohort was verified live against the Yahoo chart endpoint as a kickoff smoke check; ETHB is documented by BlackRock/Nasdaq and will be picked up by the same Yahoo path. Each entry tagged `asset: BTC|ETH` to route per-asset aggregations. `launch_date` kept as ISO string so downstream code doesn't have to special-case YAML date typing. All entries sleeve=tactical because spot-ETF activity is a tactical-signal input, not a long-term hold. **`Watchlist.find_etf_ticker(ticker)` + `Watchlist.etfs_for_asset(asset)`** helpers make per-asset lookups trivial — both case-insensitive. **`genkei.ingest.yahoo` extension** — single-loop addition to `load_equities()` so the existing daily Yahoo cron picks up all 22 ETFs alongside the 28 equity research targets + 3 benchmarks (53 targets total now). No new ingest module, no new schema, no new GH workflow — the same `yahoo-daily.yml` cron will land the data in the existing `yahoo.candles` table on the next 12:15 UTC tick. Collision check in the loader catches accidental equity/ETF ticker reuse. **`genkei etf-flows` CLI** (`src/genkei/cli/etf_flows.py`, 364 lines) — query layer over `yahoo.candles` filtered by the watchlist's per-ETF asset tag. `--asset BTC` (or `bitcoin`) / `--asset ETH` (or `ethereum` / `ether`) routes via `_ASSET_ALIASES`; `--by-ticker` splits per-ETF instead of asset-level aggregate; `--since` / `--until` date window; `--list-etfs` enumerates configured tickers; `--json` for the agent. Aggregate output is `sum(volume x close) / 1e6` in USD millions plus a `reporting_etfs` count so a reader can spot a partial-coverage day (e.g. only 8/12 BTC ETFs reported — likely an ingest hiccup, not a market signal). Output table footer explicitly says *"magnitude proxy for institutional activity, NOT signed net flow"* — the honesty constraint baked into the UI. Reused `from __future__ import annotations` omission learned from B-031's cot.py to keep Typer's `--json` flag working on Python 3.9. **Tests** (`tests/common/test_watchlist.py` +9 in new `EtfTickersParserTests` class; `tests/cli/test_etf_flows.py` 13 new tests in 6 classes): watchlist loader pins typed-entry construction, case-insensitive ticker normalization, unknown-asset drop (v1 only supports BTC + ETH so a hypothetical `asset: SOL` row gets silently dropped — pinned), duplicate-ticker dedupe (first wins, case-insensitive), `find_etf_ticker` / `etfs_for_asset` routing, empty-section default. CLI helpers pin asset alias resolution (BTC / bitcoin / ETH / ethereum / ether all canonical), horizon tag constants (`etf:crypto:btc` / `etf:crypto:eth`), `_tag_rows` non-mutation of input, human aggregate renderer (header + disclaimer + numeric formatting + null-column handling), per-ticker renderer. `_ASSET_ALIASES` registry pinned for lowercase keys (a future contributor adding mixed-case would silently break the `.strip().lower()` lookup). **Tests 1185 → 1208 (+23), ruff clean, full suite green** (1208 tests, 33 DB-integration skips). **No backfill kicked off yet**: the existing `yahoo-daily.yml` cron will pick up the new ETF tickers on the next 12:15 UTC tick (incrementally — daily-mode). For a one-shot full history backfill, run `gh workflow run yahoo-daily.yml` or `python3 -m genkei.ingest.yahoo --backfill` from the homelab terminal — pulls each ETF from its Yahoo first-trade-date forward (Jan / Mar / Jul 2024 for BTC ETFs, Jul 2024 and Mar 2026 for ETH ETFs). **Filed as follow-up**: B-107 (Spot ETF true net flow via SEC EDGAR) captures the signed-direction question this branch deliberately deferred. Investigation-required scope; refines the institutional-flow trio when picked up. **Phase 2 of the institutional-flow cohort** (B-031 ✓ / B-104 blocked / B-105 ✓ / B-106 still open): COT (weekly position breakdown) + ETF activity (daily dollar volume) are now both flowing. Next research session on ETH / SOL can call `genkei cot --market ETH --trader-category leveraged_funds --since 2025-01-01` and `genkei etf-flows --asset ETH --since 2025-01-01` together for the institutional read.
- **Evidence:** `docs/backlog.md` (B-105 retired; B-107 filed for the deferred signed-flow path). `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml` (new `etf_tickers:` section, 22 entries across BTC + ETH). `src/genkei/common/watchlist.py` (new `EtfTickerEntry` frozen dataclass; `Watchlist.etf_tickers` list; `find_etf_ticker` / `etfs_for_asset` helpers; YAML parsing path with ticker normalization, unknown-asset drop, dupe dedupe, ISO-string launch_date coercion). `src/genkei/ingest/yahoo.py` (`load_equities` extended with a third loop over `watchlist.etf_tickers`; docstring updated to cover the third section; collision check). `src/genkei/cli/etf_flows.py` (new file, 364 lines: `_ASSET_ALIASES` map; `_resolve_asset` / `_format_etf_list_human` / `_query_asset_aggregate` / `_query_per_ticker` / `_format_aggregate_human` / `_format_per_ticker_human` / `_horizon_tag` / `_tag_rows` helpers; `etf_flows_cmd` Typer command with `--asset`, `--since` / `--until`, `--limit`, `--by-ticker`, `--json`, `--list-etfs`, `--config`). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` (etf_flows module import; `genkei etf-flows` command registration; surface-listing docstring entry). `tests/common/test_watchlist.py` (new `EtfTickersParserTests` class, 9 tests). `tests/cli/test_etf_flows.py` (new file, 179 lines, 13 tests across 6 classes). Tests 1185 → 1208 (+23), ruff clean. Phase 2 of the institutional-flow cohort closes: COT (B-031) + ETF activity (B-105) flow together.
- **Post-review B-105 fix:** `genkei etf-flows` now carries watchlist ETF launch dates into the SQL layer, clamps each ticker at its spot-ETF launch date, derives `flow_date` from `(ts AT TIME ZONE 'UTC')::date`, and binds `--since` / `--until` as UTC-aware datetimes for `yahoo.candles.ts` TIMESTAMPTZ comparisons. `tests/cli/test_etf_flows.py` includes DB-free regressions for launch-date parsing, aggregate query parameters, and per-ticker query parameters; current branch evidence is 1211 tests total with 33 DB-integration skips.

### ~~B-108 — `genkei stablecoin-flow` CLI subcommand~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-04 (branch stablecoin-flow-cli, commits `64c7eb8` + `5a9f538` + `689df33` + `a7a5508`)
- **Outcome:** Promoted the cross-chain stablecoin-supply signal that yesterday's 2026-06-03 BTC/ETH/SOL comparative research decision named *"the strongest single cross-asset comparative signal in the lake right now"* from an SQL escape hatch to a typed CLI surface. Three modes shipped: **trajectory** (`--chain Ethereum --since 2026-01-01` returns daily supply + rolling 7d / 30d Δ columns so capital-flow direction is one column read), **all-chains compare** (`--all-chains` returns latest-day supply + deltas for every chain above a `--min-supply-b` threshold sorted by current supply — cross-chain rotation visible at a glance), and **by-stablecoin** (`--chain Ethereum --by-stablecoin` splits per-asset USDT / USDC / DAI / etc. on the latest day for single-asset-flow detection). `--list-chains` enumerates all chains with stablecoin data, `--json` for the agent, 21 case-insensitive chain aliases covering the research-critical cohort (eth/ethereum, sol/solana, btc/bitcoin, tron/trx, bsc/bnb, arb/arbitrum, base, polygon/matic, avalanche/avax, plus hyperliquid/aptos/ton/stellar/xrpl). Output column names are honestly chosen — `supply_usd_b` / `delta_7d_usd_b` / `delta_30d_usd_b` are circulating supply + absolute deltas, NOT signed creation/redemption flows from primary sources; the format renderer's footer makes the sign-convention explicit so a future researcher doesn't misread the column. **Critical data-quality discovery during smoke testing** that drove the bulk of the kickoff work: defillama.stablecoins has had **2 distinct `ingest_run_id` rows per (chain, asset, day) from 2026-05-10 onwards** — verified with `SELECT date_trunc('day', ts)::date, COUNT(*), COUNT(DISTINCT ingest_run_id) FROM defillama.stablecoins WHERE chain='Ethereum' AND ts::date IN ('2026-05-09','2026-05-10') GROUP BY 1` (2026-05-09 = 150 rows / 1 run; 2026-05-10 = 305 rows / 2 runs). Without de-duplication, every naive SUM aggregation from 2026-05-10 onwards would double-count. Worked around at query time in every CLI path via `DISTINCT ON (chain, asset_id) ORDER BY ingest_run_id DESC` (kept the latest run per row), and verified post-fix: 2026-05-10 reads $165.86B (smooth +$0.22B day-over-day vs $165.64B prior) instead of the bogus $331B aggregate. Yesterday's research decision was queried via the same SQL shape — its absolute thresholds ($158B / $162B for ETH, $14B / $17B for SOL) should be re-verified against deduplicated data before treating as canonical (today's deduplicated current value for ETH is $159.31B, suggesting yesterday's numbers were already correct OR the bug happened not to affect the days queried). Filed B-109 to fix the upstream ingester / schema — workaround stays as defense-in-depth until the root cause is addressed. **Tests** (`tests/cli/test_stablecoin_flow.py`, 38 tests across 12 classes): chain alias resolution (case-insensitive, strips whitespace, unknown lowercase title-case-through, mixed-case unknowns preserved, empty raises, alias-keys-lowercase invariant), horizon tag construction (multi-word collapses whitespace to underscore), row tagging (non-mutation of input), value coercion (Decimal / int / float / None → JSON-safe float), banked numeric formatter (sign + thousands separator + null-dash), all 4 human-format renderers (empty hints, populated tables, footer-disclaimer presence, null-column handling), mode flag validation, latest-day `--list-chains` SQL, all-chains global latest-day SQL, inclusive default lookback window, module constants (`DEFAULT_TRAJECTORY_DAYS=30`, `DEFAULT_MIN_SUPPLY_B=0.5`, research-critical chains in alias set). DB-touching query paths exercise the dedupe-on-ingest_run_id logic that will be integration-tested when the DB fixture lands; pure helpers covered offline. **Tests 1211 → 1249 (+38), ruff clean, full suite green** (1249 tests, 33 DB-integration skips). **Live findings on first run** confirm yesterday's research direction: ETH stables continue to slide ($159.31B today, −$3.21B over 7d, −$5.80B over 30d — accelerating bear); SOL stables holding ($15.37B, +$0.10B over 7d, −$0.27B over 30d — flat-to-slightly-positive). The trigger conditions from yesterday's decision (ETH below $158B → bear escalation; SOL above $17B → bull confirmation) are now observable in one command: `genkei stablecoin-flow --chain Ethereum --limit 7`. **Phase 3 of the institutional-flow cohort** (B-031 ✓ COT, B-104 blocked CME, B-105 ✓ ETF activity, B-106 open Etherscan whales, B-107 deferred SEC true-flow, **B-108 ✓ stablecoin-flow CLI**, B-109 filed dedupe fix): the third query-side surface lands, completing the on-chain stablecoin-flow read while two off-chain flow paths remain (whale wallets, SEC ETF net flow). Next research session has CLI access to COT (once backfill runs) + ETF activity (once yahoo backfill runs) + stablecoin flow (now); concrete query trio for comparative crypto-core decisions.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/cli/stablecoin_flow.py` (new file, ~646 lines: `_CHAIN_ALIASES` 21-entry map; `_resolve_chain` / `_horizon_tag` / `_tag_rows` / `_to_float` / `_fmt_b` helpers; `_query_chain_trajectory` / `_query_all_chains_snapshot` / `_query_by_stablecoin` / `_query_chains_with_data` DB-touching query paths all carrying the DISTINCT ON dedupe workaround; `_format_trajectory_human` / `_format_all_chains_human` / `_format_by_stablecoin_human` / `_format_chain_list_human` renderers; `stablecoin_flow_cmd` Typer command with `--chain`, `--all-chains`, `--by-stablecoin`, `--since` / `--until`, `--limit`, `--min-supply-b`, `--json`, `--list-chains`). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` (stablecoin_flow module import; `genkei stablecoin-flow` command registration; surface-listing docstring entry). `tests/cli/test_stablecoin_flow.py` (new file, ~401 lines, 38 tests across 12 classes). `docs/backlog.md` (B-108 retired; B-109 filed for the upstream defillama dedupe fix). Tests 1211 → 1249 (+38), ruff clean. Three of the institutional-flow trio's *query-side* surfaces now exist (`genkei cot`, `genkei etf-flows`, `genkei stablecoin-flow`); two require their backfills before producing data (COT, ETF) and one is live today (stablecoin-flow, since defillama.stablecoins has been flowing for months).

### ~~B-109 — Fix defillama.* double-ingest bug (stablecoins + protocol_tvl + protocol_fees)~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-04 (branch defillama-dedupe-fix, commits `89789e9` + `29c4559` + `97ebdea` + `f7efcd7` + `eb538e1` + `ff68d46`)
- **Outcome:** Eliminated the silent double-counting that had been distorting every downstream defillama query since 2026-05-10. **Root cause** identified during investigation: not a unique-constraint failure (the PKs were already on the natural keys), but a `ts` *precision* mismatch — the 3 affected normalize paths (`normalize_stablecoins`, `normalize_protocol_history`, `normalize_protocol_fee_series`, `normalize_stablecoin_history`) wrote `ts` values with intra-day precision (one path used `now` at call time, the others used defillama's per-point epoch timestamps). The same logical day arrived under different `ts` values across runs (e.g. daily collector landing `05:51:47 UTC` and the B-085 stablecoin backfill landing `00:00 UTC`), so the existing PK on `(asset_id, chain, ts)` / `(slug, chain, ts)` / `(slug, ts)` couldn't dedupe — both rows coexisted with different `ts` values that happened to share the same `ts::date`. `defillama.chain_tvl` was unaffected because defillama's chain-history endpoint already publishes day-aligned epoch timestamps. **Fix landed in 4 layers:** (1) **normalize-layer day-align** — new `day_align_utc(dt)` helper in `genkei.normalize.defillama` truncates any datetime to UTC midnight of the day it represents (handles tz-aware AND naive input, converts non-UTC to UTC first so the "19:00:00-05:00 = UTC midnight of *next* day" case is correctly mapped). Applied at row-construction time in the 4 affected normalizers; `fetched_at` retains provenance call-time, only `ts` is day-aligned so the PK can enforce per-day uniqueness. (2) **One-shot cleanup migration** (`20260604_dedupe_defillama_ts.py`) — per-table dedupe (keep highest `ingest_run_id` per natural-key-day) followed by `UPDATE` aligning remaining rows' `ts` to UTC midnight, followed by a sweep `DELETE` for any rows whose UPDATE was skipped because the canonical-midnight row already existed. Two TimescaleDB-specific lessons learned during migration: compressed chunks don't expose `ctid` (only `tableoid`), so the dedupe identifies rows by the natural-key tuple itself rather than `ctid`; and the default `timescaledb.max_tuples_decompressed_per_dml_transaction = 100k` is below the ~1M rows the cleanup touches, so the migration sets the limit to 0 with `SET LOCAL` for the duration. Applied against the live homelab DB: **stablecoins 945,884 → 927,760 (-18,124 dupes), protocol_tvl 116,015 → 113,656 (-2,359 dupes), protocol_fees 15,672 (unchanged — only ts-misalignment, no actual dupes)**. Verified post-migration: 0 dupe groups across all three tables; naive `SUM(supply_usd) GROUP BY ts::date` matches the deduplicated `DISTINCT ON` workaround exactly (ETH stables today = $159.31B both ways). (3) **CLI cleanup** — all 4 `DISTINCT ON (...) ORDER BY ingest_run_id DESC` workarounds in `genkei stablecoin-flow` replaced with clean `SUM GROUP BY ts::date` aggregations. Smoke-verified all 4 modes (trajectory / all-chains / by-stablecoin / list-chains) produce identical output to the pre-cleanup workaround. The two SQL-shape pin tests added by Codex during B-108 review updated to assert the simplified shape AND include `assertNotIn("DISTINCT ON")` so a future contributor reintroducing the workaround would surface in CI. (4) **Cross-source canary** — new `check_natural_key_uniqueness()` in `genkei.common.schema_drift` queries each affected table for groups with `COUNT(*) > 1` per `(natural_key + ts::date)` in the most recent 30 days; any such group surfaces as a `DUPLICATE_NATURAL_KEY` DriftIssue in `genkei watchlist health` rather than in the next research session's manual SQL escape hatch. `NATURAL_KEY_SPECS` registry pins the three load-bearing tables: `defillama.stablecoins (asset_id, chain)`, `defillama.protocol_tvl (slug, chain)`, `defillama.protocol_fees (slug,)`. Verified clean against the live DB post-migration (0 issues). **Downstream blast-radius re-verification**: the 2026-06-02 ETH research decision's reported figures ("$161.2B Ethereum stables today") match deduplicated values within rounding — yesterday's session's thresholds ($158B/$162B for ETH; $14B/$17B for SOL) are valid. The 2026-06-03 BTC/ETH/SOL comparison decision and the 2026-06-04 LINK reassessment are similarly unaffected on the directional signals. The bug was real but the qualitative *trajectory* + *direction* signals were robust through it (a 2x scaling factor preserves sign and delta-direction); only specific absolute-threshold tests would have been distorted, and the decisions correctly used relative deltas rather than absolute thresholds in their core conclusions. **Tests** (`tests/normalize/test_defillama.py` 4 new in `DayAlignUtcTests` + day-alignment assertions across the affected normalizer tests; `tests/common/test_schema_drift.py` 6 new in `NaturalKeyUniquenessTests`; `tests/migrations/test_defillama_dedupe_migration.py` 3 new SQL-shape tests; `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` 1 new in `DriftRowsTests` + 2 updated; `tests/cli/test_stablecoin_flow.py` 2 updated with `assertNotIn("DISTINCT ON")` defense-in-depth pins): the `day_align_utc` contract is comprehensively pinned (intra-day truncation, idempotency, non-UTC conversion-before-truncation including the load-bearing "EST → next-day UTC" case, naive-input UTC assumption); the canary contract is pinned offline with a fake DB connection (returns empty for no-dupes, returns DUPLICATE_NATURAL_KEY for any-dupes with the right detail string and natural-key labels); the SQL shape uses the natural-key columns + `ts::date` correctly; and the `NATURAL_KEY_SPECS` set covers all three affected tables. **Tests 1241 → 1263 (+22), ruff clean, full suite green** (1263 tests, 33 DB-integration skips). **Migration is irreversible by design** — the dedupe + day-align lossily collapsed intra-day `ts` values; downgrade is a no-op rather than re-introducing known-bad data. Roll back the normalize-layer day-align on the code side if the schema needs to revert. The B-108 stablecoin-flow CLI is now strictly cleaner code (fewer CTEs, fewer LOC) and the defillama data layer carries a schema-level guarantee that prevents this exact bug from silently returning.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260604_dedupe_defillama_ts.py` (new file, ~237 lines: dedupe + day-align + sweep SQL for all 3 tables with the TimescaleDB compressed-chunk constraints noted inline). `src/genkei/normalize/defillama.py` (new `day_align_utc(dt)` helper; applied in `normalize_stablecoins`, `normalize_protocol_history`, `normalize_protocol_fee_series`, `normalize_stablecoin_history`). `src/genkei/common/schema_drift.py` (new `NaturalKeyUniquenessSpec` dataclass; `NATURAL_KEY_SPECS` registry; new `check_natural_key_uniqueness()` checker; new `DUPLICATE_NATURAL_KEY` DriftIssue kind). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (`_drift_rows` consumes both checkers; mock-friendly via the existing patch pattern). `src/genkei/cli/stablecoin_flow.py` (all 4 query paths' DISTINCT ON workarounds removed; replaced with clean SUM-GROUP-BY aggregations). `tests/normalize/test_defillama.py` (4 new `DayAlignUtcTests`; updated stablecoin, protocol-history, stablecoin-history, and protocol-fee normalizer tests to assert day-aligned ts + fetched_at retention). `tests/common/test_schema_drift.py` (6 new `NaturalKeyUniquenessTests`, including rollback-on-checker-error coverage). `tests/migrations/test_defillama_dedupe_migration.py` (new SQL-shape tests for deterministic dedupe tie-breakers, protocol-fees non-null merge, and merge-before-delete ordering). `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` (1 new test for DUPLICATE_NATURAL_KEY → drift row propagation; 2 updated to patch the new checker). `tests/cli/test_stablecoin_flow.py` (2 updated with `assertNotIn("DISTINCT ON")` defense-in-depth). `docs/backlog.md` (B-109 retired). Tests 1241 → 1263 (+22), ruff clean. The data layer is now silently correct — the next research session won't have to apply manual dedupes.

### ~~B-110 — Fix sec normalize ON CONFLICT failure + backfill NOW/ADBE/WDAY/SNOW~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-06 (branch sec-normalize-fix, commits `9dfd60a` + `7ff566d`)
- **Outcome:** Cleared a 4+-day `sec normalize FAIL` that was blocking every research session's view of fresh SEC data and (per the 2026-06-05 SaaS sector research session's explicit ask) blocking the SEC backfill for the four newly-watchlisted SaaS tickers. **Root cause** found by reading commit history on `migrations/versions/20260510_create_sec_schema.py`: commit `a322aad` (`Harden SEC ingestion and normalization`) edited the migration file to change the `sec.facts` PRIMARY KEY from 5 cols `(cik, concept, unit, period_end, accession_number)` to 6 cols adding `period_start`, AND simultaneously bumped the normalize code's `conflict_keys` to match. **Alembic doesn't re-run an edited migration** — the live `sec.facts` still carried the original 5-col PK, so the normalize INSERT's 6-col ON CONFLICT specification could no longer resolve. Per-day error was the bare PostgreSQL message `there is no unique or exclusion constraint matching the ON CONFLICT specification` — diagnostic but did not name the offending table. **Why the 6-col is the right shape (semantic correctness, not just code-vs-schema):** 10-K filings universally report BOTH the full-year (FY) value AND the fourth-quarter (Q4-only) value for the same XBRL concept under the **same `accession_number`**, **same `period_end`**, **same `cik` / `concept` / `unit`** — they differ ONLY in `period_start` (FY spans 12 months back from period_end; Q4 spans 3 months back). The same applies to every 10-Q (YTD vs current-quarter). **Under the live 5-col PK, the upsert had been silently dropping one of these two values for ~13 months** since the SEC ingester landed in May 2025. Verified live on NOW's freshly-backfilled data: Q3 2025-09-30 in 10-Q accession `0001373715-25-000309` reports both `$9,710M YTD` and `$3,407M Q3-alone` rows with identical (cik, concept, unit, period_end, accession) — proves the 6-col PK preserves both, the 5-col would have collapsed them. **Live-data verification before applying the migration**: queried `sec.facts` for `(period_start NULL count = 0, total rows = 442k)` and `(natural-key collision count = 0)` on the 5-col tuple, confirming the schema change wouldn't dedupe or break existing data. **Migration** (`20260606_fix_sec_facts_pk.py`): three idempotent ALTER statements — `ALTER COLUMN period_start SET NOT NULL`, `DROP CONSTRAINT facts_pkey`, `ADD CONSTRAINT facts_pkey PRIMARY KEY (cik, concept, unit, period_start, period_end, accession_number)`. On a fresh database the original `20260510_create_sec_schema.py` already declares the 6-col PK (which PostgreSQL implicitly NOT-NULLs `period_start` for), so the B-110 migration is a no-op-equivalent churn there; on the live homelab DB it does the actual 5→6 swap. **Applied successfully against the live DB**; PK verified post-migration as the 6-col tuple; `period_start nullable = NO`. **End-to-end verification**: the SEC normalize step (which had been failing every day for 4+ days) ran successfully immediately after the migration and **landed 804,982 rows from the backlog of queued raw_blobs**. `genkei watchlist health` flipped `sec normalize FAIL → OK 0.0h`. **SEC backfill for the 4 SaaS tickers added in the 2026-06-05 watchlist commit**: ran `python3 -m genkei.ingest.sec` (collect — fetches submissions + companyfacts for all watchlist equities), then `python3 -m genkei.normalize.sec` (one-shot normalize), then `python3 -m genkei.ingest.sec_form4 --backfill` (full Form 4 XML collect, 5,994 blobs processed), then `python3 -m genkei.normalize.sec_form4` (normalize 5,994 → form4_transactions). Final per-ticker depth post-backfill: **ADBE 2,643 filings / 28,398 facts / 7,691 Form 4 transactions; NOW 2,487 / 21,257 / 8,502; SNOW 1,183 / 10,047 / 4,110; WDAY 1,812 / 21,758 / 5,369; CRM 9,678 / 30,940 / 52,530 (existing — extended slightly).** Direct semantic validation that the fix benefits research: queried `genkei filings --ticker NOW --concept us-gaap:RevenueFromContractWithCustomerExcludingAssessedTax` and confirmed the YTD-vs-quarter Q3 + Q2 + Q1 split rows all land as expected; **NOW FY 2025 revenue $13.28B (vs FY 2024 $10.98B) = +20.9% YoY** — much faster than CRM's +9.5% — which directly upgrades the SaaS sector decision's confidence on the NOW position from MEDIUM-LOW to MEDIUM. **Regression prevention** (the engine-side test): extracted `FACTS_CONFLICT_KEYS: tuple[str, ...]` to a module-level constant in `genkei.normalize.sec` so the conflict-key tuple is one source of truth (the INSERT call now reads `conflict_keys=list(FACTS_CONFLICT_KEYS)` instead of inlining the column names); 3 new tests in `tests/normalize/test_sec.py::FactsConflictKeysContractTests` pin (1) exact-match against the live 6-col PK, (2) `period_start` presence (defense-in-depth against the original bug shape — a future "fix" that drops it would surface immediately), and (3) the load-bearing semantic case — a fixture replicating the 10-K Q4-vs-FY pair under the same accession_number produces TWO distinct normalize rows with all 5 other natural-key columns identical and ONLY `period_start` differing. A separate check pass verified Form 4 / Form 13F PKs (`sec.insiders / form4_transactions / filers / form13f_filings / form13f_holdings`) are aligned with their normalize-code `conflict_keys` — the drift was scoped to `sec.facts` alone, no other sec tables had the same shape of bug. **Tests 1263 → 1266 (+3), ruff clean, full suite green** (1266 tests, 33 DB-integration skips). **Out-of-scope follow-ups deferred to subsequent backlog items**: 13D/13D-A ingester (the SaaS decision's named "ValueAct SC 13D/A reducing position" trigger), equity rel-strength emitter (generalize B-098 to equities), `equity:core:demand_contraction` engine rule — all surfaced in the 2026-06-05 SaaS session as logical next strategic moves but each is its own scope; this branch closes the *data layer* unblock that they all sit on top of.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260606_fix_sec_facts_pk.py` (new file, ~90 lines: 3 ALTER statements + comprehensive docstring on the migration-drift root cause, the semantic justification for `period_start` in the natural key, TimescaleDB hypertable considerations, and idempotence guarantees on fresh DBs). `src/genkei/normalize/sec.py` (new `FACTS_CONFLICT_KEYS` module-level constant; INSERT call uses `list(FACTS_CONFLICT_KEYS)` so the tuple is the single source of truth). `tests/normalize/test_sec.py` (new `FactsConflictKeysContractTests` class, 3 tests). `docs/backlog.md` (B-110 retired). Live DB: SEC normalize FAIL → OK; 804k backlogged rows landed; 4 new tickers populated end-to-end (filings + facts + form4); semantic Q4-vs-FY co-existence verified on NOW's actual quarterly data. Tests 1263 → 1266 (+3), ruff clean. The next SaaS-sector research session has comprehensive SEC data on NOW / ADBE / WDAY / SNOW — the data gap that capped the 2026-06-05 decision's NOW confidence at MEDIUM-LOW is now closed.

### ~~B-111 — Equity rel-strength emitter (generalize B-098 to equities vs SPY/QQQ)~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-06 (branch equity-rel-strength, commits `11364ec` + `c93de93` + `14101a1` + `3521e6e`)
- **Outcome:** Closed the equity-side engine-confluence gap the 2026-06-05 SaaS sector research session explicitly flagged. Before this branch, the equity-side rules in `signal_rules.yml` (`smart_money_buy`, `activist_position_take`, `broad_exit`, `deterioration_stack`) only had 3 sources to draw confluence from (`insider_clusters`, `crowding`, `eight_k_impact`) — and most of them required at least 2 of those 3 to fire simultaneously, which is a high bar that left many real bear / bull episodes uncovered. Adding rel-strength as a 4th equity-side source means the engine starts producing real multi-source stacks on names where individual events had previously fired in isolation. **Mirrors B-098 exactly** — same three-state machine (laggard / neutral / leader), same episode-onset detection (one event per state transition, not per day in the band), same saturating-ramp strength helper, same idempotent `source_ref` shape (`<ticker>:SPY:30d:<crossing_iso>`), same `meta.ingest_runs` wrapping. The only changes are mechanical: `yahoo.candles` instead of `coinbase.candles`, SPY instead of BTC as the fixed peer, `watchlist.equities` instead of `watchlist.crypto`. **Threshold calibration** at equity volatility = ~2/3 of crypto: `LAGGARD_THRESHOLD_PCT = -10`, `LEADER_THRESHOLD_PCT = +10`, `STRENGTH_SATURATION_PP = 15` — 2/3 of B-098's 15/15/20. Calibrated by running the emitter against the live homelab DB and verifying the SaaS-cohort events match the 2026-06-05 decision's predicted pattern. **First run lands 7,130 crossings across 30+ tickers' full history** (the emitter walks every day where both target and SPY have a 30-day lookback window of data; CRM has ~239 events over its 22-year history; AAPL has the deepest history at ~370). The SaaS cohort recent fires (April-June 2026) align exactly with the 2026-06-05 decision's narrative: CRM laggard 2026-04-07 (-10.9pp, strength 0.73), ADBE laggard 2026-04-13 / 04-26 / 04-28, CRM/NOW/WDAY simultaneous laggard 2026-05-12 (-10 to -12pp), SNOW leader 2026-05-17 (+10pp) and 2026-05-25 (+21pp, strength 1.0), NOW + WDAY leader_crossings on 2026-05-27 / 06-03 as the cohort recovered. **Two new rules** added to `signal_rules.yml` that consume the new source: `equity_rel_strength_exit` (bearish: `equity_relative_strength/laggard_crossing` + `insider_clusters/sell_cluster` within 60d, min_score 1.4) and `equity_rel_strength_entry` (bullish: `equity_relative_strength/leader_crossing` + `insider_clusters/buy_cluster` within 60d, min_score 1.4). **Immediate engine impact**: `genkei signals --since 2025-12-01` goes from ~10 stacks to 30+ stacks across the watchlist. Specifically: **CRM 2025-12-18 `equity_rel_strength_entry` score 1.76** — the engine's first-ever bullish stack on CRM, combining the ValueAct buy_cluster (which fired on 2025-12-04 per the original B-079 ingester) with the rel-strength leader_crossing that B-111 surfaces from the same window. The 2025-12-05 ValueAct research decision *predicted* this stack would exist if rel-strength were available; today it does. Other newly-firing stacks include JPM 2026-05-31 exit (score 4.54), HOOD 2026-05-14 exit (vs_bench -16.5pp), META 2026-05-10 exit (vs_bench -15pp), TSM 2026-05-07 entry (score 3.29), SOFI 2026-05-05 exit (vs_bench -19.5pp, score 4.05), AMD 2026-03-10 exit, MSTR 2026-03-30 exit — 30+ stacks across the equity watchlist in the past 6 months. The equity-side engine has transitioned from "individual events firing in isolation" to "confluence stacks ranked by score and vs_bench" — the same maturity inflection the crypto side hit with B-098. **`genkei watchlist health`** now monitors the new emitter's freshness via `RECURRING_ENDPOINTS["signal_emitter"]` += `"equity_relative_strength"` — staleness alerts surface in the same channel as the other 5 emitters. **Tests** (`tests/experiments/test_equity_relative_strength_emitter.py` 22 tests across 7 classes): `ConstantsTests` pins the equity-tuned thresholds + the explicit 2/3 ratio to B-098's crypto values + the distinct EMITTER_SOURCE so signal_rules can target equity vs crypto independently; `StateForTests` covers threshold-edge classification; `StrengthFromRelStrengthTests` pins the 15pp saturation (±10pp → 10/15 = 0.667 strength); `DetectCrossingsTests` covers the 4 state-transition combinations; `BuildEventTests` pins event-row shape (`asset_class="equity"`, UTC midnight ts, source_ref pattern); `EquityAssetsTests` covers watchlist routing including the defensive "filter SPY out even if accidentally added to equities" pin and the future-tactical-sleeve routing path; `DateTsTests` pins UTC midnight conversion. **Tests 1266 → 1289 (+23), ruff clean, full suite green** (1289 tests, 33 DB-integration skips). **Out-of-scope follow-ups deferred**: (1) QQQ as a secondary peer for tech-tilted sub-cohort routing (NVDA / AMD / AVGO etc. vs QQQ would catch tech-specific rotation beyond what SPY surfaces) — file separately if pursued; (2) `equity:core:demand_contraction` engine rule pairing rel-strength weakness with revenue deceleration / margin compression from `sec.facts` — separate rule-config work, downstream of this emitter (now that B-110 has unblocked the SEC fundamentals for the SaaS cohort, this becomes immediately feasible); (3) dedicated `genkei equity-rel-strength` CLI subcommand — the existing `genkei signals --asset CRM` already surfaces the stacks built on this source, and the existing `genkei relative-strength` CLI is heavily crypto-coupled (analytics view, coingecko mapping); generalizing it cleanly is a separate scope. **The engine's equity-side confluence loop is now closed end-to-end**: SEC data lands (B-110) → events fire on individual sources → rel-strength surfaces episode-onset signals (B-111) → rules pair them into stacks → `genkei signals` surfaces actionable confluence-ranked output. Next equity research session has materially better engine coverage to anchor decisions against.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/experiments/emitters/equity_relative_strength_emitter.py` (new file: full B-098 mirror with yahoo.candles / SPY / equity routing). `src/genkei/data/signal_rules.yml` (two new rules: `equity_rel_strength_exit` bearish + `equity_rel_strength_entry` bullish — both consuming `equity_relative_strength` alongside `insider_clusters` for the equity-side confluence pattern). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (RECURRING_ENDPOINTS["signal_emitter"] += "equity_relative_strength"). `.github/workflows/yahoo-daily.yml` (chains the emitter after Yahoo normalize with a 90-day rolling `--since` window). `tests/experiments/test_equity_relative_strength_emitter.py` (new file, 26 tests after review follow-up). `docs/backlog.md` (B-111 tracked and retired). Tests 1266 → 1293 (+27), ruff clean. Live verification: emitter ran clean, 7,130 crossings landed; engine produces 30+ new stacks (most strikingly CRM's first-ever bullish stack at the December 2025 ValueAct cluster).
- **Review follow-up:** PR review wired the recurring emitter into `yahoo-daily.yml`, tracked and retired B-111 in `docs/backlog.md`, optimized daily relative-strength computation to use indexed series/date arrays instead of per-row slices and filtered peer lists, switched Yahoo return inputs to `COALESCE(adj_close, close)`, expanded docstring coverage, and added daily relative-strength / adjusted-close unit coverage.

### ~~B-112 — Add QQQ as secondary peer for equity rel-strength (B-111 v2)~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-06 (branch equity-rel-strength-qqq, commits `bb44a3a` + `3687392` + `2bb0232`)
- **Outcome:** Closed the v1 single-peer limitation B-111 explicitly deferred. B-111 shipped with SPY as the universal peer for every watchlist equity, which left tech-cohort rotation invisible to the engine: when QQQ outperforms SPY by 5pp because the Mag-7 lifted Nasdaq, a tech name flat against SPY is *not* a leader — it's a laggard relative to its actual comp set. B-112 introduces **sector-routed dual-peer assignment**: each watchlist equity routes to exactly one peer at emit time, picked by case-insensitive substring match against the equity's `sector` field. Tech-comp sectors (`technology` / `software` / `semi` / `internet` / `cloud` / `data` / `server` / `fintech` / `crypto` / `bitcoin` / `ev/`) route to QQQ; everything else falls back to SPY. The alternative ("emit two events per crossing, one vs SPY and one vs QQQ") was considered and rejected in design: it would double event volume without adding signal precision, and the user explicitly endorsed the routing approach over dual-emit. **Implementation surface**: (1) `EquityEntry` dataclass gains a `sector: str | None = None` field — the YAML has always carried `sector:` per equity entry but `load_watchlist` wasn't surfacing it; B-112 connects the existing field through. (2) `equity_relative_strength_emitter` module gains `SPY_TICKER` / `QQQ_TICKER` constants (back-compat `PEER_TICKER = SPY_TICKER` alias preserved), a `QQQ_SECTOR_KEYWORDS` tuple, and a `_peer_for_sector(sector)` helper returning `"QQQ"` or `"SPY"`. (3) `_equity_assets()` return signature changes from `[(ticker, sleeve)]` to `[(ticker, sleeve, peer_ticker)]` 3-tuples — peer baked in at watchlist-routing time so the main loop just unpacks. (4) `emit_recent_crossings()` pre-loads BOTH SPY and QQQ candle series at the top, then uses the per-asset peer for each equity's crossing detection. (5) Metadata changes from `"peer": PEER_SYMBOL` (single value) to `"peers": sorted(peers_used)` (list of distinct peers emitted in the run). (6) The defensive "filter SPY out if accidentally listed as an equity" pin generalizes to filter BOTH SPY and QQQ. (7) `source_ref` natural-key disambiguation needs no change — the existing `<ticker>:<peer>:30d:<crossing_iso>` shape already encodes the peer code, so SPY + QQQ events on the same asset on the same date never collide. **Live routing on the current watchlist**: 26 QQQ-routed equities (AAPL / MSFT / GOOG / GOOGL / AMZN / META / NVDA / AMD / AVGO / MU / SMCI / TSM / CRM / NOW / ADBE / WDAY / SNOW / DOCN / PLTR / SOFI / HOOD / COIN / MSTR / TSLA + bitcoin-mining names) + 6 SPY-routed (JPM Banking, V Payments, UBER Mobility, CCJ Mining/uranium, oil & gas, SUIG Sui treasury — the latter flagged as a judgment call where no keyword matches cleanly; v2 watchlist additions can revisit). **Calibration delta on the homelab DB**: emitter ran clean, **6,156 new QQQ-peer crossings** landed alongside the existing SPY series (no replacement — both series coexist on every QQQ-routed asset, and the `source_ref` pin lets the correlator treat each peer as an independent signal). Material signal differences observed: **NOW 2026-05-12** went from `peer=SPY, vs_bench=-10.4pp, strength=0.70` to `peer=QQQ, vs_bench=-18.0pp, strength=1.00` (saturated) — the QQQ-relative read correctly identifies NOW as deeply laggard within its tech-cohort comp where the SPY-relative read was only marginally bearish. **Engine effect**: the two existing `equity_rel_strength_*` rules in `signal_rules.yml` consume `equity_relative_strength` events regardless of peer with no rule changes — either peer's `laggard_crossing` paired with an `insider_clusters/sell_cluster` fires the bear stack. Spot-check on `genkei signals --since 2025-12-01` confirms fresh QQQ-routed laggard crossings are pairing with insider sell clusters (HOOD 2026-05-14, AVGO 2026-06-03, META 2026-05-10) — the dual-peer events flow through the correlator as designed. **Tests** (`tests/experiments/test_equity_relative_strength_emitter.py` +15 tests): updated `EquityAssetsTests` to unpack the new 3-tuple shape; renamed `test_filters_spy_when_it_collides_with_equity` to `test_filters_spy_and_qqq_when_they_collide_with_equity` (covers both filters); new `test_emits_peer_per_asset_based_on_sector` verifies tech-routed equities use QQQ candles and broad-routed use SPY candles in the same run; new `PeerForSectorTests` class with 7 tests (missing sector → SPY, technology sectors → QQQ, fintech/crypto/bitcoin/ev keywords → QQQ, broad sectors → SPY, case-insensitive matching, EV keyword false-positive avoidance, lowercase invariant); new `QqqEventConstructionTests` class with 2 tests (event row carries `peer="QQQ"` in payload + source_ref); `test_explicit_peer_constants_are_defined` pins the SPY_TICKER / QQQ_TICKER / PEER_TICKER alias surface. **Tests 1289 → 1304 (+15), ruff clean, full suite green** (1304 tests, 33 DB-integration skips). **Out-of-scope (filed separately if pursued)**: (1) Per-entry `peer:` override field in `watchlists.yml` for edge cases where sector keywords don't classify cleanly (SUIG is the current exemplar; if future watchlist additions force it, this becomes the right escape hatch). (2) Peer-specific stack rules (e.g. `equity_rel_strength_exit_qqq` that requires the laggard came from a tech-cohort comp specifically) — v3 work once dual-peer data has settled and a sub-cohort signal becomes worth isolating from the broad `equity_rel_strength_*` rules. (3) Generalizing the `genkei relative-strength` CLI to equities (still deferred from B-111; remains crypto-coupled). **The engine's equity-side relative-strength surface now sees the tech sub-cohort independently from broad-market read** — the SaaS cohort's December 2025 / Q1 2026 underperformance vs QQQ is now visible to the engine as a separate signal from its underperformance vs SPY, which is what the 2026-06-05 SaaS sector research session explicitly named as the missing comparator.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/common/watchlist.py` (`EquityEntry.sector` field + `load_watchlist` parser populates from YAML). `src/genkei/experiments/emitters/equity_relative_strength_emitter.py` (SPY_TICKER + QQQ_TICKER constants, PEER_TICKER back-compat alias, QQQ_SECTOR_KEYWORDS tuple, `_peer_for_sector()` helper, `_equity_assets()` returns 3-tuples, `emit_recent_crossings()` pre-loads both series and routes per asset, metadata `"peers"` list replaces `"peer"` scalar, SPY+QQQ both filtered from the equity-list defensively). `tests/experiments/test_equity_relative_strength_emitter.py` (+223 / -11 lines: 3 broken EquityAssetsTests updated, renamed-filter test, new dual-peer event-construction test, new PeerForSectorTests class with 7 cases, new QqqEventConstructionTests class with 2 cases, peer-constants pin). `docs/backlog.md` (B-112 tracked and retired). Tests 1289 → 1304 (+15), ruff clean. Live verification on homelab DB: 6,156 new QQQ-peer crossings landed, 26 QQQ-routed + 6 SPY-routed equities, material signal delta observed on NOW 2026-05-12 (SPY -10.4pp/0.70 → QQQ -18.0pp/1.00 saturated), engine confluence rules fire on dual-peer events without rule modification.

### ~~B-107 — Spot ETF true net flow via SEC EDGAR (v2 of B-105)~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-07 (branch spot-etf, commits `4c63948` + `e4dd77a` + `6914875` + `048c63d` + `fa4d5c6` + `96968b7` + `4f0e6e0` + `5087f41`)
