- **Outcome:** Closed the canonical "are creations exceeding redemptions on a given day" signal that the 2026-06-02 ETH / SOL / SUI research sessions all named as the missing institutional-flow surface. B-105 v1 had shipped a *magnitude proxy* (dollar-volume from `yahoo.candles`) but no *signed direction* — both views are needed, and B-107 adds the signed-direction half. **Headline title is a misnomer** — the original B-107 spec assumed SEC EDGAR daily filings carried daily creation/redemption baskets or daily shares-outstanding. Phase 1 investigation killed that assumption: spot crypto ETFs are grantor trusts (SIC 6221), NOT registered investment companies, so N-CEN / N-PORT / Form 8937 / Form NPX (the daily-ish forms the spec listed) either don't apply or only file annually. IBIT's actual EDGAR cadence is 10-Q (quarterly), 10-K (annual), 8-K (event-driven), FWP / 424B3 / POS AM / S-1/A (marketing + registration). 10-Q gives 4 quarter-end checkpoints per year — useful for triangulation but too coarse for the live signal. **Pivot path:** issuer product pages. Phase 1b probed iShares / Fidelity / Grayscale / Bitwise / ARK individually — iShares is the clear winner: a public, no-auth, no-Cloudflare JSON product-screener endpoint (`https://www.ishares.com/us/product-screener/product-screener-v3.1.jsn?...`) returns ~1.9 MB covering all 530 of their US ETFs in a single request. The three crypto products live in that feed with `aladdinAssetClass="Digital Assets"`: IBIT (BTC, $46.2B AUM), ETHA (ETH, $4.45B), ETHB (Staked ETH, $458M). Each entry exposes `navAmount` + `navAmountAsOf` + `totalNetAssets` + `totalNetAssetsFundAsOf` at daily T+1 cadence. **Math**: `shares_outstanding=total_net_assets/nav_per_share`; `net_flow_usd=(shares_today-shares_yesterday)*nav_today`. IBIT on 2026-06-05 derives to 1.367B shares against $46.2B AUM at $33.81 NAV — matches IBIT's published market cap. Multi-issuer expansion (FBTC / BITB / GBTC / ARKB) was considered for v1 but deferred to v2.1 (filed as B-113): ARKB is Cloudflare-walled, GBTC rate-limits scripted access, FBTC URL is not yet identified, BITB looks plausible but unprobed. SEC 10-Q historical backfill is also deferred (filed as B-114). **Implementation surface**: (1) New migration `f5d9c0e1a407` creates `etf` schema + `etf.fund_snapshots` table keyed on `(ticker, snapshot_date)` with columns for ticker / snapshot_date / issuer / asset / cusip / isin / nav_per_share_usd (NUMERIC(20,8)) / total_net_assets_usd (NUMERIC(20,2)) / shares_outstanding (NUMERIC(20,4)) / source_endpoint / fetched_at / ingest_run_id. Plain table (not hypertable): 3 funds × 252 trading days × 5y ≈ 4k rows steady-state. CHECK constraints pin asset ∈ {BTC, ETH}, nav > 0, tna ≥ 0. (2) New ingester `src/genkei/ingest/ishares.py` (398 lines) pulls the product-screener JSON, filters to watchlist `etf_tickers` where `issuer == "BlackRock"`, derives shares via TNA / NAV at 4-decimal quantization, writes raw blob + bulk_upsert. Backfill is NOT supported by the feed (returns only current snapshot); a separate historical NAV source is the v2.1 work in B-114. Critical decision: NAV and TNA as-of dates must match; mismatched rows are skipped until iShares republishes aligned fields so no `(ticker, snapshot_date)` row combines metrics from different dates. (3) CLI extension adds `--net-flow` to `genkei etf-flows`. The default B-105 dollar-volume mode is unchanged — the two modes are complementary: dollar-volume answers "is the ETF trading heavily today?", net flow answers "are creations exceeding redemptions?". Net flow computed at query time via a SQL window: `(shares_outstanding-LAG(shares_outstanding)OVER(PARTITION BY ticker ORDER BY snapshot_date))*nav_per_share`. **Critical SQL decision**: the LAG window runs in a *subquery* with no since/until filter, then the outer query applies the date filter — if the WHERE were inside, the first row in the filter window would lose its predecessor and falsely report NULL flow when the predecessor snapshot exists. The asset filter goes INSIDE the subquery so the partition stays per-asset (otherwise ETHA could end up as the LAG predecessor of an IBIT row ordered by date). The renderer surfaces NULL net_flow as `(first day)` rather than `$0` because zero would lie about what we know. (4) `genkei watchlist health` registers `ishares` in both `PRIMARY_TABLES` and `RECURRING_ENDPOINTS` — endpoint label is `collect` per the universal convention pinned by `test_every_source_expects_at_least_a_collect_endpoint`. (5) New workflow `.github/workflows/ishares-daily.yml` runs at 13:00 UTC (~9am ET) on the self-hosted Beelink runner — after iShares publishes T+1, well before next-day open. Idempotent on `(ticker, snapshot_date)`. **Phase 1 investigation doc** (`docs/sources/spot-etf-net-flow.md`) preserves the EDGAR dead-end + per-issuer probe results so a v2.1 attempt doesn't repeat the discovery work. **Live verification on homelab DB**: collector ran clean, 3 rows landed for IBIT/ETHA/ETHB at 2026-06-05; `genkei etf-flows --asset BTC --net-flow` returns IBIT with `(first day)` marker (correct — only one snapshot exists); `genkei watchlist health` shows `ishares.collect OK 0.0h` + `etf.fund_snapshots OK`. **Tests** (`tests/ingest/test_ishares.py` 29 new tests + `tests/cli/test_etf_flows.py` +11 tests): `ModuleConstantsTests` pins SOURCE_NAME / ISSUER_FILTER / PRODUCT_SCREENER_URL; `CoerceDecimalTests` covers the iShares `{d, r}` numeric envelope (prefers `r`, falls back to `d` with commas stripped, dash → None); `CoerceStringTests` covers the same envelope for string fields; `ParseAsOfDateTests` pins YYYYMMDD-int preference over human-string parsing for locale-free dates; `ParseSnapshotsTests` (10 cases) covers the full extractor: 3 BlackRock crypto ETFs land + IVV (Equity-class non-crypto) is filtered out, shares-outstanding arithmetic verified against the live DB row, watchlist provides asset+issuer (feed's own asset class is intentionally ignored), mismatched NAV/TNA as-of dates are skipped, missing-required-field skip, zero-NAV skip, zero-TNA allowed, negative TNA skip, empty payload, non-dict-payload raise, case-insensitive ticker match. CLI test additions (`QueryNetFlowTests` 6 cases + `FormatNetFlowHumanTests` 4 cases) capture SQL via FakeCursor and pin the load-bearing window-vs-filter ordering, asset-inside-subquery, params order; renderer tests pin `(first day)` for NULL and explicit `+`/`-` sign formatting. `test_watchlist_cmd.py` updated to include `ishares` in the registry pin. **Tests 1304 → 1347 (+43), ruff clean, full suite green** (1347 tests, 33 DB-integration skips). **Out-of-scope follow-ups filed as new backlog items**: B-113 (multi-issuer expansion to FBTC/BITB/GBTC/ARKB), B-114 (SEC 10-Q quarter-end backfill). **The institutional-flow loop the research sessions named is now closed for the dominant issuer**: BlackRock's spot crypto ETFs publish daily NAV + TNA → the ingester lands snapshots → derived net flow surfaces creations vs redemptions → `genkei etf-flows --asset BTC --net-flow` is the one-command answer to "are institutions buying or selling today?". The next ETH / BTC / SOL research session has materially better engine coverage for the institutional-positioning question than the 2026-06-02 sessions did.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260607_create_etf_fund_snapshots.py` (etf schema + fund_snapshots table). `src/genkei/ingest/ishares.py` (new file: product-screener fetch, watchlist filter, shares derivation, idempotent upsert). `src/genkei/cli/etf_flows.py` (+174 / -23 lines: `--net-flow` flag, `_query_net_flow` with windowed SQL, `_format_net_flow_human` renderer with first-day marker + signed-flow formatting). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (PRIMARY_TABLES + RECURRING_ENDPOINTS registration for ishares.collect). `.github/workflows/ishares-daily.yml` (new daily cron at 13:00 UTC on self-hosted Beelink). `tests/ingest/test_ishares.py` (new file, 29 tests). `tests/cli/test_etf_flows.py` (+11 tests for --net-flow). `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` (registry pin). `docs/sources/spot-etf-net-flow.md` (new file: Phase 1 investigation + v2.1 deferred work). `docs/backlog.md` (B-107 retired; B-113 + B-114 filed as v2.1 follow-ups). Tests 1304 → 1347 (+43), ruff clean. Live verification on homelab DB: 3 rows landed for IBIT/ETHA/ETHB at 2026-06-05; CLI surfaces `(first day)` markers correctly until day 2 lands; watchlist health monitors the new source end-to-end.

### ~~B-088 — Sui on-chain validator + staking-flow ingester~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-07 (branch sui-validator, commits `9832514` + `3612942` + `24fdcd2` + `b0d1bd8` + `4ef187f`)
- **Outcome:** Closed the second-biggest crypto-tactical data gap the 2026-05-20 SUI research session named — "no Sui-chain validator / staking flow" — which had left "are stakers committing more capital or unbonding" unanswerable on a name the user actively trades. The session's bear thesis on SUI (idiosyncratic 22pp underperformance vs SOL over 1y) couldn't be confirmed or refuted by on-chain conviction data because that data didn't exist in the lake. B-088 fills that gap. **Key simplification vs the B-088 spec**: the spec assumed Blockvision's managed RPC was the data path (mirroring B-082's Etherscan-V2-keyed pattern for LINK). Phase 1 probing of the public Sui mainnet fullnode (`https://fullnode.mainnet.sui.io`) showed the standard JSON-RPC methods — `suix_getLatestSuiSystemState` and `suix_getValidatorsApy` — work without auth, without Cloudflare, without any observed rate limit. Skipping Blockvision removed an API-key registration step, a D-020 graceful-skip code path, and a third-party-dependency vector entirely. **Implementation surface**: (1) New migration `a6e7d8f9c012` creates the `onchain.sui_validators` table keyed on `(epoch, validator_address)`. **Schema decision**: NEW table rather than extending `onchain.staking_events` (B-082's LINK table) with a chain discriminator — Sui data is snapshot-shaped (per-validator-per-epoch state) while staking_events is event-shaped (per-transaction Staked/Unstaked/UnbondingPeriodStarted), structurally distinct. Forcing both into one schema would NULL out half the columns of every row and corrupt the event-stream semantics; two tables in one `onchain` schema is the cleaner fit. **MIST columns are NUMERIC(40, 0)** not BIGINT because Sui's max supply (10B SUI = 10^19 MIST) exceeds BIGINT's max (~9.2×10^18) — today's totalStake of 7.25B SUI is within BIGINT, but a future inflation event or single-validator stake concentration could push past the boundary, and NUMERIC's ~16-byte-per-row cost is cheap insurance against overflow corruption. Per-row columns include voting_power, stake_amount_mist, next_epoch_stake_mist, pending_stake_mist, pending_withdraw_mist (the actual flow signal — deposits in flight + withdrawals in flight), commission_rate_bps, gas_price, apy (joined from suix_getValidatorsApy), staking_pool_activation_epoch, staking_pool_deactivation_epoch (lifecycle), rewards_pool_mist. Follow-up migration `b8c9d0e1f2a3` adds named non-negative checks for the nullable MIST balance columns so already-created tables get the same data-integrity guard as fresh installs. Volume estimate: 129 validators × 365 epochs/year ≈ 47k rows/year — tiny, plain table not hypertable. (2) New collector `src/genkei/ingest/sui_staking.py` (487 lines) issues two JSON-RPC POSTs per run (`suix_getLatestSuiSystemState` + `suix_getValidatorsApy`), parses the validator list, joins APY by `suiAddress = apys[].address`, writes successful raw blobs to `meta.raw_blobs`, and bulk-upserts the rows. Critical decisions: validators missing from the APY response are KEPT with `apy=None` rather than dropped (staking/flow signal is more load-bearing than APY signal — a partial-APY-failure shouldn't lose the stake data); if the APY RPC itself fails, the run records `partial_endpoints` and still lands validator snapshots with `apy=NULL`; parse failures record `partial_endpoints` before failing loudly; defective validator records (missing suiAddress or stakingPoolSuiBalance) are silently skipped with a warning; `pending_stake_mist` and `pending_withdraw_mist` default to `Decimal(0)` because those columns are NOT NULL. **Backfill is NOT supported by v1** — the public RPC's `suix_getEpochs` returns "Method not found" (indexer-side only), so historical reconstruction would need a different data path. Forward-only from the day of first run; documented as a v2 follow-up. Idempotent on `(epoch, validator_address)` so same-epoch reruns refresh stake/flow fields while preserving previously captured APY during degraded APY runs. (3) `genkei watchlist health` registers `sui_staking` in `PRIMARY_TABLES` + `RECURRING_ENDPOINTS` with endpoint label `collect` per the universal convention. (4) New workflow `.github/workflows/sui-staking-daily.yml` runs at 06:00 UTC daily on the self-hosted Beelink runner — Sui epoch cadence is ~24h so daily catches each roll-over without crowding the 13:00 UTC ishares window. (5) The SUI 2026-05-20 research decision file's "Backlog implications" note marked B-088 resolved with a copy-pastable `genkei query` showing the headline acceptance: `SELECT SUM(stake_amount_mist)/1e9 AS total_staked_sui, (SUM(pending_stake_mist) - SUM(pending_withdraw_mist))/1e9 AS net_pending_flow_sui FROM onchain.sui_validators WHERE epoch >= (SELECT MAX(epoch) - 30 FROM onchain.sui_validators) GROUP BY epoch ORDER BY epoch DESC`. **Live verification on homelab DB**: collector ran clean, 129 rows landed for epoch 1151 (the current Sui mainnet epoch). Headline aggregates match upstream perfectly: 7.25B SUI total staked (matches the system state's `totalStake` field), 129 active validators (matches `len(activeValidators)`), avg APY 1.45%, **+2.2M SUI net pending inflow this epoch** (commitments exceed withdrawals — the canonical "are stakers committing capital or unbonding" signal). Top 5 validators by stake are Mysten Labs (218M SUI, 2% commission, the Sui Foundation operator), a second Mysten Labs operator at 181M SUI, Coinbase (155M SUI, 10% commission — the highest commission among top validators), Galaxy Digital (151M SUI, 5%), and Aftermath (142M SUI, 4%). **Tests** (`tests/ingest/test_sui_staking.py` 39 new tests): `ModuleConstantsTests` pins SOURCE_NAME / COLLECT_ENDPOINT_LABEL / SUI_RPC_URL / both method names so a typo doesn't go silently; `CoerceIntTests` covers the u64-as-string envelope including bool-rejection (a sneaky True would coerce to 1 via int() and corrupt voting_power); `CoerceDecimalTests` covers MIST values + APY decimals + native int/float; `MsToUtcDatetimeTests` round-trips a known epoch-start ms with explicit UTC tzinfo; `ParseValidatorRowsTests` (16 cases) covers the full extractor end-to-end: 2 valid + 1 defective row → 2 rows out, epoch + epoch_start_ts carried from system state, MIST values preserved (no unit conversion at write time), APY joined by validator_address, APY epoch mismatch leaves APY null, missing-APY validator kept with apy=None, pending_flow defaults to Decimal(0) for NOT NULL columns, validators missing suiAddress / stake are skipped, malformed payloads (system state without epoch, list-not-dict, activeValidators-not-list) raise ValueError loudly, commission_rate stored as basis points. New `RpcPostTests` pin read-only POST retry opt-in; `CollectTests` cover APY RPC degradation (rows still land with `apy=NULL` and partial metadata), stale APY reruns and per-validator APY omissions preserving existing APY on conflicts, and parse-failure metadata before re-raise. `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` updated with `sui_staking` in both registry pins. **Tests 1344 → 1388 (+44), ruff clean, full suite green** (1388 tests, 33 DB-integration skips). **The crypto-tactical engine now sees the Sui staking surface**: a research session running `/research SUI` tomorrow can pull staking trajectory, per-validator concentration, commission/APY distribution, and the daily net-flow signal — exactly the inputs the May 2026 session named as missing. The November 2026 `/reflect-decisions` cycle will have ~5 months of staking-flow data to triangulate against the original 2026-05-20 trim-position decision.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260607_create_onchain_sui_validators.py` + `migrations/versions/20260607_add_sui_validator_balance_checks.py` (onchain.sui_validators schema with NUMERIC(40,0) MIST columns + check constraints including nullable balance columns + epoch-DESC indexes). `src/genkei/ingest/sui_staking.py` (new file: JSON-RPC POST helper, coerce helpers for u64-as-string, ms-to-UTC-datetime, parse_validator_rows extractor, collect() entrypoint). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (PRIMARY_TABLES + RECURRING_ENDPOINTS registration for sui_staking.collect). `.github/workflows/sui-staking-daily.yml` (06:00 UTC daily cron on self-hosted Beelink). `tests/ingest/test_sui_staking.py` (new file, 39 tests). `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` (registry pin update). `docs/research/decisions/2026-05-20-sui-position-assessment.md` (Backlog implications #3 marked resolved with copy-pastable query). `docs/backlog.md` (B-088 retired). Tests 1344 → 1388 (+44), ruff clean. Live verification on homelab DB: 129 rows landed for epoch 1151, total staked 7.25B SUI (matches upstream), net pending inflow +2.2M SUI this epoch, top validators are Mysten / Coinbase / Galaxy / Aftermath at expected stake levels.

### ~~B-089 — SUI token unlock / vesting schedule data source~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-07 (branch sui-token-unlock, commits `5d8112f` + `0170afd` + `73f0330` + `fba3f84` + `ebd2625` + `2efa262`)
- **Outcome:** Closed the remaining SUI on-chain data gap from the 2026-05-20 research session — partially. The session named "no SUI token unlock schedule" as a load-bearing supply-side gap; the bear thesis on SUI is that aggressive VC vesting (Series A + Series B + Early Contributors) compresses the token through scheduled unlocks. Phase 1 surveyed 8 candidate sources (DefiLlama emissions, Sui Foundation blog, CryptoRank vesting page, Dropstab, Tokenomist.ai, CoinGecko, MystenLabs GitHub, on-chain Sui RPC vesting-object discovery) and found exactly ONE viable free path: CryptoRank's public Next.js `__NEXT_DATA__` SSR payload exposes the full batch schedule for ONE of SUI's 8 allocation categories — Community Reserves (10.648% of supply, 85 monthly batches from 2023-05-03 TGE through 2030-05-01). The remaining 7 categories — Allocated After 2030 (52.172%), Stake Subsidies (9.494%), **Series A (7.142%)**, **Series B (6.956%)**, **Early Contributors (6.134%)**, Community Access Program (5.820%), Mysten Labs Treasury (1.635%) — are paywalled across all surveyed free sources. The three highlighted VC tranches are exactly the ones that drive the bear thesis, so this resolution is **partial-by-design** rather than complete. Per user direction (`AskUserQuestion` answered "Ship partial ingester for Community Reserves only"), v1 ships the partial collector rather than going survey-only. **Implementation surface**: (1) New migration `b7f8e9d0c113` creates `onchain.sui_unlocks` keyed on `(allocation_name, unlock_date)` with denormalized `allocation_total_tokens` + `allocation_total_percent_of_supply` per row so the headline "SUI unlocking in next N days" query is a trivial `SUM(unlock_tokens)` without a cross-join to an allocations-master table — the ~85-row denormalization cost is trivial. NUMERIC(20,0) for absolute SUI counts and NUMERIC(20,4) for derived `unlock_tokens` to preserve the 4-decimal precision the percent math produces. Co-located with `onchain.sui_validators` (B-088) so cross-source queries — e.g. "is the next unlock landing during a net-outflow staking epoch?" — don't need cross-schema joins. (2) New collector `src/genkei/ingest/sui_unlocks.py` (400 lines) fetches the CryptoRank vesting page, extracts `__NEXT_DATA__` via regex, walks to `props.pageProps.vestingInfo.allocations`, and filters to the module-level `KNOWN_FREE_ALLOCATIONS = ("Community Reserves",)` tuple. **Critical extension point**: that tuple is the entire code change needed if a future allocation becomes free — adding a name there flips it on, no schema migration required because the table was designed to extend cleanly. Defensive parsing pinned in tests: trailing-space strip on CryptoRank's `"Community Reserves "` so the PK stays stable across runs; paywalled allocations (batches=None) silently skipped; duplicate-date batches keep first-seen so a single bulk_upsert doesn't see colliding rows in one batch (which psycopg's COPY-based upsert hates); allocation totals denormalized onto every row; missing `vestingInfo` raises ValueError loudly. (3) `genkei watchlist health` registers `sui_unlocks` in `PRIMARY_TABLES` + `RECURRING_ENDPOINTS` with the standard `collect` endpoint label. (4) New workflow `.github/workflows/sui-unlocks-daily.yml` runs at 07:00 UTC daily on the self-hosted Beelink runner — slots between `sui-staking-daily` (06:00) and `ishares-daily` (13:00). The vesting schedule is mostly static (past batches are immutable once published) but daily catches any forward revisions promptly; idempotent on the PK so missed/repeated runs are harmless. (5) The SUI 2026-05-20 research decision file's Backlog implications #2 was updated to mark partial resolution with explicit caveats about the gated VC categories so the next `/reflect-decisions` cycle doesn't over-read the partial coverage. **Phase 1 investigation doc** (`docs/sources/sui-unlocks.md`) captures the per-source survey + "what would unblock full coverage" path so the next paid-data conversation has the discovery work already done. **Live verification on homelab DB**: collector ran clean, 85 rows landed for Community Reserves. Headline query `SELECT SUM(unlock_tokens)/1 AS sui_unlocking FROM onchain.sui_unlocks WHERE unlock_date BETWEEN CURRENT_DATE AND CURRENT_DATE + N` returns 4.00M SUI in next 30 days (0.04% of total supply), 8.01M in 60d (0.08%), 12.01M in 90d (0.12%), 48.04M in 365d (0.48%) — these are Community Reserves only; the full picture would include the gated VC tranches at materially larger magnitudes. **Tests** (`tests/ingest/test_sui_unlocks.py` 28 new tests): `ModuleConstantsTests` pin SOURCE_NAME / COLLECT_ENDPOINT_LABEL / CRYPTORANK_SUI_VESTING_URL / KNOWN_FREE_ALLOCATIONS so a contributor adding a new allocation has to update tests + docs in lockstep; `CoerceDecimalTests` cover the JSON-numeric envelope including bool-rejection (is_tge is bool — Decimal coercion of True/False must not silently equal 1/0); `ParseIsoDateTests` pin the `'2023-05-03T00:00:00.000Z'` round-trip; `ExtractNextDataTests` cover the missing-tag + unparseable-JSON failure paths; `ParseAllocationsTests` (12 cases) cover the full extractor — trailing-space strip, KNOWN_FREE_ALLOCATIONS gating, batches=None skip, unlock_tokens derivation arithmetic to 4 decimals, is_tge flag preservation, vesting_type lowercasing, duplicate-date drop, denormalized allocation totals, missing-vestingInfo raise, non-list-allocations raise, empty-allocations empty-result. `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` updated with `sui_unlocks` in both registry pins. **Tests 1380 → 1416 (+36), ruff clean, full suite green** (1416 tests, 33 DB-integration skips). **Out-of-scope follow-up filed as B-115**: extending coverage to the 7 paywalled allocations once Sui Foundation publishes structured data, the paid-data budget opens, Tokenomist's free tier expands, or community publishes verified canonical addresses for on-chain vesting-object queries. **The SUI on-chain data surface is now structurally complete** (B-088 + B-089 v1 together): staking flow + validator concentration via `onchain.sui_validators`, plus partial unlock schedule via `onchain.sui_unlocks`. The next `/research SUI` session has materially more answer-able questions than the May 2026 session did — with the explicit limitation that VC-driven unlock pressure still requires manual estimation against published static tokenomics until B-115 ships.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260607_create_onchain_sui_unlocks.py` (onchain.sui_unlocks schema + index). `src/genkei/ingest/sui_unlocks.py` (new file: __NEXT_DATA__ extractor, parse_allocations with KNOWN_FREE_ALLOCATIONS gating, collect() entrypoint). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (PRIMARY_TABLES + RECURRING_ENDPOINTS registration for sui_unlocks.collect). `.github/workflows/sui-unlocks-daily.yml` (07:00 UTC daily cron on self-hosted Beelink). `tests/ingest/test_sui_unlocks.py` (new file, 28 tests). `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` (registry pin update). `docs/sources/sui-unlocks.md` (new file: per-source survey + "what would unblock full coverage" path). `docs/research/decisions/2026-05-20-sui-position-assessment.md` (Backlog implications #2 marked partially-resolved with explicit caveats). `docs/backlog.md` (B-089 retired; B-115 filed as v2 follow-up). Tests 1380 → 1416 (+36), ruff clean. Live verification on homelab DB: 85 rows landed for Community Reserves; `genkei query` returns 12.01M SUI unlocking in next 90 days from this allocation alone (caller must treat as partial).

### ~~B-086 — Map the full Chainlink staking surface (cross-source reconciliation)~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-07 (branch chainlink-staking, commits `17ead4d` + `fdc65eb` + PR #98 follow-up)
- **Outcome:** Closed the multi-pool reconciliation gap surfaced by the first B-082 backfill in May 2026 — but with one substantive correction to the spec along the way. The spec assumed DefiLlama tracks chainlink-staking across "the v0.1 community pool (legacy), a separate node-operator-only pool, and possibly other staking-related contracts" and that the v0.2 community pool was holding ~6.5M LINK (~$64M) against DefiLlama's $414.8M TVL — a 6.5× gap. Phase 1 investigation pulled DefiLlama's actual adapter source from `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/DefiLlama/DefiLlama-Adapters/main/projects/chainlink/index.js` and found a much cleaner picture: chainlink-staking is exactly 3 LINK-balance owners (no "other contracts" surprise), and **the v0.2 community pool now holds 40.9M LINK**, not 6.5M — the spec's discrepancy estimate was stale, captured during the early ramp window before the pool fully filled. Etherscan-probed contract identities: `CommunityStakingPool` (v0.2, 40.9M LINK, already covered as `chainlink-v02`), `OperatorStakingPool` (v0.2, 1.7M LINK, **the missing pool that B-086 ships**), and a legacy v0.1 `Staking` contract (0.46M LINK, <1% of total). **Implementation surface is still small**: adding the operator pool is a new `PoolConfig` entry (`chainlink-v02-operator`) reusing the shared v0.2 `Staked` / `Unstaked` / `UnbondingPeriodStarted` event topics, plus operator-only parser coverage for `OperatorRemoved(address,uint256,uint256)` and `Slashed(address,uint256,uint256,uint256)`. The v0.1 contract is INTENTIONALLY out of scope for B-086 and filed as **B-116** because (a) it emits different event signatures requiring `PoolConfig` extension with per-pool event-topic overrides + a separate parse path, and (b) the two v0.2 pools alone already reconcile within 3% of DefiLlama's TVL so the v0.1 contract isn't load-bearing for the reconciliation acceptance criterion. **Live backfill** initially landed chainlink-v02-operator events covering blocks 18671472 → 25238109 (Nov 2023 → Jun 2026); the PR #98 parser follow-up adds coverage for the 5 historical `OperatorRemoved` logs found by Etherscan topic scan, while `Slashed` currently has no records. **Reconciliation metric split:** contract-balance / TVL reconciliation remains event-derived `SUM(staked - unstaked)` and gives community 40,875,000 LINK + operator 1,731,903 LINK = **42,606,903 LINK captured**. Etherscan-probed live LINK balances are 40,875,544 + 1,731,903 = 42,607,447 — diff is 544 LINK (0.0013%) on the community pool from rounding in `amount_token`'s decimal storage, exact on the operator pool. Active operator principal is a separate research metric: it must subtract `OperatorRemoved` and `Slashed`, and must not double-subtract later `Unstaked` logs from removed-principal withdrawals. At our DB's LINK price of $7.68 (`coingecko.market_data` latest), captured contract balance = $327M vs DefiLlama's $338M — **3.4% gap**, well under the spec's 10% acceptance target. The remaining 3.4% gap is entirely explained by (a) the unwired v0.1 contract's 0.46M LINK (~$3.5M, ~1%) and (b) LINK price drift between our DB's coingecko snapshot and DefiLlama's TVL calc (~2%). **Operator pool flow behavior is structurally different from community pool**: 104+16 stake/unstake events vs the community pool's 17,488+3,141 — operator bonding/unbonding is institutional-frequency (event volume scales with operator-roster changes, not with retail flow). Active principal delta is meaningful there in a way capped-pool net flow is not, so the module docstring now separates contract-balance reconciliation from active-principal analysis. **Tests** (`tests/ingest/test_onchain_staking.py`): pin the v0.2 surface (both pools ship by default, addresses distinct so a typo wouldn't silently re-ingest the same events under two slugs, operator pool reuses `LINK_DECIMALS` + the community pool's `deployment_block`, module constants match the `PoolConfig` values), pin the operator-only topic hashes, and assert `OperatorRemoved` / `Slashed` parse as principal-reduction event rows. **Out-of-scope follow-up filed as B-116**: enable the v0.1 legacy contract via PoolConfig per-pool event-topic overrides + parser extension. **The full Chainlink staking surface is now structurally complete for the 99% of TVL** that matters; the remaining 1% (v0.1 unwind) is tracked but not load-bearing. The November 2026 `/reflect-decisions` cycle on any LINK position decision will have a materially more honest on-chain demand picture — operator pool bonding/removal/slashing events as a separate institutional-flow signal, plus the community pool's UnbondingPeriodStarted intent count (which trended from ~150/month in 2024 to ~400/month in 2025-2026, a 2.5× increase in stakers losing patience).
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/ingest/onchain_staking.py` (new `CHAINLINK_V02_OPERATOR_POOL_ADDRESS` + `CHAINLINK_V02_OPERATOR_POOL` PoolConfig + `CHAINLINK_V01_POOL_ADDRESS` constant; `DEFAULT_POOLS` now `[CHAINLINK_V02_POOL, CHAINLINK_V02_OPERATOR_POOL]`; deployed operator-only `OperatorRemoved` / `Slashed` topic constants; module docstring rewrite covering multi-pool surface + TVL reconciliation methodology + operator-vs-community active-principal distinction + v0.1 deferral). `tests/ingest/test_onchain_staking.py` (default-pool tests + operator principal-reduction topic/parser tests). `docs/backlog.md` (B-086 retired; B-116 filed for v0.1 enablement). Ruff clean and focused onchain-staking tests green. Live verification on homelab DB: initial operator pool backfill landed base staking events covering Nov 2023 → Jun 2026; Etherscan topic scan confirms 5 additional historical `OperatorRemoved` logs now covered by the parser; contract-balance reconciliation remains 42.61M LINK captured vs Etherscan's 42.61M (0.0013% diff) and DefiLlama's $338M TVL (3.4% gap explained by v0.1 contract + price drift).

### ~~B-106 — Etherscan whale-flow tracker (top-N ETH wallet net flow)~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-08 (branch eth-whale-flow, commits `182801b` + `90d0e7f` + `d0b4634` + `f8abea4` + `712d27a` + `c46f5f7`)
- **Outcome:** Closed the last unfilled research-session-named gap from the 2026-06-02 ETH session — "are large long-term ETH wallets net-selling?", the user's "OG sellers" framing. The implementation is a daily-snapshot ingester against Etherscan v2 (reusing the B-082 ETHERSCAN_API_KEY — no new secret) that lands one row per `(address, ts)` per day in `onchain.eth_whale_flows`. The `onchain.eth_whale_flows_aggregate` view rolls up per-category per-day so the headline question is a one-line `genkei whales --category exchange` call. **Implementation surface**: (1) New migration `c8e9f0a1b224` creates the `onchain.eth_whale_flows` table keyed on `(address, ts)` + the aggregate view. NUMERIC(38, 18) for ETH amounts (lossless against wei), NUMERIC(20, 2) for USD (cents precision is all anyone reads). CHECK constraint pins category to the four documented values mirroring the parser's gate so a typo fails at parse time, not at insert time. The aggregate view does `(ts AT TIME ZONE 'UTC')::date` so daily groupings are stable regardless of the Postgres session timezone. (2) New collector `src/genkei/ingest/etherscan_whale_flow.py` (582 lines) — per address per snapshot day: `account/balance` + `account/txlist` over the day's block range, filtered to `isError=0` (reverted txns move no real value), Σ(incoming) − Σ(outgoing) on `value` for net flow, count for tx_count. **Window-boundary trick**: today's snapshot uses (today-midnight, latest-head-block) instead of (today-midnight, tomorrow-midnight) because Etherscan rejects future-timestamp block lookups with "Block timestamp too far in the future" — the naive midnight-to-midnight approach broke on the very first live run. Historical/backfill days use midnight-to-midnight UTC both sides via `getblocknobytime`. ETH USD pricing pulls the latest price from `coingecko.market_data` via a single inline SELECT per run; USD columns are NULL when coingecko hasn't backfilled, ETH columns are still load-bearing. D-020 graceful skip when ETHERSCAN_API_KEY unset (run lands successfully with 0 rows + loud warning, matching B-082 + the original D-020 design from CoinGecko). Idempotent on the `(address, ts)` PK so same-day reruns overwrite earlier-in-day numbers with later ones. Backfill mode via `--since YYYY-MM-DD` walks the calendar one day at a time. (3) New CLI subcommand `genkei whales` (382 lines) with `--category` / `--address` / `--since` / `--until` / `--json` / `--list-addresses`. `--address` and `--category` are mutually exclusive (one drills per-wallet, the other aggregates). Category aliases (`cex`=exchange, `staking`=custodian, `treasury`=foundation) for ergonomic CLI use. **Trap caught during the CLI build**: this module originally had `from __future__ import annotations`, which under PEP 563 makes Typer unable to unpack the OptionInfo metadata at decoration time — `--json` was being silently auto-derived to `--json-out` from the parameter name. Removing the future import restored eager annotation evaluation. Worth pinning the pattern: any future Typer-decorated CLI module in this repo should NOT use `from __future__ import annotations`. (4) `EthWhaleAddressEntry` dataclass + watchlist parser support 0x-prefixed 40-char lowercased hex (Etherscan returns lowercase; the parser lowercases at read time so checksum casing in YAML doesn't matter). **YAML quirk** documented in the commit: unquoted `0x...` addresses parse as hex ints under PyYAML's YAML-1.1-compatible behavior, so all addresses in `watchlists.yml` are written as quoted strings. (5) v1 watchlist seed: 20 addresses across 3 categories — 2 foundation (EF + Vitalik), 3 custodian (Lido stETH + Beacon Deposit Contract + Lido/Curve), 15 exchange (Binance / Coinbase / Kraken / OKX / Bybit / Bitfinex / Gemini). Whale category intentionally empty at v1 because the provenance bar ("publicly-documented attribution beyond 'Lookonchain said so'") is higher than community attributions usually meet. (6) Daily cron in `etherscan-whales-daily.yml` at 14:00 UTC — mid-US-trading-day so the 24h window captures the prior 24h ending mid-day, matching when most CEX-flow research happens. workflow_dispatch input accepts a `since` for manual backfill via the GH Actions UI. (7) `genkei watchlist health` registers `eth_whale_flow` in both PRIMARY_TABLES and RECURRING_ENDPOINTS so OK/STALE/FAIL/MISSING/EMPTY tags surface alongside the other sources. (8) Methodology doc at `docs/sources/eth-whale-addresses.md` (91 lines) records the four criteria each candidate address must satisfy (public provenance, stable identity, material balance, behavior maps to the signal), the four category labels and how each one's flow sign should be read (the critical sign inversion: exchange-inflow = SELL pressure, not exchange buying), the v1 seed list with per-address provenance + balance-at-add-time, and the four hard limits the data exposes (incomplete address list, sign inversion on exchanges, rate-limit ceiling, 24h-boundary noise). **Live verification on homelab DB**: 20 rows landed for 2026-06-08. Headline aggregate (`genkei whales`) returned: exchange category +141,758 ETH net inflow across 28,332 transactions = **+$240M of sell-side pressure today**; custodian category +19,440 ETH (mostly Beacon Deposit Contract stake commitments + Lido stETH inflow); foundation category 0 ETH (neither EF nor Vitalik moved today). Top-flow address Coinbase 10 logged +72,487 ETH on 2,129 transactions — confirms the "users sending to CEX to sell" interpretation. **Known v1 limitation observed live**: Binance 14 hit the 10,000-tx page-size cap, so its day's flow may be under-reported for ultra-high-activity exchange addresses. Documented in the source + flagged for v2 if the under-reporting becomes material. **Tests** (`tests/ingest/test_etherscan_whale_flow.py` 25 new tests across 5 classes): `ModuleConstantsTests` pins SOURCE_NAME / COLLECT_ENDPOINT_LABEL / ETHERSCAN_API_KEY_ENV / ETH_DECIMALS; `WeiToEthTests` covers the lossless 18-decimal conversion across 1 ETH / 0.5 ETH / Binance-scale / zero; `ComputeNetFlowTests` (9 cases) pins the load-bearing arithmetic — pure inflow / pure outflow / mixed / reverted-tx skip / case-insensitive address match (Etherscan returns lowercase, watchlist might be checksum-cased) / self-to-self nets to zero with tx_count=1 (a subtle correctness case) / non-touching skip / malformed-value skip / empty list; `BuildSnapshotTests` (4 cases) pins USD columns derived only when price known + watchlist metadata carried through; `IterSnapshotDatesTests` + `UtcMidnightTests` pin calendar walking. `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` updated with `eth_whale_flow` in both registry pins. **Tests 1423 → 1453 (+30), ruff clean, full suite green** (1453 tests, 33 DB-integration skips). **The ETH-side institutional-flow surface is now structurally complete**: COT (B-031) gives weekly position changes, IBIT/ETHA snapshots (B-107) give signed ETF net flow, equity rel-strength (B-111/B-112) reads the crypto-equity proxy basket, and whale-flow (B-106) gives the on-chain "OG sellers" answer. The November 2026 `/reflect-decisions` cycle on any ETH position decision will have all four of these data layers to triangulate against.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260608_create_onchain_eth_whale_flows.py` (eth_whale_flows table + aggregate view). `src/genkei/ingest/etherscan_whale_flow.py` (new file: Etherscan v2 client + 24h window resolver + net-flow arithmetic + D-020 graceful skip). `src/genkei/cli/whales.py` (new file: per-category + per-address query + sign-convention disclaimer in every human render). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` (whales subcommand registration). `src/genkei/common/watchlist.py` (EthWhaleAddressEntry dataclass + load_watchlist parser with 0x-hex + category gating). `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml` (20-address v1 seed under eth_whale_addresses). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (PRIMARY_TABLES + RECURRING_ENDPOINTS registration for eth_whale_flow.collect). `.github/workflows/etherscan-whales-daily.yml` (14:00 UTC daily cron on self-hosted Beelink with optional since input). `tests/ingest/test_etherscan_whale_flow.py` (new file, 25 tests). `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` (registry pin update). `docs/sources/eth-whale-addresses.md` (new file: curation methodology + 4 hard-limit disclosures). `docs/backlog.md` (B-106 retired). Tests 1423 → 1453 (+30), ruff clean. Live verification on homelab DB: 20 rows landed for 2026-06-08 with the headline aggregate showing +141K ETH (+$240M) exchange-category net inflow = real sell pressure, +19K ETH custodian inflow = staking commitment, 0 ETH foundation flow.
- **PR #99 review follow-up:** txlist fetches now page until complete and filter by exact `timeStamp` window; raw balance/txlist payloads land in `meta.raw_blobs`; today's incremental flow uses a true rolling 24h window; historical backfills leave balance columns NULL rather than writing today's latest balance; USD flow uses the ETH price at or before the snapshot date; watchlist parsing fails fast on malformed whale-address config; CLI output preserves `Decimal` precision and carries the required horizon tag.

### ~~B-033 — GDELT news/event ingester~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-09 (branch gdelt, commits `1abb0c8` + `7a6f87e` + `01fd7fb` + `bb88cfb` + `bdd200c`)
- **Outcome:** Lands the news / topic / entity / tone dimension of the data lake that no prior source covered, unblocking B-043 (`genkei news`) and B-056 (news-sentiment-vs-next-day-returns experiment). GDELT 2.0 GKG publishes 15-minute CSV files with themes, entities, locations, and V1.5 tone fields; this collector filters those rows to watchlist matches before writing `gdelt.gkg`. The schema uses a TimescaleDB hypertable on `published_at`, composite PK `(published_at, gkg_record_id)`, GIN indexes on `themes` and `matched_assets`, compression after 30 days, and 365-day retention. Incremental mode anchors on `lastupdate.txt`; backfill mode walks full UTC days from `00:00` through `23:45`, rejects future `until` dates, and clamps `since` to the retention floor. Fresh CSVs land in `meta.raw_blobs` as `{"csv": ...}` before parsing, and re-runs copy cached raw blobs into the current ingest run before re-parsing. Watchlist matching covers equity names, crypto names with symbol fallback for short assets such as SUI, protocol names, and 13F filer names. Per-slot 404s remain benign-empty because GDELT occasionally skips a 15-minute file upstream.
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260609_create_gdelt_gkg.py` (`gdelt.gkg` hypertable + retention/compression). `src/genkei/ingest/gdelt.py` (HTTPS GDELT URL builder, `latest_gkg_timestamp`, 15-minute timestamp generators, watchlist match-term builder, row parsers, raw-blob cache/store/copy path, 404-as-empty fetch loop, bulk upsert, `collect`, `collect_backfill`, argparse). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` and `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` register `gdelt` in health monitoring. `tests/ingest/test_gdelt.py` covers parsing, SUI symbol fallback, asymmetric lat/lon failures, date-window generation, future-`until` validation, raw-blob store/copy behavior, URL formatting, `lastupdate.txt`, zip decompression, CSV filtering, and retention constants. `tests/ingest/test_gdelt_integration.py` covers filtered upsert, ingest-run success metadata, and same-window idempotency against testcontainers. `.github/workflows/gdelt-daily.yml` runs daily at 14:30 UTC and documents `since` precedence over `hours`.

### ~~B-043 — Implement `genkei news` subcommand~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-09 (branch genkei-news, commits `6ae3a5c` + `6f5bf0a` + `d101574`)
- **Outcome:** Closes Phase 3 — the CLI surface moves 21/22 → **22/22 real subcommands** (the last typed surface was a stub pending the GDELT data this branch finally consumes). Builds directly on top of B-033's `gdelt.gkg` lake, which yesterday landed the news / topic / entity / tone dimension nothing else in the lake covered. **Surface** (`genkei news`): seven combinable filters — `--ticker AAPL` (watchlist equity), `--asset BTC` (watchlist crypto), `--theme ECON_BITCOIN` (GIN-array contains), `--topic "<free text>"` (case-insensitive substring across `document_identifier` + `array_to_string(themes, ' ')`), `--since` / `--until` (UTC date window on `published_at`), `--tone-min N` / `--tone-max N` (V1.5 average tone gate), `--source nytimes.com` (case-insensitive exact match on `source_common_name`), `--limit N` (default 30 clusters), `--json`, `--config` for watchlist override. Watchlist resolution routes through the canonical `load_watchlist` and per-asset-class lookup helpers; unknown ticker / asset fails loudly rather than silently matching zero rows. `--ticker` and `--asset` are mutually exclusive. Tone-min > tone-max and since > until both rejected at parse time. **Clustering choice — lightweight v1 (decided upfront)**: GROUP BY `(published_at::date UTC, source_common_name)`, sort by `article_count DESC` with tie-break on day DESC, truncate to `--limit`. Theme-aware clustering (Jaccard over the themes array, "10 stories about AAPL earnings across multiple sources") was the explicit alternative and is filed as a deferred follow-up — the raw article rows stay queryable via `genkei query` for ad-hoc shapes the typed surface doesn't express. Picked v1 because the data is fresh enough that we'd be guessing about real cluster shapes anyway; ships sooner, reads cleaner, easy to iterate on once the cron has accumulated a real history. **Cluster shape**: each emits `day` (ISO date), `source` (lower-case domain), `article_count`, `mean_tone` (mean over articles whose tone is non-NULL; None when every article in the bucket is NULL-tone — explicitly distinguished from "0.0 mean"), `matched_assets` (set-union of all per-article matched labels across the cluster, sorted for stable output), `sample_urls` (up to `SAMPLE_URLS_PER_CLUSTER`=3 most-recent unique URLs — dedup within cluster catches GDELT's occasional duplicate landings of the same URL across adjacent 15-min slots). **SQL shape**: single SELECT against `gdelt.gkg` with WHERE filters AND-joined; GIN index on `matched_assets` keeps `@> ARRAY[label]` cheap; `(published_at AT TIME ZONE 'UTC')::date` for the `--until` upper bound so callers don't have to think about timezone-shifted boundaries. **Article pool cap**: 2,000 — the default `--limit 30` clusters needs roughly 30×60 = 1,800 articles to produce meaningful per-cluster counts after sort+truncate; pinned by `PoolCapTests`. **Human formatter**: header line surfaces the active filter summary, one row per cluster with article count + mean tone (±%) + matched assets + indented sample URLs; horizon footer matches the `genkei whales` style ("cross-sleeve | research/news — consume alongside prices, filings, on-chain"). Empty-result path returns a help hint pointing at `python3 -m genkei.ingest.gdelt --hours 24` rather than a bare 0-row table. **JSON shape**: `{summary: {ticker, asset, theme, topic, since, until, tone_min, tone_max, source, pool_size}, clusters: [...]}` — pool_size lets the agent know whether the pool cap was hit (a hint to narrow the filters). **Stub-pointer test removed**: `tests/cli/test_main.py::CliHelpTests::test_stub_subcommand_exits_nonzero_with_backlog_pointer` literally asserted "news is still a stub (B-043)". Its premise no longer holds, so it goes — no other stubs remain in the CLI surface. **Tests 1525 → 1556 (+31)**: 7 test classes covering `_resolve_watchlist_label` (None / ticker / asset / mutex / unknown-fails-loud paths), `_build_article_query` (matched_label + theme GIN-contains, topic ILIKE-on-URL-and-themes-array, since lower-bound and until UTC-date upper-bound, tone window, case-insensitive source match, stable param order across combined filters), `_cluster_articles` (date+source grouping, tie-break sort, tone-mean over non-NULL, matched_assets union+sort, sample URL dedup + cap, --limit truncate, NULL source / NULL date defensive paths), `_day_sort_key` encoding, `_format_clusters_human` (empty hint + populated render with horizon footer + sample URL bullets), end-to-end `main()` invocation paths with `_fetch_articles` mocked (JSON payload contains both summary and clusters; --since > --until rejected; --tone-min > --tone-max rejected), and a `PoolCapTests` floor pin. **Docs caught up**: README CLI surface gains the `genkei news` row; `docs/architecture.md` Last updated line moves to 2026-06-09, Phase 3 roadmap promoted to **22/22 done**, code-layer tree's `cli/` block gains the news row, the B-056 note flips from "blocked on B-033 GDELT" to "now unblocked — gdelt.gkg lake is live and `genkei news` is the query surface". **Live verification deferred** — the cron-firing for B-033's collector hasn't happened yet on this workstation's clock, so `gdelt.gkg` is empty locally; `python -m genkei.cli news --asset BTC` will return the empty-help-hint output until either the cron fires or `python -m genkei.ingest.gdelt --hours 24` is run manually. The CLI's behaviour against a populated table is exercised end-to-end via the mocked-`_fetch_articles` path in `CmdInvocationTests`; a real live smoke is the natural next step once the table has rows. **Phase 3 closure context**: this completes the work D-019 + B-037/038/039 originally scoped — the typed CLI is the agent's stable data interface, every command supports `--json`, every command resolves human-friendly tickers through the canonical watchlist, every command has unit tests + a help string. **Deferred follow-ups (filed implicitly via the cluster-shape decision)**: theme-aware Jaccard clustering as a `--cluster-mode themes` flag if (date, source) proves too coarse; a `--no-cluster` raw-mode that just dumps the underlying article rows for cases where the human wants the long tail; B-056 (news sentiment vs next-day returns experiment) — the input data + query surface are both now in place, making it the natural Phase 5 follow-up.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/cli/news.py` (new file: `_resolve_watchlist_label`, `_build_article_query`, `_fetch_articles`, `_cluster_articles`, `_day_sort_key`, `_format_clusters_human`, `news_cmd` Typer entry point; module docstring documents the filters + the explicit lightweight-vs-theme-aware clustering tradeoff). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` (replaces `app.add_typer(_stub("news", "B-043"), name="news")` with `app.command("news", help=...)(news.news_cmd)`; surface comment block bumped `B-043, stub` → `B-043 ✓`; imports include the new `news` module). `tests/cli/test_news.py` (new file, 31 tests across 7 classes — `ResolveWatchlistLabelTests`, `BuildArticleQueryTests`, `ClusterArticlesTests`, `DaySortKeyTests`, `FormatClustersHumanTests`, `CmdInvocationTests`, `PoolCapTests`). `tests/cli/test_main.py` (drops the now-obsolete `test_stub_subcommand_exits_nonzero_with_backlog_pointer` — its premise no longer holds). `README.md` (CLI table gains the `genkei news --asset BTC --since 2026-05-01` row). `docs/architecture.md` (Last updated line, Phase 3 promoted to 22/22, code-layer tree's `cli/` block gains news, B-056 unblocked note). `docs/backlog.md` (B-043 retired). Tests 1525 → 1556 (+31), ruff clean.

### ~~B-056 — Experiment: news sentiment vs next-day returns~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-09 (branch news-sentiment, commits `87881d3` + `2a29f08` + `3cee373` + `e04540a`)
- **Outcome:** First **cross-source feedback-loop experiment** that consumes both halves of the news layer shipped yesterday — `gdelt.gkg` (B-033) on the sentiment side, `coingecko.market_data` / `yahoo.candles` on the return side — and asks the load-bearing question: does the news data we now collect have any predictive relationship with forward asset returns? Phase 5 advances 8/11 → **9/11 detectors shipped**; the experiment is the natural next-link in the chain after B-043 because the same `matched_assets` filter that lets the agent query news clusters now also lets the experiment slice tone time-series per asset without re-deriving labels. **Scope decision — v1 lightweight (decided upfront, mirrors the B-043 (date, source) call)**: single-asset analysis, no macro-regime conditioning, no market-hours alignment for equities, no weekend-effect handling, no volume-weighted tone aggregation. The experiment's purpose at v1 is "does the signal exist at all," not "is it tradeable under regime X" — premature optimization on the latter blocks ever shipping the former. Each non-goal is documented in the module docstring with the explicit alternative + the rationale for deferring. Cross-asset "does negative-tone day predict drawdown for any tracked asset" mode is the natural v2 follow-up. **Pure-function pipeline** (`src/genkei/experiments/news_sentiment.py`): `ArticleRow` / `SentimentPoint` / `ReturnPoint` / `AlignedPoint` / `QuartileMean` / `CorrelationReport` dataclasses model each stage's typed shape; `aggregate_daily_sentiment` groups GKG articles by `(asset, day)` with NULL-aware mean-tone math (a bucket where every article has NULL tone still emits a row carrying the article_count signal but mean_tone=None — explicitly distinguished from "0.0 mean"); `compute_daily_returns` walks a (days, closes) series computing day-over-day pct returns with defensive None-close-passes-through-without-poisoning-prev-denom + zero-prior-close-yields-None guards; `align_sentiment_with_returns` left-joins sentiment day-N against return day-(N + horizon_days) with O(1) lookup via a (asset, ts) index dict, drops rows below `min_articles_per_day` and rows with calendar-gap return-side misses (common for equities over weekends/holidays); `compute_correlation` emits both Pearson + Spearman (Pearson catches linear, Spearman catches monotonic-not-linear — the practical value of having both surfaced upfront in the docstring), plus a per-tone-quartile mean forward-return breakdown that answers the practical "do the most-negative news days predict drawdowns?" question without imposing a functional form. **Confidence floor**: `MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_SIGNAL = 30` — the conventional threshold for "approximately normal" sampling distributions of correlation coefficients under standard assumptions. Below the floor the report returns `status="insufficient_data"` with all-None statistics. This is load-bearing — better to surface "we don't have enough data yet" loud than to publish a meaningless correlation from a 5-day sample. The GDELT cron started 2026-06-09, so this experiment will honestly return `insufficient_data` until ~mid-July 2026 unless a multi-month GDELT backfill seeds the lake first. **Statistical helpers** (pure Python, no numpy/scipy dep since neither is in the project deps — pinned by inspecting pyproject before writing): `_pearson_correlation` returns None on zero-variance inputs rather than 0, `_spearman_correlation` = Pearson on rank-transformed inputs, `_rank_with_ties` does average-rank for tied values so Spearman handles repeated tone (an article cluster on a flat-tone day doesn't break the math). `_quartile_means` splits the tone-sorted series into N equal-sized buckets with the last bucket grabbing the remainder so integer-division truncation doesn't silently drop trailing rows; ties at quartile boundaries split by sort position (deterministic). **Lake loaders**: `load_articles_for_asset(asset_label, since, until)` reads `gdelt.gkg` filtered by `matched_assets @> ARRAY[asset_label]` (the GIN index from B-033 keeps this cheap) with UTC-date window filters mirroring B-043's `(published_at AT TIME ZONE 'UTC')::date` pattern; `load_price_returns(asset, asset_class, coingecko_id, since, until)` reads `coingecko.market_data.price_usd` for crypto and `yahoo.candles.COALESCE(adj_close, close)` for equity — `adj_close` is the split/dividend-adjusted close that's the right field for total-return analysis, with `close` as a fallback for the rare older row missing adj_close so the experiment doesn't silently drop those days. **CLI** (`src/genkei/cli/news_sentiment.py` → `genkei news-sentiment`): `--ticker` (equity) / `--asset` (crypto) mutex; the lookup resolves the asset via the canonical watchlist + returns `(asset_label, asset_class, coingecko_id)` so the loader doesn't have to re-derive routing. `--since` / `--until` UTC date window, `--horizon-days N` (default 1 = "next-day" per B-056's original framing), `--min-articles-per-day N` (default 3 — single/two-article days are mostly noise; 3 is the smallest sample where one outlier doesn't dominate the daily mean), `--json`. Human formatter renders the headline Pearson + Spearman with sign-explicit format strings, a per-tone-quartile table with `n` / `mean_tone` / `mean_forward_return_pct` columns; insufficient-data path includes a backfill hint pointing at `python3 -m genkei.ingest.gdelt --backfill --since YYYY-MM-DD` so the agent knows what to do. JSON payload mirrors the `CorrelationReport` shape with `asset_class` denormalized in for downstream consumers. Horizon footer explicitly tags this as "experimental — predictive signal, not a trade trigger" to discourage anyone (including the agent) from treating an insufficient-data day's near-zero correlation as a green light. **Acceptance criteria divergence from the original 2026 backlog text**: original called for "Notebook produces correlation table + a chart" — but D-017 (Claude Code over notebooks) explicitly chose the experiments-module + CLI shape over notebooks, and every Phase 5 experiment since (`insider_clusters`, `protocol_revenue`, `relative_strength`, `macro_regime`, `tvl_drawdown`, `eight_k_impact`, `crowding_monitor`, `watchlist_scoring`) has followed that pattern. Following the established pattern keeps the correlation surface composable with `/research` (the agent can `genkei news-sentiment --asset BTC --json` mid-research-session) instead of trapped in a notebook the agent can't ingest. The "chart" half of the original acceptance criterion is intentionally deferred — surfacing rich plots in CLI output is brittle; if the visual matters more than the table, the agent reads the JSON and renders the chart inside a research session via Claude's plotting tools rather than a hard-coded matplotlib in the CLI. **Tests 1557 → 1595 (+38)**: 30 experiment tests across 9 classes (`AggregateDailySentimentTests` pinning the per-(asset, day) grouping + NULL-tone-with-non-NULL-count path + UTC-day boundary correctness + independent pos/neg mean math; `ComputeDailyReturnsTests` pinning the first-day-None / zero-prior-close-guard / None-close-preserves-prior-denom / length-mismatch-raises paths; `AlignSentimentWithReturnsTests` pinning horizon=1 and horizon=5 join shapes, min-articles drop, NULL-tone drop, missing-return-day drop, horizon validation; `PearsonCorrelationTests` pinning perfect ±1 / zero-variance None / uncorrelated near-zero; `SpearmanCorrelationTests` showing monotonic-nonlinear catches what Pearson misses + average-rank ties + all-ties returns None; `QuartileMeansTests` pinning equal-size when divisible, last-bucket-grabs-remainder when not, invalid n_quartiles raises; `ComputeCorrelationTests` exercising the insufficient_data path at floor-1 and the ok path at floor with perfect-correlation surfacing both Pearson and Spearman; `ConstantsTests` pinning the three load-bearing constants — `DEFAULT_HORIZON_DAYS=1`, `DEFAULT_MIN_ARTICLES_PER_DAY=3`, `MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_SIGNAL=30` — so future changes have to be explicit, especially the observation floor which guards against publishing meaningless small-sample correlations). 8 CLI tests across 2 classes (`ResolveAssetOrExitTests` covering equity/crypto routing + mutex guards + neither-passed-fails-loud + both-passed-fails-loud; `CmdInvocationTests` running the full pipeline via `main(["news-sentiment", ...])` with the lake loaders mocked to assert the insufficient_data help-hint renders, the ok-status renders Pearson/Spearman/quartile blocks, the JSON payload carries `asset_class`/`status`/`pearson`/`spearman`/4 quartiles, and --since > --until is rejected). Ruff clean across all touched files. **`zip(strict=True)` workaround**: ruff B905 wants explicit strict on zip(); Python 3.10+ supports it but the workstation venv is 3.9 (G-002 — known limitation). Both call sites already have explicit length guards, so the strict is redundant safety; dropped with `# noqa: B905` comments instead so local tests pass without holding production code hostage to a stale venv. CI runs 3.12 where strict would work fine. **Live verification deferred**: at branch close-out time `gdelt.gkg` has not yet accumulated enough days for the experiment to clear the 30-observation floor on any asset; `genkei news-sentiment --asset BTC` returns `status=insufficient_data` with the backfill hint, exactly as designed. The mocked-loader path in `CmdInvocationTests` exercises the full happy path end-to-end at synthetic n=31 — confidence is high that the math is right; the live signal lands organically as the cron accumulates real days. **Deferred follow-ups (filed implicitly via the v1 non-goals)**: macro-regime conditioning (would let queries like "does negative tone predict drawdowns *during risk_off regimes specifically*"); market-hours alignment for equities (overnight news vs intraday news effect); weekend-effect modeling (Monday returns get dropped silently in v1 because the alignment can't find Saturday/Sunday returns); volume-weighted tone aggregation (a 10-article tone-5 day and a 100-article tone-5 day weight identically today); cross-asset mode; a charting helper for `/research` to render quartile bars without re-implementing the math.
- **Evidence:** `src/genkei/experiments/news_sentiment.py` (new file, ~440 lines: dataclasses + `aggregate_daily_sentiment` / `compute_daily_returns` / `align_sentiment_with_returns` / `compute_correlation` pure functions + `_pearson_correlation` / `_spearman_correlation` / `_rank_with_ties` / `_quartile_means` stat helpers + `load_articles_for_asset` / `load_price_returns` lake loaders). `src/genkei/cli/news_sentiment.py` (new file, ~280 lines: `news_sentiment_cmd` Typer entry point + `_resolve_asset_or_exit` watchlist routing + `_compute_report` pipeline composer + `_format_human` / `_quartile_to_dict` / `_report_to_dict` output formatters). `src/genkei/cli/__init__.py` (registers the `news-sentiment` command + adds the surface-comment row; imports the new module). `tests/experiments/test_news_sentiment.py` (30 unit tests across 9 classes — see above). `tests/cli/test_news_sentiment.py` (8 unit tests across 2 classes — see above). `README.md` (CLI table gains the `genkei news-sentiment --asset BTC` row). `docs/architecture.md` (Last updated line, Phase 5 promoted to 9/11, code-layer tree's `experiments/` and `cli/` blocks gain news_sentiment / news-sentiment). `docs/backlog.md` (B-056 retired). Tests 1557 → 1595 (+38), ruff clean.

### ~~B-029 — BEA ingester~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-10 (branch bea-ingester, commits `9434475` + `2382348` + `0107e26` + `1147a5f` + `ee3be0f` + `f5464a4`)
- **Outcome:** **Closes the largest remaining gap in macro inputs** — the macro regime classifier (B-059) currently runs on 4 FRED series (DGS10, BAMLH0A0HYM2, VIXCLS, DTWEXBGS) which is rates/credit/vol/FX but completely misses the real-economy growth dimension. BEA's NIPA dataset adds GDP, personal income, PCE (Fed's spending gauge), PCE-core inflation (Fed's preferred inflation gauge), corporate profits, and the savings rate — every macro signal a research session would actually want from BEA. Direct downstream consumer (`experiments/macro_regime.py`) already exists, so the new data lands somewhere immediately useful instead of sitting in the lake waiting for an experiment. **Scope decisions (all three confirmed upfront)**: (1) **NIPA-only for v1** — the other 12 BEA datasets (MNE, FixedAssets, ITA, IIP, Regional, GDPbyIndustry, ...) are deferred per the explicit design call; every macro signal we'd actually want from BEA lives in NIPA, and the curated v1 watchlist exercises it. (2) **Latest-only, NOT vintage-aware** — BEA's API has no vintage-date parameter the way FRED's `realtime_start` does; revisions overwrite in place at the source. The migration's PK omits a vintage column to match. v2 vintage-awareness would add `fetched_at_date` to the PK if we ever need a private revision trail. (3) **Curated 10-line watchlist** rather than "every NIPA line" — the lake stays focused on the lines that actually feed research and the regime classifier. **Schema** (migration `e0f1a2b33446`): new `bea` schema with `bea.series` (entity dim, PK `series_id`) and `bea.observations` (time-series fact, hypertable on `ts` with 90-day chunks matching FRED, compression > 30 days with `segmentby=series_id`). `series_id` is the composite `<table_id>:<line_number>` key (e.g. `T10101:1`) since NIPA series are scoped per-table; bare line numbers aren't globally unique and bare table ids don't identify a single line. **`frequency` in the PK** is load-bearing — BEA returns the same NIPA line at quarterly AND annual cadences depending on the request and both are research-useful (quarterly for high-frequency signals, annual for chart-friendly long views); the PK lets both coexist without clobbering. CHECK constraints pin `frequency IN ('Q', 'A', 'M')` and `line_number >= 0`. Provenance trio (`source_endpoint`, `fetched_at`, `ingest_run_id`) on every fact row matches the lake-wide D-005 convention. **Collector** (`src/genkei/ingest/bea.py`): hits `https://apps.bea.gov/api/data/?UserID=<key>&method=GetData&datasetname=NIPA&TableName=<id>&Frequency=<f>&Year=ALL&ResultFormat=JSON` — one URL per **unique `(table_id, frequency)` tuple**, NOT per (table_id, line_number). The watchlist groups multiple lines per table (T20100 has lines 1 / 24 / 35; T20804 has lines 1 / 25); fetching whole tables and filtering at the normalizer is 7 HTTP calls instead of 10, simpler error recovery, lower partial-state risk. Rate limit: 2 req/s (BEA publishes no documented cap; this is the polite default we use for "be reasonable" free APIs). **API-key redaction mirrors FRED's G-015 fix** — `UserID=<key>` lives in the query string, so URLs landed in `meta.raw_blobs.url` go through `_redact_key` to replace the key with `***` before insert. The audit table stays safe to share for replay/debug. **200-OK-with-error envelope detection** — bad table-id / missing-key returns HTTP 200 with `{"BEAAPI": {"Error": ...}}` or `{"BEAAPI": {"Results": {"Error": ...}}}` (BEA's nested shape); the collector detects both, extracts the `APIErrorDescription` for the partial-endpoints log, and records the target as a failure rather than treating the 200 as success. **Normalizer** (`src/genkei/normalize/bea.py`): per-blob dispatch by `bea_<table_lower>_<freq_lower>` endpoint-name prefix; loads the watchlist + builds a `(table_lower, freq_lower) → set[line_number]` index for O(1) per-blob filter lookup; for each watched line on the table, emits a series row + an observation row keyed on `(series_id, ts, frequency)`. **TimePeriod parser** handles BEA's three documented period formats: `"2024Q1"` (quarter → period-start UTC: 2024-01-01), `"2024M03"` (month-start: 2024-03-01), `"2024"` (annual → Jan 1). Quarter > 4, month > 12, and 3/5-digit "year" strings all return None defensively. **Missing-value sentinel `"..."`** is parsed as None — the row still emits so consumers know BEA *had* a row, just no value (mirrors FRED's `"."` G-017 handling). **Thousands-separated DataValues** (`"23,128.3"`) — BEA's nominal-dollar series come with commas; strip before float conversion. **Frequency-mismatch guard** — if the collector asked for `Q` and a row parses as `A` (shouldn't happen but the assertion protects the PK invariant), log a warning and drop the row rather than risk a PK collision. **Watchlist seed** — 10 NIPA lines across 6 tables, deliberately curated to fill the three dimensions FRED can't reach: growth (T10101:1 Real GDP %Δ, T10105:1 nominal GDP, T70100:5 Real GDP per capita — annual), income/spending (T20100:1 Personal Income, T20100:24 PCE total, T20100:35 Savings rate as %DPI, T20302:1 Real PCE %Δ), inflation (T20804:1 PCE headline, T20804:25 **PCE Core — Fed's preferred inflation gauge**), earnings (T11400:4 Corporate Profits w/ IVA+CCAdj). All quarterly except per-capita GDP which is naturally annual. **`BeaSeriesEntry` dataclass + parser block** in `src/genkei/common/watchlist.py`; `find_bea(series_id)` lookup helper on `Watchlist`; per-entry dedup on `(table_id, line_number, frequency)` and explicit frequency-set validation against `{Q, A, M}` at parse time. **Health integration** — `bea` registered in `PRIMARY_TABLES` (pointing at `bea.observations`) and `RECURRING_ENDPOINTS` (with both `collect` and `normalize` since BEA follows FRED's two-stage raw-blob → upsert pattern). `ExpectationsRegistryTests` updated to pin both registries so a silent drop fails CI rather than being discovered weeks later. **Daily cron** (`bea-daily.yml`) at **11:30 UTC** on the self-hosted Beelink runner — 30 min after FRED-daily so the two free-API macro pulls don't double-load the runner's morning slot. Daily polling is overkill given BEA's quarterly release cadence (advance estimate ~30 days after quarter end, second/third estimates ~60/~90 days), but matches FRED's "just upsert every day, the schema is idempotent" pattern and we get free freshness monitoring via `genkei watchlist health`. **Tests 1595 → 1645 (+50, all unit), ruff clean**: 23 collector tests across 8 classes (module constants, TableTarget blob-endpoint shape, load_targets dedup + sort + empty-section guard, URL builder param ordering, API-key redaction, BEA error-envelope detection across canonical-success / top-level-error / nested-Results-error / list-wrapped-error / missing-Results / non-dict shapes, _extract_bea_error covers all four extraction paths, require_api_key env-present/env-missing paths); 26 normalizer tests across 4 classes (parse_bea_value covering numeric strings / thousands-separators / `...` / empty / garbage / native numerics / None; parse_time_period covering all four quarters / annual / monthly / invalid quarter / invalid month / 3-and-5-digit years / garbage types; _parse_note_refs splitter; normalize_table covering canonical row → series + observations / watchlist-filter drops unwatched lines / missing-value → None / within-blob dedup last-wins / frequency-mismatch drops with warning / 5 defensive malformed-payload returns / thousands-separator passthrough / annual-payload happy path). All 50 tests pass against synthetic payloads; **live verification deferred** until `BEA_API_KEY` is registered + added to GH Actions secrets and the cron fires — same operational gate as FRED's first run. **Docs** — `docs/sources/bea.md` (~170 lines) records the v1 10-line watchlist by dimension, the 12 deferred datasets, the endpoint contract (URL shape, response envelope, TimePeriod formats), the schema with `frequency` PK rationale, six v1 limitations a consumer should know (latest-only, no backfill flag, `...` sentinel, thousands-sep values, 200-OK-with-error envelope, API-key redaction), how it runs (cron + manual replay + health integration), the current query path (`genkei query` over `bea.observations`), and the natural follow-ups (B-066 regime classifier extension, v2 vintage-awareness, future typed CLI surface). **Deferred follow-ups** (filed implicitly via the v1 scope calls): typed `genkei macro --source bea` or standalone `genkei bea` CLI subcommand (today's path is `genkei query` + raw SQL — works for the agent but adds friction); v2 vintage-aware schema (`fetched_at_date` in the PK) if a research session ever needs as-of backtests against historically-known BEA values; remaining 12 BEA datasets (MNE / FixedAssets / ITA / IIP / Regional / GDPbyIndustry / UnderlyingGDPbyIndustry / InputOutput / IntlServTrade / APIDatasetMetaData / IIPMaDM); B-066 regime classifier extension to consume BEA growth/income/spending/inflation inputs once enough history accumulates (the cron upserts every day starting tomorrow). **Phase 2 progress**: this advances the ingester surface further toward "every meaningful free macro source landed" — Treasury Fiscal Data (B-030), EIA energy (B-032), and per-source docs (B-036) are the remaining Phase 2 items in this priority tier; everything else is paid-data gated (B-084) or TOS-blocked (B-104).
- **Evidence:** `migrations/versions/20260610_create_bea_schema.py` (`bea` schema, `bea.series`, `bea.observations` hypertable + compression policy). `src/genkei/common/watchlist.py` (`BeaSeriesEntry` dataclass with `series_id` property + `find_bea` lookup + `bea:` parser block with `(table_id, line_number, frequency)` dedup). `src/genkei/data/watchlists.yml` (new `bea:` section, 10 curated NIPA lines across 6 tables under three dimension comment blocks). `src/genkei/ingest/bea.py` (collector: `TableTarget` dataclass with blob-endpoint shape, `load_targets` watchlist-driven target dedup + sort, `require_api_key` env reader with signup hint, `build_table_url` with stable param order, `_redact_key` mirroring FRED's G-015 fix, `collect` orchestrator wrapping `db.ingest_run`, `_fetch_target` with full failure-mode coverage, `_is_bea_error_envelope` + `_extract_bea_error` detecting BEA's 200-OK-with-error responses across all three documented shapes, argparse + `__main__` for `python -m`). `src/genkei/normalize/bea.py` (normalizer: `parse_bea_value` handling `"..."` sentinel + thousands-separators + native numerics, `parse_time_period` covering all three TimePeriod formats with defensive validation, `_parse_note_refs` splitter, `normalize_table` per-blob parser with watchlist-line filter + within-blob dedup + frequency-mismatch drop, `latest_collector_run_id` + `fetch_raw_blobs` shared lookup helpers, `_build_watched_lines_index` watchlist-derived filter cache, `normalize` orchestrator with series-dedup-across-blobs last-wins, argparse + `__main__`). `src/genkei/cli/watchlist.py` (`PRIMARY_TABLES` + `RECURRING_ENDPOINTS` gain `bea` rows; comment block explains the collect+normalize cadence rationale). `tests/cli/test_watchlist_cmd.py` (`ExpectationsRegistryTests` updated to include `bea` in both registry pins). `tests/ingest/test_bea.py` (23 unit tests across 8 classes). `tests/normalize/test_bea.py` (26 unit tests across 4 classes). `.github/workflows/bea-daily.yml` (daily cron at 11:30 UTC on `[self-hosted, beelink]`; reads `BEA_API_KEY` from GH Actions secrets; same collect → parse-id → normalize-with-source-run-id shape as fred-daily). `docs/sources/bea.md` (~170 lines: v1 coverage by dimension, deferred datasets list, endpoint contract, schema with `frequency` PK rationale, six v1 limitations, how-it-runs section, query path, follow-ups). `docs/backlog.md` (B-029 retired). Tests 1595 → 1645 (+50), ruff clean.

### ~~B-030 — Treasury Fiscal Data ingester~~
- **Resolved:** 2026-06-11 (branch fiscal-data, commits `bb96744` + `31d4242` + `56607c6`)
- **Outcome:** Lands the **public-debt + Treasury cash + cost-of-debt** dimensions of the macro stack — completes the fiscal-side companion to FRED (rates/credit/vol/FX, B-028) and BEA (real-economy growth, B-029). v1 ships **9 curated series across 4 endpoints** of the open `api.fiscaldata.treasury.gov` API. Three load-bearing dimensions are wired: **debt level** (daily total / public-held / intragov via `debt_to_penny`), **Treasury cash** (daily TGA closing balance — load-bearing liquidity signal via `operating_cash_balance`), and **cost of debt** (monthly interest expense via `interest_expense` + weighted-avg yield per security class via `avg_interest_rates`). **Scope decisions**: (1) **No auth required** — Treasury Fiscal Data is fully open, no API key, no registration, no key-redaction concerns (departs from FRED + BEA which both need keys). (2) **Latest-only, NOT vintage-aware** — matches BEA's B-029 call; Treasury revises in place at the source so the migration's PK is `(series_id, ts)` with no vintage column. v2 vintage-awareness would add `fetched_at_date` if needed. (3) **Time-series scope only** — `auctions_query` is explicitly deferred to v2 because it's event-shaped (per-auction high_yield + bid_to_cover + security_term + total_tendered) and warrants a separate `treasury.auctions` event table, not the `(series_id, ts, value)` time-series schema. (4) **Curated 9-series watchlist** rather than "every Treasury field" — the lake stays focused on what feeds research and the regime classifier. **Schema** (migration `f1a2b3c44557`): new `treasury` schema with `treasury.series` (entity dim, PK `series_id` TEXT) and `treasury.observations` (time-series fact, hypertable on `ts` with 90-day chunks matching FRED + BEA, compression > 30 days with `segmentby=series_id`). `series_id` is a **friendly TEXT key chosen in the watchlist** (e.g. `TOTAL_PUBLIC_DEBT`, `TGA_CLOSING_BAL`, `AVG_RATE_BILLS`) — unlike BEA where the source publishes a structured key (`T10101:1:Q`), Treasury surfaces dozens of fields per row across multiple endpoints, so a human-curated key reads better at query time and stays stable if Fiscal Data ever renames an internal field. **`row_filter` is JSONB** on `treasury.series` so consumers can see at a glance which row of a multi-row endpoint a series projects from (e.g. `TGA_CLOSING_BAL` selects `account_type = "Treasury General Account (TGA) Closing Balance"`). **`frequency` is NOT in the PK** (departs from BEA's `(series_id, ts, frequency)`) — each Treasury series is locked to a single cadence by construction (`debt_to_penny` is daily, `interest_expense` is monthly); same line at two cadences would be two watchlist entries with distinct `series_id`s. CHECK constraint pins `frequency IN ('D', 'W', 'M', 'Q', 'A')`. **Collector** (`src/genkei/ingest/treasury.py`): hits `https://api.fiscaldata.treasury.gov/services/api/fiscal_service<endpoint>?sort=record_date&page[size]=10000&page[number]=N&format=json` — **one URL family per unique endpoint, NOT per series**. The three `debt_to_penny` columns and the four `avg_interest_rates` rows fold into one HTTP fetch per endpoint; the normalizer filters down to each watched `(value_field, row_filter)` tuple at parse time. 4 endpoint fetches per run instead of 9. **Pagination**: `EndpointTarget._fetch_all_pages` loops every page using the response's `meta.total-pages` as the source of truth, with a short-page fallback when `meta.total-pages` is absent and a `MAX_PAGES=200` runaway guard. The combined payload merges every page's `data` array into a single object so the normalizer sees one canonical blob per endpoint. **Rate limit**: 2 req/s (Treasury publishes no documented cap; same "be polite" default we use for BEA + other free APIs). **Blob-endpoint slug** (`treasury_v2_accounting_od_debt_to_penny__record_date`) reverse-maps to the endpoint path + date field so the normalizer can dispatch deterministically. **Normalizer** (`src/genkei/normalize/treasury.py`): per-blob dispatch by reverse-slug lookup; loads the watchlist + groups series by endpoint/date-field tuple for O(1) per-blob dispatch. For each row in the endpoint payload, walks every series targeting that endpoint and emits an observation if the watchlist `row_filter` matches (or unconditionally if no filter). `parse_record_date` accepts ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` strings, native `date`/`datetime` objects, and TZ-aware datetimes (converted to UTC). `parse_value` handles Treasury's thousands-separated strings (`"36,176,659,847,936.05"`), the literal `"null"` sentinel (case-insensitive), `N/A`, `n/a`, `-`, native numerics, and boolean rejection (defensive against contract change). `row_matches_filter` is case-sensitive string comparison via `str(...)` coercion so the watchlist YAML doesn't have to care about numeric-string types Treasury occasionally emits. **Missing-series raise** — if a watched series matches no rows in its blob (Treasury renamed an `account_type`, dropped a column, etc.), the normalizer raises rather than silently emitting an empty result; catching the contract change loud is the point. **Watchlist seed** — 9 series across 4 endpoints under three dimension blocks: debt level (`TOTAL_PUBLIC_DEBT`, `DEBT_HELD_PUBLIC`, `INTRAGOV_HOLDINGS`), Treasury cash (`TGA_CLOSING_BAL`), cost of debt (`TOTAL_INTEREST_EXPENSE_MTD`, `AVG_RATE_TOTAL_INTEREST_BEARING`, `AVG_RATE_MARKETABLE_BILLS`, `AVG_RATE_MARKETABLE_NOTES`, `AVG_RATE_MARKETABLE_BONDS`).
